## Edit 11
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/98420ef47dccdada.tex

Replace a stale proposed exact-oracle experiment with its completed, strictly scoped result.

Substantive destination: All source/native/orientation/rights obligations remain; complete test counts, costs, algebra and scope are in repair29:discovery.

Before:
~~~text
For adoption, the useful next experiment is narrowly specified. Supply
an exact independence oracle for this six-variable law, verify the
retained graph and separating sets, and compare a version with the
shortcut removed. Inspect the graph mutation, correlation and
orientation implementations as part of that experiment. If the shortcut
is necessary for a specialized admissible model class, state and prove
the required restriction. Until then, Aitiotita is a source for
comparison and possible component reuse, with direct adoption of this
path held pending that review. Its Apache 2.0 license does not establish
the statistical correctness of the path. \citep{exec13_java_aitiotita}
~~~
After:
~~~text
The exact-oracle comparison in Section~\ref{repair29:discovery} now tests
this repair on 1,381 declared cases. Full subset search recovers every
reference skeleton in that suite, while the screened candidate fails on 49;
the depth-two candidate leaves 165 incorrect skeletons, all explicitly
marked incomplete. The derivation here explains the same witness used in
that comparison, rather than a new independent replication. These project
searches do not execute the Java distribution or establish statistical
calibration. A specialized oracle could justify the shortcut only with its
own restriction and proof. Aitiotita therefore remains a comparison and
conditional component candidate; direct adoption still requires native
execution, graph-mutation and orientation checks. Its Apache 2.0 license
does not establish the statistical correctness of the path.
\citep{exec13_java_aitiotita}
~~~

## Edit 16
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/ad84e7f88d7e93fe.tex

Distinguish search incompleteness from the opening graph ambiguity and temporal confidence problem.

Substantive destination: All original covariance laws, monetary values, restrictions and uncertainty arguments remain unchanged.

Before:
~~~text
A labelled whole-line result can be the correct
answer to a well-defined question.
~~~
After:
~~~text
A labelled whole-line result can be the correct
answer to a well-defined question. A separate computational issue arises
when a search has not tested the conditioning sets needed to remove a link.
Section~\ref{repair29:discovery} compares a repair on exact-oracle cases and
shows why an unfinished search needs an explicit status. Search completeness,
statistical uncertainty and identification ambiguity are different limitations.
~~~

## Edit 17
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/sponsor-body.tex

Align sponsor with the previously completed discovery result now integrated in the reader.

Substantive destination: Original sponsor evidence and investment account retained; added evidence is explicitly bounded.

Before:
~~~text
These are conditional studies, with Monte Carlo uncertainty reported in the
monograph. They explain why observation assumptions and information costs
must remain visible in a completed report.
~~~
After:
~~~text
These are conditional studies, with Monte Carlo uncertainty reported in the
monograph. They explain why observation assumptions and information costs
must remain visible in a completed report.

A separate exact-oracle discovery comparison tests a computational repair on
1,381 declared graph cases. Full subset search recovers every reference
skeleton in the suite; an all-neighbour screening shortcut fails on 49.
A depth-two limit leaves 165 incorrect skeletons, all reported as unfinished
searches. These are project computations under exact graph oracles, not a
Java benchmark, a field failure rate or a calibrated bank discovery service.
~~~

BSD terms. Consider behind an identified model; it does not establish the
required discovery-to-effect workflow.\\

sDBN & Reference and temporal prototype candidate & Obtain reuse terms; qualify
clock, row dependence, candidate/directed outputs and transport.\\

failure meaning; establish a suitable target before temporal/effect claims.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
~~~

intervention argument. \\
MacroFinance & Funding consumer; select one model & Use its designated
BayesFilter authority, with action basis, horizon, exposure and structural
meaning declared. \\
MacroFinance worktrees & Group, retain revisions & The directories
\path{MacroFinance-ccma-g-v7-neutra-r9} and
\path{MacroFinance-dz5-neutra} are separate checkouts within this family;
neither is an additional independent product comparison. \\
FinMarketing & Priority consumer & Map source, feature, target and value
records while preserving source ownership, availability and decision clocks. \\
dataml & Priority observation exchange & Review artifact and assumption
records before translating them; declared JSON output is a transport boundary,
not verified causal truth. \\
cardnpv & Valuation provider or consumer & Preserve historical minus
perfect-execution value, admitted horizon and continuation; do not rename it
as a policy effect. \\
DynareMCP & Model provider; conditional integration & Retain model equations
and observation timing; its inspected CLI/in-process profile does not establish
strict stdio conformance. \\
MathDevMCP & Mathematical evidence tool & Pass explicit obligations and
premises; inspect its restricted-use terms and transport before adoption. \\
research-assistant & Source navigation & Consider the inspected read-only
route; retain genuine PDFs and technical anchors outside generated summaries. \\
SMEwallet & Relevant; interface deferred & The inspected mathematical
primitives are not an estimator. Specify its target/accounting bridge before
selecting an adapter. \\
MOONeural & Adjacent solver; defer & Require a concrete nuisance or numerical
task and applicable rights; a provenance notice alone is no license grant. \\
hponas & Adjacent tuning; defer & Require a declared learner, training split
and license review; tuning success does not qualify causal inference. \\
humanvoice & Editorial; defer & A later prose revision must preserve claims
and citations; editorial changes cannot provide scientific acceptance. \\
dsge\_hmc & Container; defer & Three nested checkouts were observed. Select a
canonical model and interface before treating the container as a consumer. \\
claudecodex & Exclude from causal runtime & Shared agent policy infrastructure
supplies no causal algorithm or bank observation interface. \\
.local & Exclude support directory & Ambient environment files are not a
declared application dependency; contents were outside the inspection. \\
causal & Producer and standalone client & Preserve the distinction between
program-control commands and the scientific library, CLI and MCP surfaces. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

## Edit 35
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/72f64a0c152b5f00.tex

Develop a consequential retained-source limitation: distinguish discrete influence diagrams from the separate conditional-Gaussian network family.

Substantive destination: Existing information-clock, causality and licence arguments remain verbatim. Added banking alternatives explain the consequence of a documented model-class restriction without claiming a native test.

Before:
~~~text
outcomes.

The vendor's published licence text
~~~
After:
~~~text
outcomes.

The selected influence-diagram route has a further restriction that matters
for a loan portfolio: its manual excludes continuous chance nodes. The
separate conditional-Gaussian Bayesian-network facility does not remove
that restriction. That facility describes Gaussian variables whose means
are linear in their continuous parents, allows the coefficients and variances
to depend on discrete parents, and excludes continuous parents of discrete
nodes. These are different documented model families within the same
product. A continuous balance history therefore cannot be carried into the
decision diagram merely because another HUGIN model accepts a continuous
measurement. \citep{gap29hugin}

One possible comparator uses explicit balance and arrears categories in a
supplied decision model. Their boundaries, transition probabilities and
utilities would then be part of the economic approximation: customers in a
single category need not have the same response to a contact. A second
comparator retains continuous states in a separately justified policy-value
calculation, such as the construction in Chapter~\ref{rewrite:servicing} and
its fitted extensions. The choice should turn on whether categorisation
preserves the decision and its material uncertainty. The inspected manual
establishes the representation boundary; it does not quantify the loss from
coarsening a bank's records or demonstrate a native servicing trial.

The vendor's published licence text
~~~

## Edit 74
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/e6fc8271214ca907.tex

Resolve the explicitly uninspected policy solver delegate using pinned primary wrapper/native sources and an exact, non-executed algebraic witness.

Substantive destination: Original fold, prefit-model, history, support and calibration limits remain. The vague delegate obligation becomes an explained class-orientation path, augmented-call limitation, scoped witness and concrete qualification/refusal alternatives. No original mathematics or adverse result is removed.

Before:
~~~text
Nested nuisance-fitting folds and outer policy-evaluation folds are different
controls. An adapter must preserve both, record any supplied prefit models and
avoid translating a method name into a blanket calibration claim. Source comments
about the orientation of one weighted-learning solver require inspection of that
delegate before they are inherited as established behaviour. For the present
proposal, polle is a conditional original-R-worker candidate with named method,
history, support and evaluation contracts.~\citep{exec24_policiesr_polle}
~~~
After:
~~~text
Nested nuisance-fitting folds and outer policy-evaluation folds are different
controls. An adapter must preserve both, record any supplied prefit models and
avoid translating a method name into a blanket calibration claim. The
outcome-weighted delegate supplies a concrete reason to preserve the resolved
solver and dependency versions as well.~\citep{exec24_policiesr_polle}

The pinned polle wrapper selects \texttt{ipop} by default and passes that
choice to DTRlearn2. Inspection of DTRlearn2 2.1 and WeightSVM 1.7-16
traces the alternative \texttt{svm} route beyond the warning in polle's
comments. WeightSVM converts the signed binary responses to factor codes.
Its native classifier orders classes by first appearance and gives the
first class the positive decision-function sign. The special native rule
for literal signed labels does not apply after this factor conversion.
DTRlearn2 copies the returned support-vector coefficients into its own
decision function without translating the returned class order back to the
signed response. Positive output can consequently refer to the opposite
action from the one its downstream threshold assumes.
\citep{rel15policydelegates}

An exact project witness isolates the convention. Take two one-dimensional
points at -1 and +1 with matching signed labels, positive unit weights and
unit penalty. The stipulated hard-margin solution has dual weights 1/2.
When the negative observation appears first, the native convention gives
the negative class a positive score; importing its coefficients without
the class translation gives slope -1 and, under the inspected bias
reconstruction, intercept zero. The returned action labels are reversed.
Reversing the row order gives slope +1. This is an algebraic witness using
an exact solution, not execution of either solver. The small diagonal
regularizer and finite numerical tolerances require separate native tests.
The source comparison and polle's retained relabelling test motivate those
tests; an unrun test file is not a passing result.
\citep{rel15policydelegates,rel15delegatewitnesses}

The default safeguard also has a boundary. DTRlearn2's augmented branch
calls \texttt{owl\_aug} without forwarding the selected solver; that
delegate defaults to \texttt{svm}. Thus setting \texttt{ipop} at the outer
interface does not select it for this branch. The initial worker should
exclude the augmented hinge-loss route until the call is repaired and
qualified, and test row permutations, action relabelling and both
augmentation settings at the exact dependency versions. A different
qualified learner is a substantive alternative. For the present proposal,
polle remains a conditional original-R-worker candidate with named method,
history, support and evaluation contracts; source inspection establishes
neither calibrated policy value nor native acceptance.
\citep{exec24_policiesr_polle,rel15policydelegates}
~~~

## Edit 75
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/291dfdd96a75e082.tex

Replace the unresolved learnDBM inner-path statement with inspected same-revision stationarity, assignment-weight and stopping contracts.

Substantive destination: All lineage counts, distinct-caller caution and intervention-regime limits survive. Added exact likelihood witnesses explain consequential mismatches; no Java execution or convergence claim is introduced.

Before:
~~~text
learnDBM is a related implementation. At the selected revisions, 15 of the
17 shared paths in the repositories' dynamic-network source directory have
identical Git blobs. Their observation classes and CLI entry points differ:
learnDBM accepts a cluster count and invokes \texttt{MultiNet.clust} with the
requested stationarity flag. The inner clustering path is not inspected here.
The learnDBN CLI finding cannot automatically be attributed to this different
caller. Nor do learned clusters necessarily represent externally assigned
intervention regimes; that interpretation needs another identification
argument.~\citep{exec15_javatemporal_learndbn,exec15_javatemporal_learndbm}
~~~
After:
~~~text
learnDBM is a related implementation. At the selected revisions, 15 of the
17 shared paths in the repositories' dynamic-network source directory have
identical Git blobs. Their observation classes and CLI entry points differ:
learnDBM accepts a cluster count, supplies the requested stationarity flag
to \texttt{MultiNet}, and invokes its clustering method. The learnDBN CLI
finding cannot automatically be attributed to this different caller.
Inspection of the inner class at the same learnDBM revision nevertheless
reveals a separate option mismatch: training and the inner reassignment
pass the declared flag, but the final stored reassignment calls
\texttt{computeClusters(networkPrev, true, false)}. Its stationary branch
uses transition network zero at every date.
\citep{exec15_javatemporal_learndbn,exec15_javatemporal_learndbm,rel15multinet}

This matters even when every required conditional probability is available.
In an authored two-transition example with equal component weights, let
the observed-path probabilities be 0.9 then 0.1 for one component and 0.6
then 0.6 for the other. Their likelihoods are 0.09 and 0.36, giving
normalized weights 1/5 and 4/5. Reusing the first transition gives 0.81
and 0.36, hence weights 9/13 and 4/13. The final routine samples a hard
assignment, so this discrepancy changes its sampling probabilities; it
does not imply a deterministic native outcome in every run.
\citep{rel15multinet,rel15delegatewitnesses}

Two other distinctions prevent a generic EM claim. The reassignment helper
gets mixture weights from the object's stored clustering while the loop
updates a local count array; these are not synchronized within that loop.
Its classification branch chooses the greatest trajectory likelihood
before mixture weights are applied. For weights 0.9 and 0.1 and
likelihoods 0.4 and 0.8, the likelihood-only choice favors the second
component, whereas the weighted products 0.36 and 0.08 favor the first.
The loop can switch from stochastic to classification assignment after
one hundred iterations; that switch is not a hard iteration limit.
An adapter must specify the intended mixture objective, propagate the
stationarity option and qualify the stopping and assignment behavior.
These are selected-source findings and exact illustrations, not an
executed Java failure or a proof that all clustering paths fail. Learned
clusters also need not represent externally assigned intervention regimes;
that interpretation still needs an identification argument.
\citep{rel15multinet,rel15delegatewitnesses,exec15_javatemporal_learndbm}
~~~

## Edit 78
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/d587fa2f22fbb93b.tex

Resolve the explicitly open public summary caller and expose its scale-restoration and validation contracts.

Substantive destination: Preserves draw-first aggregation, target-cohort responsibility and all outstanding weight/draw safeguards; adds the inspected caller distinction, exact monetary witness and reversal test.

Before:
~~~text
The inspected flexBCF native SATT routine evaluates each stored ensemble on supplied rows, averages within the draw and then calculates quantiles. Its weighted companion divides by the supplied weight sum. The routine name does not select treated rows: cohort selection must already be reflected in its inputs. These selected functions do not settle how all public callers validate empty cohorts, nonfinite weights, zero sums or category levels. The proposed adapter must require explicit cohort and weight contracts; complete caller tracing remains open.~\citep{exec21_cxx_flexbcf}
~~~
After:
~~~text
The inspected flexBCF native SATT routine evaluates each stored ensemble on supplied rows, averages within the draw and then calculates quantiles. Its weighted companion divides by the supplied weight sum. The routine name does not select treated rows: cohort selection must already be reflected in its inputs. Following the exported caller at the same revision reveals a more specific problem. In \texttt{average\_tree\_fits}, the treatment branch selects the treatment trees but passes \texttt{treat = FALSE}. The native routine consequently applies prognostic restoration, multiplying by the outcome standard deviation and adding the outcome mean. By contrast, \texttt{get\_tree\_fits} passes \texttt{TRUE} for treatment trees and its native predictor restores only the scale. The supplied predictors reach the corresponding evaluation fields in either branch; the consequential difference here is the additive outcome mean.~\citep{exec21_cxx_flexbcf,rel16flexcallers}

Let $g_i^{(s)}$ denote a standardized treatment-tree evaluation, let $q_i$ be nonnegative target weights with positive sum, and let $\bar Y$ and $s_Y$ be the training outcome mean and standard deviation. The intended and incorrectly restored portfolio draws are
\begin{equation}
 T^{(s)}=s_Y\frac{\sum_i q_i g_i^{(s)}}{\sum_i q_i},
 \qquad \widetilde T^{(s)}=T^{(s)}+\bar Y.
 \label{rel16:flex-scale}
\end{equation}
This difference shifts the posterior mean and every translation-equivariant posterior quantile by the same amount. Consider an authored draw for twenty equally weighted customers: ten standardized evaluations are $1/5$ and ten are $2/5$. With an outcome mean of USD 100 and standard deviation of USD 10, the intended average gain is USD 3; prognostic restoration produces USD 103. At an assumed USD 5 action cost, the corresponding margins are USD $-2$ and USD 98. The two routes imply opposite decisions despite using the same tree evaluations. These are stipulated exact calculations, not output from an executed package or evidence about an actual bank.~\citep{rel16flexcallers,rel16flexwitnesses}

A useful qualification test follows from the meaning of an effect. Adding a common constant to every observed outcome leaves the centered, standardized training outcomes unchanged and should leave the action contrast unchanged, for the same fitted draws and settings. An unwanted additive outcome mean instead transfers that constant into the reported effect. The adapter should test this invariance and compare draw-wise weighted averages from the individual-evaluation path with the summary path after repairing the branch. Simply subtracting the outcome mean downstream would leave the original caller defect and other boundary contracts unresolved.~\citep{rel16flexcallers,rel16flexwitnesses}

The caller rejects a single-row prediction and negative weights, but the inspected caller and weighted routine do not establish a complete contract for finite aligned weights with positive sum or nonempty retained draws. The native routine accesses the first stored draw and divides by the supplied weight sum. A further small-cohort hazard comes from the default progress interval: for two through nine rows, its floor-of-one-tenth rule gives zero, which reaches an integer remainder operation even when verbose output is disabled. The individual-evaluation caller similarly derives its default from the number of draws. These source paths require explicit positive progress intervals, valid draw counts and input checks before native testing. They are findings about the pinned implementation, not a claim that every entrypoint fails or that a particular exception has been observed.~\citep{rel16flexcallers,rel16flexwitnesses}
~~~

## Edit 79
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/d587fa2f22fbb93b.tex

Complete the public training-order and treated-only design trace at the already selected revision.

Substantive destination: Preserves the native count/offset finding and cross-library warning; replaces the open tracing task with documented conventions, aligned identifiers and remaining validation conditions.

Before:
~~~text
The selected flexBCF trainer derives the control count from prognostic and treatment design sizes and offsets treatment updates accordingly. Its wrapper transposes inputs and passes the treatment indicator. Training order and treatment-specific rows therefore require tracing through public callers and documentation. Assuming the same row layout across all four libraries would hide a consequential boundary error.~\citep{exec21_cxx_flexbcf}
~~~
After:
~~~text
The selected flexBCF trainer derives the control count from prognostic and treatment design sizes and offsets treatment updates accordingly. Its public wrapper transposes the supplied matrices and passes the treatment indicator without rearranging rows. The same-revision manual explicitly requires controls before treated subjects; its worked construction supplies prognostic predictors for all subjects but treatment predictors only for treated subjects. The native traversal and residual offsets follow that arrangement. An adapter must apply one common permutation to outcomes, treatment, identifiers and prognostic predictors, then form the treated-only design in the corresponding order. Sorting just the treatment vector would corrupt the correspondence. For subjects $(a,b,c,d)$ with labels $(1,0,1,0)$, a stable valid order is $(b,d,a,c)$ and the treatment design contains $(a,c)$. This identity check is an authored fixture, not a fitted forest.~\citep{exec21_cxx_flexbcf,rel16flexcallers,rel16flexwitnesses}

The manual also requires continuous predictors scaled to $[-1,1]$ and zero-indexed categorical levels. The inspected parsers check selected matrix and level-list dimensions; they do not establish validation of every observed category, outcome scale or treatment-row correspondence. The training wrapper standardizes outcomes before the native call, so a constant outcome needs an explicit supported disposition. These checks should be retained alongside the documented input order and scale conventions. Assuming the same row layout across all four libraries would hide a consequential boundary error.~\citep{exec21_cxx_flexbcf,rel16flexcallers}
~~~

## Edit 82
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/d587fa2f22fbb93b.tex

Close the missing dedicated flexBCF original with its identification, estimation restrictions and adverse empirical evidence.

Substantive destination: Additive subsection; every prior mathematical, implementation, alternative and historical-coverage passage remains. Distinguishes trend identification from equal-level modeling and versioned source defects.

Before:
~~~text
\subsection{Integration choices and the remaining census}
~~~
After:
~~~text
\subsection{A scalable longitudinal forest still needs an identification model}
\label{rel17:flex-paper}
The original flexBCF study by \citet{rel17flexpaper} supplies a consequential comparison between computational feasibility and scientific adequacy. The inspected original is arXiv version 2, posted on 11 May 2023 and carrying a 15 May 2023 title-page date. It is distinct from both a fresh replication and an audit of the selected 2025 package. The authors analyze the 2022 ACIC challenge: 3,400 synthetic datasets, comprising 200 replications of each of seventeen mechanisms, with treatment assigned to medical practices after two pre-treatment years. For banking, the analogous question is the effect of a branch-level intervention on customers served by treated branches. It requires a branch assignment clock, a specified customer population and a defense of the comparison across branches.~\citep{rel17flexpaper}

Their identification argument uses consistency and no anticipation, overlap, and conditional independence of untreated outcome changes and assignment given baseline covariates. Define $Z$ as branch assignment, $X$ as permitted baseline information, $s$ as a pre-intervention date and $t$ as a post-intervention date. Let $m_{zt}(x)=E(Y_t\mid Z=z,X=x)$. The corresponding conditional effect among treated units is
\begin{equation}
 \tau_{\rm T}(x,t)
 =m_{1t}(x)-m_{0t}(x)-\{m_{1s}(x)-m_{0s}(x)\}.
 \label{rel17:flex-did}
\end{equation}
The paper proves this difference-in-differences identity in its Appendix S1. Its stated conditional independence is stronger than the equality of conditional untreated mean changes needed for this mean identity. A portfolio ATT must further average over the declared treated population; an unqualified all-customer average is a different target. Neither a fitted propensity feature nor a large number of customer rows verifies the identifying assumptions.~\citep{rel17flexpaper}

The estimation model adds a separate restriction. It represents the outcome mean by a common prognostic function of covariates and time, plus a treatment function activated only after intervention. Before intervention, that specification forces treated and untreated groups with the same included covariates to have the same expected outcome level. Parallel untreated trends alone do not impose equal baseline levels. To see the distinction, consider equally likely branch groups with no varying observed covariates. Their pre-intervention monetary means are USD 10 and USD 14. Untreated outcomes rise by USD 3 in both groups; the second group receives an intervention adding USD 2. Observed post-intervention means are therefore USD 13 and USD 19. The post-period gap is USD 6, but subtracting the USD 4 pre-period gap identifies the USD 2 effect. At an assumed USD 3 per-customer intervention cost, the identified margin is USD $-1$, whereas treating the post-period gap as the effect yields USD 3. This exact law satisfies overlap and parallel untreated increments while violating equal baseline levels. It does not assert that a particular fitted forest necessarily returns the post-period gap.~\citep{rel17flexpaper,rel17flexwitness}

The challenge analysis also conditions the treatment component on some post-treatment practice-composition summaries. The authors explicitly assume that intervention does not change attrition and hence practice composition. A bank must examine that assumption when assistance changes account closure, migration or the customers remaining at a branch. If composition responds to intervention, conditioning on its later value can change the causal question or introduce selection. A serious alternative is to model changes under a defended trend restriction, with a fixed population and dependence-aware evaluation; it is not enough to feed later composition into a flexible learner and retain the original target label.~\citep{rel17flexpaper}

Computational improvement did not remove the inferential limitation in the reported challenge. For nominal 90\% overall-ATT credible intervals, the original beneficiary-level submission had mean coverage 52.15\% across the seventeen mechanisms; the practice-level analysis had mean coverage 83.68\%. Its mean RMSE rose from 20.54 to 23.04 when aggregated to practices. The paper reports that flexible propensity estimation often increased interval width and coverage, while point-estimation differences were modest. These historical summaries are attributed results, not independent reconstructions, and they do not establish a universal preference for aggregation, a propensity learner or a sparsity prior. The authors discuss outcome-model misspecification, omitted pre-treatment outcomes or trends, and limited sensitivity analysis as explanations and limits.~\citep{rel17flexpaper}

The study motivates examining tree persistence, categorical partitioning and memory allocation, but its runtime narrative supplies no matched bank total-cost estimate. Nor does its empirical performance diagnose the later pinned caller defect above: these concern different versioned evidence. For a bank prototype, the useful comparison separates the identifying trend restriction, additional response-model restrictions, customer and branch sampling units, composition assumptions, and the reporting path. Faster computation merits adoption only when the resulting estimand and uncertainty remain appropriate to the decision. A failed calibration or an unsupported composition assumption should reverse an otherwise attractive computational recommendation.~\citep{rel17flexpaper,rel16flexcallers}

\subsection{Integration choices and the remaining census}
~~~

## Edit 84
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/62cabebeedc23664.tex

Integrate the completed financing-source comparison and full observational-equivalence proof before the formulation evaluation discussion.

Substantive destination: Every original formulation paragraph remains. The complete delivered financing text, mathematics, figure, reported results and limits survive; only section depth and citation namespace change, with an added project-evidence citation.

Before:
~~~text
\hypertarget{evaluation-before-a-costly-bank-study}{%
~~~
After:
~~~text
\subsection{A convincing explanation is not yet an intervention estimate}
\label{closure30:formulation}

A corporate borrower says that a tight credit line prevents it from accepting
an overseas order. A language assistant may recover the relevant passage,
connect it to the financial statements and explain why working capital matters.
The lender still faces a different question: how would a specified change in
credit terms affect exports, repayment and contribution? The answer depends on
why the borrower faces the constraint and what the proposed facility changes.
Good extraction is useful evidence preparation. It does not supply the missing
comparison between alternative lending decisions.

\subsubsection{What the financing-and-exports study actually measures}

Li's September 2025 preprint is directly relevant to this distinction. Its
proposed procedure first extracts financing constraints, then export outcomes,
and finally asks a language model to explain a mechanism using economic
theories. The first extraction records a constraint, its severity, supporting
evidence and type. The second records an export metric, value, description and
direction. These explicit intermediate records would help an analyst challenge
an unsupported interpretation of a company report. Equations (1)--(10) define
the information passed between these stages. \citep[Sections 3.1--3.4]{rel18li}

The reported Financing-Export Dataset contains 2,000 Chinese listed firms
over 2015--2019, combining accounts, trade information and text. The stated
comparison uses the same inputs for the proposed prompts and model baselines.
It evaluates financing-constraint classification, subsequent export
participation prediction, repeated-answer consistency and economic relevance
scored by experts. Table~\ref{closure30:exports} reproduces the two rows needed
to understand the claimed improvement; these are the author's reported
scores, not an independently reproduced experiment.

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{lrrrr}
\toprule
Method & Constraint F1 & Export accuracy & Consistency & Relevance\\
\midrule
Baseline prompts & 0.82 & 76.8\% & 0.78 & 4.1/5\\
Proposed staged prompts & 0.84 & 78.5\% & 0.81 & 4.4/5\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Selected reported results from Li's Table 1. The first two columns
measure classification and prediction. Consistency measures repeated outputs;
relevance is an expert assessment of the explanation. None is an estimated
effect of a lending intervention.}
\label{closure30:exports}
\end{table}

The ablation is revealing. Removing the explicit economic-theory guidance
leaves reported F1 and export accuracy at 0.84 and 78.5\%, while consistency
falls to 0.77 and relevance to 3.8/5. This supports the narrower reported
comparison that the guidance changes explanation scores in that experiment.
It does not show that an intervention contrast becomes identified. The
relevance criterion explicitly rewards alignment with economic theory, so
improvement on it is not independent confirmation of the proposed mechanism.
\citep[Sections 4.2.2--4.6; Tables 1--2]{rel18li}

Sections 3 and 4 do not state a treatment-assignment design, a structural
restriction identifying a specified lending effect, or an estimator and
uncertainty interval for that effect. They also do not provide sufficient
model-version, prompt, partition and uncertainty detail to reconstruct the
reported score differences from the paper alone. Those limitations restrict
the use of the evidence; they do not establish that the reported scores are
false. The useful proposal role is a candidate for extracting financial
evidence and developing hypotheses, to be compared on tasks with independently
checked records. Its explanation score cannot value a credit facility.

\subsubsection{The same observed story can imply different lending effects}

Consider a deliberately small model in which $F=1$ denotes a financing
constraint and $Y=1$ denotes export participation. Both are binary. For the
moment, suppose every company report states $F$ and $Y$ perfectly, and an
assistant extracts them without error. This assumption gives the assistant
the best possible measurement of these two variables. It lets us isolate the
identification problem from a language model's mistakes.

In the first explanation, an unobserved distress state $U$ causes both the
constraint and the export outcome. Let $U$ be Bernoulli with probability
$1/2$, and let independent errors $E_F,E_Y$ each be Bernoulli with probability
$1/5$. All three inputs are mutually independent. Define
\begin{equation}
 F=U\mathbin{\oplus}E_F,\qquad
 Y=(1-U)\mathbin{\oplus}E_Y,
 \label{closure30:confounded}
\end{equation}
where $\oplus$ is exclusive-or: it flips a binary value when the error is one.
Distress makes the constraint more likely and participation less likely.
Changing $F$ alone leaves the equation for $Y$ unchanged. In this model,
\begin{equation}
 \Pr\{Y=1\mid\doop(F=0)\}
 =\Pr\{Y=1\mid\doop(F=1)\}=\frac12.
 \label{closure30:noeffect}
\end{equation}

The observed contrast nevertheless looks substantial. Symmetry gives
$\Pr(F=1)=1/2$ and $\Pr(U=1\mid F=1)=4/5$. Consequently,
\begin{align}
 \Pr(Y=1\mid F=1)
 &=\frac45\frac15+\frac15\frac45=\frac8{25},\\
 \Pr(Y=1\mid F=0)
 &=\frac45\frac45+\frac15\frac15=\frac{17}{25}.
\end{align}
The participation difference between constrained and unconstrained firms is
$-9/25$, or minus 36 percentage points, even though the intervention effect
is zero. It reflects the unobserved distress composition of the groups.

In the second explanation, financing itself changes participation. Let
$Z$ be Bernoulli with probability $1/2$ and $E$ independently Bernoulli with
probability $8/25$, and set
\begin{equation}
 F=Z,\qquad Y=(1-F)\mathbin{\oplus}E.
 \label{closure30:causal}
\end{equation}
Now the conditional and interventional risks coincide: they are $17/25$
under $F=0$ and $8/25$ under $F=1$. Removing the constraint raises participation
by $9/25$. Both explanations generate exactly the same table of observations:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{crr}
\toprule
 & $Y=0$ & $Y=1$\\
\midrule
$F=0$ & $4/25$ & $17/50$\\
$F=1$ & $17/50$ & $4/25$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Each row sums to $1/2$. Exact enumeration of the independent binary inputs
agrees with these algebraic probabilities and with both intervention
calculations. The constants are stipulated for the example; none is an
estimate for Chinese firms or a bank portfolio. \citep{rel18witness}

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[>=stealth,every node/.style={font=\small}]
\node (u) at (0,1.5) {$U$: distress};
\node (f) at (-1.5,0) {$F$: constraint};
\node (y) at (1.5,0) {$Y$: participation};
\draw[->] (u)--(f);\draw[->] (u)--(y);
\node[align=center] at (0,-0.8) {Common-cause explanation\\effect of relief: zero};
\node (f2) at (5,0) {$F$};\node (y2) at (7,0) {$Y$};
\draw[->] (f2)--(y2);
\node[align=center] at (6,-0.8) {Financing explanation\\effect of relief: $9/25$};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The two constructed mechanisms share the observed distribution
of financing constraints and export participation. A report containing only
these facts cannot distinguish their intervention effects.}
\label{closure30:twoworlds}
\end{figure}

Suppose the report is a deterministic transcript $T=t(F,Y)$, recording those
two facts with their values and dates. Then
\begin{equation}
 \Pr(F=f,Y=y,T=t)=\Pr(F=f,Y=y)\,\ind\{t=t(f,y)\}
\end{equation}
is identical in the two explanations. Any fixed extraction or prediction rule
using that information has the same observable performance in both. Random
prompt variation independent of the firm adds no distinction between the two
observational laws. Even an always-consistent explanation can therefore be
attached to two different intervention answers.

Real reports may contain additional information about distress, assignment
or mechanisms. The construction does not exclude that possibility. Such
information must be established and incorporated into the model; a score for
the fluency or plausibility of the resulting explanation cannot substitute
for it. The two effects displayed here demonstrate nonidentification in this
example, not sharp bounds over every compatible causal model.

\subsubsection{Knowledge claims need a task-specific test}

Mugleston and colleagues discuss a broader philosophical question: whether
the internal representations of a language model deserve to be called
knowledge. Their argument considers justified true belief, compressed
representations, contextual understanding and the limitations of the Chinese
room analogy. Section 5 also distinguishes informative coherence from merely
repeating an answer, and Section 9 leaves important claims about understanding
unsettled. The paper describes its purpose as a thought experiment.
\citep[Sections 3.3--6 and 9]{rel18mugleston}

That discussion is relevant to how an analyst interprets assistance, but it
does not resolve a lending estimand. A system can organize useful knowledge
without knowing which of the two mechanisms in Figure~\ref{closure30:twoworlds}
generated a particular dataset. Conversely, calling the system a statistical
text generator does not establish that a specific extracted passage is wrong.
The practical assessment concerns the record, the proposed causal restriction
and the decision that follows from it. Philosophical labels cannot replace
those checks.

For a credit decision, the first commitment is an action the lender can carry
out: for example, an offer with specified limit, maturity, price and eligibility
at a fixed date. Setting an abstract constraint indicator to zero is not
automatically that offer. Take-up, competing lenders, borrower responses and
the cost of capital can intervene between the offer and exports. The target
may concern an offered facility, the facility actually drawn, or net
contribution after expected losses. Those quantities require distinct
comparisons.

A useful assisted study would retain the quoted passages and dates, distinguish
the lender's action from the borrower's condition, and set out alternative
causal explanations. An analyst could then defend additional measurements,
a suitable experiment or a credible source of assignment variation. The
evaluation should score faithful extraction, correct formulation and justified
uncertainty separately. A strong extraction score is valuable on its own;
the effect estimate becomes valuable only when its identification and financial
interpretation are also supported.

\hypertarget{evaluation-before-a-costly-bank-study}{%
~~~

## Edit 87
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/f26f479adef0a0f2.tex

Close three named temporal software omissions using authenticated primary source and exact authored banking consequences.

Substantive destination: Additive three-family comparison; all existing equations, negative results, alternatives and implementation contracts remain in the manuscript.

Before:
~~~text
\subsection{Reuse choices and the next affordable experiment}
~~~
After:
~~~text
\subsection{DBN++: filtering depends on model identity and the evidence clock}
\label{rel19:dbnpp}

DBN++ makes the supplied-model distinction especially clear. Its February
2016 revision describes filtering in discrete-time finite-state dynamic
Bayesian networks. The three advertised routes are variable elimination in
an unrolled network, the interface forward algorithm, and a forward algorithm
using algebraic decision diagrams. The model file supplies factors and the
mapping between slices; the evidence file supplies a sequence of observed
states. These paths do not estimate a causal structure or identify the effect
of a servicing action. They can nevertheless be useful reference calculations
when a bank has justified a particular latent-state model. The experimental
file format must then carry the ordered state dictionary and the physical
meaning of a transition. \citep{rel19dbnpp}

The first evidence time matters. Both interface implementations construct the
initial forward distribution, project it through the transition factors, and
then update it with the first observation. Let $D_t$ denote a binary borrower
distress state, let $K(d'\mid d)$ be its supplied transition law, and let
$\ell_{t+1}(d')$ be the likelihood of the next observed flag. If $p_t$ is the
current state distribution, the intended update is
\begin{equation}
 p_{t+1}(d')=
 \frac{\ell_{t+1}(d')\sum_d K(d'\mid d)p_t(d)}
 {\sum_z\ell_{t+1}(z)\sum_d K(z\mid d)p_t(d)}.
 \label{rel19:filter}
\end{equation}
Thus a wrapper must say whether its initial distribution precedes the first
evidence interval. Feeding evidence already used to construct that distribution
would be a different experiment. The denominator must be positive: an
impossible observation has no conditional distribution under the supplied
model. The inspected dense and diagram normalization methods divide without
an explicit positive-mass guard. The parser and caller also do not establish
a nonempty evidence horizon before the unrolled route accesses its first
record. Validate these conditions and return their scientific or input failure
meaning instead of a seemingly valid probability. \citep{rel19dbnpp}

Model identity is a separate concern for a persistent service. In each
\texttt{project} overload, function-static vectors retain the variable pointers
and transition factors from the first initialization. Later calls append and
remove the current forward factor but do not rebuild those retained vectors
for a different model. The shipped CLI loads one model, a narrower use than
a service evaluating several portfolios or actions in one process. Replacing
its factor objects while reusing the function can therefore retain the earlier
kernel; changing variable objects also raises lifetime and identity questions.
This is a static source finding, not an executed failure of every CLI request.
A prospective adapter needs model-scoped state and a concurrency contract.
\citep{rel19dbnpp}

An exact constructed example shows the monetary significance of substituting
the kernel. Take $P(D_t=1)=1/4$, with flag likelihoods $1/5$ in state zero and
$4/5$ in state one. Kernel A gives distress probabilities $1/10$ and $4/5$
from current states zero and one; kernel B gives $2/5$ and $9/10$. The next
distress probabilities before the flag are $11/40$ and $21/40$. Equation
\eqref{rel19:filter} then gives posterior probabilities $44/73$ and $84/103$.
Suppose, as an additional stipulated causal payoff law, a subsequent action
prevents USD~14 of loss only in the distressed state and costs USD~10 for
every customer. Its expected net value is USD~$14p-10$: approximately
USD~$-1.56$ under A and USD~1.42 under B. Reusing A when B was requested reverses
the action recommendation. The arithmetic assumes the same variable identities
and well-defined kernels; it does not predict the result of dangling pointers,
nor does a filtered probability establish the stipulated causal payoff law.
\citep{rel19dbnwitness}

The finite-state label also needs an algorithm-specific qualification. The
diagram constructor uses one Boolean variable for each domain variable and
selects its positive literal whenever a state index is nonzero. A three-state
dictionary such as current, delinquent and default is therefore not represented
injectively by that construction: indices one and two select the same literal.
For masses $(1/5,3/10,1/2)$, this Boolean mapping preserves only the combined
positive-state mass $4/5$. This demonstrates loss of state identity, not the
exact output of CUDD when supplied an invalid nonbinary evaluation input. A
future diagram route must reject this domain or implement and verify a proper
multivalued encoding; agreement on binary examples cannot qualify ternary
borrower states. \citep{rel19dbnpp,rel19dbnwitness}

The reuse decision is consequently limited to a supplied-model filtering
reference pending those contracts and numerical tests. The build points to
CUDD 3.0.0 through a host-specific directory. README and inspected source
headers declare LGPL version 3 or later, whereas the root LICENSE contains
GPL version 3 text. That discrepancy requires clarification before a
distribution decision; this comparison does not resolve it legally. Neither
a faster forward pass nor a license label supplies causal identification.
\citep{rel19dbnpp}

\subsection{EISALab/DBN: anomaly processing changes the observation experiment}
\label{rel19:eisadbn}

The July 2013 EISALab/DBN revision contains a flat-file anomaly-detection
application. Its Bayesian driver selects ordinary, robust, mixture and online
Kalman variants, Rao--Blackwellized particle filters and scale-recursive
methods from supplied model specifications. The inspected path is therefore
relevant to observation construction and state estimation. It does not
establish a causal structure learner merely because the repository is named
DBN. The wrapper calls an external batch-EM routine for ordinary Kalman
learning; the robust Kalman class's two \texttt{Learn} overloads explicitly
perform no learning. The common command-line learning option cannot be treated
as evidence that every selected filter fits new parameters. The external
numerical implementations remain outside this inspection. \citep{rel19eisadbn}

Its clock and output policies are consequential. The driver advances an
expected timestamp by a configured number of seconds and creates all-missing
records while awaiting the next observation. An optional sufficiently long
gap resets the filter and starts another training sequence; the shown reset
branch supports only the ordinary Kalman and first particle-filter choices.
The chronology check rejects any record earlier than the expected timestamp
before applying the nominal symmetric timing tolerance. A banking adapter must
therefore define positive time resolution, arrival ordering, gaps and restart
meaning explicitly. A missing interval, a delayed report and a new customer
history must not acquire the same interpretation by accident. \citep{rel19eisadbn}

The ordinary filter wrapper returns an observation, lower and upper predictive
limits, a predictive mean and an anomaly indicator. In the optional cleaned
file, a nonmissing observation flagged as anomalous is written as NA. That
policy may help isolate sensor failures, but a large genuine response to a
bank action is not a sensor failure. Consider an exact randomized example:
customer type $U$ and action $A$ are independent Bernoulli variables with
probability one half, and monetary outcomes obey
\begin{equation}
 Y(0)=100,\qquad Y(1)=100+100U,\qquad
 R=\mathbf 1\{Y\leq150\}.
 \label{rel19:cleaning}
\end{equation}
Here $R$ records retention after a stipulated anomaly rule, and all outcomes
are accurate measurements. The population treatment effect is USD~50.
Among retained treated customers only type zero remains; both retained group
means are USD~100, so their contrast is zero. At a stipulated action cost of
USD~20, the population net benefit is USD~30 while the retained-row comparison
suggests USD~$-20$. Randomization before cleaning does not justify the latter
comparison after outcome-dependent selection. This is a constructed law,
not an assertion that the external filter necessarily implements this threshold
or produced these results. \citep{rel19dbnwitness}

The defensible alternative retains the original outcome, flag, missingness
reason and transformation history as separate fields. A causal analysis must
justify any exclusion, model the measurement or selection mechanism when
appropriate, and examine sensitivity when that mechanism cannot be learned.
Prediction intervals concern the stipulated observation model; they are not
automatically intervals for an action effect. The inspected Makefile refers
to external libraries and absolute directories on the original developer's
machine, while the complete tree supplies no file named as a license or
copyright notice. These limits favor a scientific and interface reference
until dependency, rights and numerical behavior are resolved. They do not
negate the potential usefulness of anomaly detection for its own task.
\citep{rel19eisadbn}

\subsection{pdbntk: a parallel skeleton is not a fitted dynamic model}
\label{rel19:pdbntk}

The July 2013 pdbntk revision describes a parallel dynamic-network toolkit,
but the actual demonstration path is unfinished. Its executable declares
hidden-state models and allocates 40,000 sequences of length 100, then passes
a \texttt{DummyInfEngine} to the parallel driver. That engine performs an
arithmetic workload and returns one for each sequence, with an empty vector
of sufficient-statistic objects. The iteration loop repeatedly calls its
expectation routine; the maximization routine is empty and is never called,
and the convergence flag is never updated. If those loops complete as written,
the last accumulated value is 40,000, irrespective of the observations. Its
printed label \texttt{loglik} does not make it a fitted likelihood. No timing,
successful build or native execution is inferred here. \citep{rel19pdbntk,rel19dbnwitness}

The alternative junction-tree interface does not close the gap: its sequence
evidence entry returns zero and its sufficient-statistic method returns an
empty vector. A separate tabular parser checks header identities and numeric
characters but appends an empty observation map without inserting the parsed
values. These are concrete reasons to reject the inspected path as an admitted
learning backend. They do not show that the borrowed Mocapy++ and libDAI
libraries lack their own inference capabilities. The driver also shares one
inference-engine pointer across its OpenMP region; replacing the dummy with
a mutable engine would require an explicit state-ownership design before
parallel correctness could be claimed. \citep{rel19pdbntk}

For banking, an acceptable future EM implementation would need an observation
contract, actual posterior sufficient statistics, a defined parameter update,
an evaluated objective and convergence diagnostics. Those requirements concern
parameter estimation in a supplied model; successful EM would still not turn
an observational hidden-state model into an identified intervention model.
The selected build depends on Boost, OpenMP, GMP, LAPACK and borrowed native
libraries. Inspected libDAI and Mocapy headers carry distinct notices, while
the tree has no project-root license. Resolve their respective scope before
reuse. The appropriate current role is an unfinished design reference, with
no claimed bank benefit from parallelism. \citep{rel19pdbntk}

\subsection{Reuse choices and the next affordable experiment}
~~~

Before:
~~~text
\subsection{Rust causal labels require method-level inspection}
~~~
After:
~~~text
\subsection{What the Julia delegates preserve, and what the model must identify}
\label{rel22:survival-delegates}

The survival proposal becomes more precise when the two named delegates are
read alongside their caller. The selected SurvivalBase revision declares
version 0.1.0 in June 2026; SurvivalDistributions declares 0.1.1 in July 2026.
Both carry direct MIT notices. These are pre-cutoff source snapshots, not an
observed dependency resolution or a tested installation of SurvivalModels.
Their narrower roles are valuable: SurvivalBase constructs formula terms,
and SurvivalDistributions supplies distribution families and hazard operations.
Neither supplies the treatment assignment, censoring independence or
transport assumptions needed for an intervention recommendation.
\citep{rel22survbase,rel22survdist,rel22survcaller}

SurvivalBase's \texttt{Surv} term produces two columns: observed time and an
event indicator. Its README explicitly limits the current binding to
right-censored observations. The selected term has no entry-time field or
pair of interval-censoring endpoints. The parent general-hazard likelihood
then uses the event contribution minus cumulative hazard from origin. This
composition is coherent for its stated observation type, but passing two
convenient columns cannot encode a delayed-entry experiment. Under independent
entry and censoring, conditional on the modeled covariates, an observation
entering at time $l$ and exiting at time $t$ instead contributes
\begin{equation}
 \ell=\delta\log h(t)-H(t)+H(l),\qquad t>l,
 \label{rel22:entry-likelihood}
\end{equation}
where $h$ is the hazard, $H$ its cumulative integral and $\delta$ indicates an
observed event. The last term conditions on survival to entry. It is not
recovered by relabeling entry as an interval-censoring endpoint or by retaining
only the exit time. \citep{rel22survbase,rel22survcaller,rel22survwitness}

An exact small construction shows the consequence for risk costing. Two
accounts enter at month four; one defaults at month five and the other is
right censored at month six. In a stipulated exponential model with constant
rate $\lambda$, the conditional exposure is three account-months and the
maximum-likelihood rate is $1/3$. Treating both accounts as observed from
origin gives eleven account-months and a rate of $1/11$. For a currently
surviving account, an assumed USD~100 loss on default gives next-month
expected losses of USD~28.35 and USD~8.69, respectively, using
$100(1-e^{-\lambda})$. These are calculations under the constructed
observation law, not observed bank losses or fitted Julia outputs. They show
why a right-censored worker must reject unsupported entry information or
delegate to a likelihood that retains it; an engine's convenient schema
cannot decide the scientific clock. \citep{rel22survwitness}

Even correctly encoded observations need an identifiable parameterization.
The parent general-hazard implementation composes the baseline as
\begin{equation}
 h(t\mid x,z)=h_0(te^{z\alpha})e^{x\beta},\qquad
 H(t\mid x,z)=H_0(te^{z\alpha})e^{x\beta-z\alpha}.
 \label{rel22:gh-composition}
\end{equation}
The second expression follows by changing variables in the hazard integral.
The caller's time and hazard multipliers implement this distinction; simply
multiplying cumulative hazard by the hazard-level factor would omit the
time-change correction. Yet retaining both coefficient blocks in an output
does not prove that the data distinguish them. Take a Weibull baseline with
shape $k>0$, scale $s>0$ and one covariate shared by the two blocks, so $z=x$.
Then
\begin{equation}
 H(t\mid x)=(t/s)^k
       \exp\!\left(x\{(k-1)\alpha+\beta\}\right).
 \label{rel22:weibull-ridge}
\end{equation}
Only the displayed linear combination enters the conditional law. Replacing
the coefficient pair by
$(\alpha+c,\beta-(k-1)c)$ leaves every hazard, survival probability and
right-censored likelihood contribution unchanged. For shape two, the pairs
$(0,\log 3)$ and $(\log 2,\log(3/2))$ are an exact example. One labels the
covariate as a pure hazard-level change; the other assigns part to the time
scale, although they predict the same event distribution.
\citep{rel22survcaller,rel22survwitness}

The parent removes coefficient blocks that are inactive by method definition
before inverting an observed Hessian. That avoids one source of singularity,
but its general-hazard branch retains both blocks and does not remove this
baseline-dependent ridge. With an exponential baseline, an accelerated-hazard
time coefficient disappears altogether because the baseline hazard is
constant. A finite optimizer result, inverse-Hessian request or table headed
\emph{identified parameters} cannot establish separate identification. The
defensible alternatives are to restrict the scientific model, report the
identified combination and its uncertainty, or justify a different baseline
and covariate design with diagnostics for weak identification. No distribution
fit alone turns either coefficient into a causal effect of payment holidays.
These statements concern exact parameter redundancy; no optimizer failure
frequency is claimed. \citep{rel22survcaller,rel22survwitness}

Parameter domains also have to survive the call boundary. The selected
LogLogistic family represents a logistic distribution for log event time.
Writing its location as $\mu\in\mathbb R$ and its positive scale as $\sigma$,
its median is $e^\mu$. The parent's baseline-fitting path exponentiates every
baseline parameter, however, including this location. For a baseline-only fit
without regression covariates, a median of half a month requires
$\mu=\log(1/2)<0$ and is outside that transformed fitting domain. Expressing
the same law in days gives a median of fifteen and location $\log 15>0$.
Changing units should not change whether a physical distribution belongs to
the admitted model. A distribution-specific transformation must distinguish
unrestricted locations from positive scales and shapes. Regression may
absorb some scale changes in other designs, so this example is deliberately
limited to the baseline-only fit. It neither asserts that every baseline is
affected nor predicts the optimizer's returned approximation.
\citep{rel22survdist,rel22survcaller,rel22survwitness}

\subsection{Tail behavior and the meaning of a survival output}
\label{rel22:survival-tail}

The piecewise-constant hazard delegate exposes a second consequential
boundary. It declares that the last supplied hazard continues after the final
finite time interval. Its inspected quantile loop nevertheless returns that
interval's endpoint when the requested cumulative hazard exceeds the
accumulated finite-grid hazard. The cumulative-hazard tail refers to
\texttt{L} although the argument is \texttt{D}; the expectation method takes
\texttt{L} but accesses \texttt{D}. Those names are not bound by the inspected
module. The abstract hazard fallback likewise calls numerical helpers that
the selected module does not import or define. These are specific source
obstacles to admitting these paths, not evidence that every distribution in
the package fails. Fixing a variable name alone would not establish the
intended tail law or qualify the numerical implementation.
\citep{rel22survdist}

For a one-month grid interval with hazard $1/5$ per month continuing forever,
the stipulated law is simply exponential. Its ninetieth percentile is
\begin{equation}
 q_{0.9}=5\log 10\simeq11.513\text{ months},
 \label{rel22:tail-quantile}
\end{equation}
whereas the finite-loop trace returns one month. Survival at that endpoint
is still about 0.819, so the difference cannot be dismissed as a negligible
tail. At a stipulated continuous receipt rate of USD~100 per month until
termination, with no discounting, competing event or cost, expected total
receipts are USD~500. Receipts restricted to the first month are only
USD~90.63. Both are legitimate targets when labeled; silently imposing the
grid endpoint would substitute one for the other. A bank requiring a finite
valuation horizon should specify that horizon and integrate to it. A model
claiming an unbounded mean must establish a proper tail and finite expectation.
The example reconstructs the source branch algebraically and does not run the
downloaded Julia implementation. \citep{rel22survdist,rel22survwitness}

Output names are another source of silent substitution. In the parent,
\texttt{predict\_expected} returns cumulative hazard; the survival method
exponentiates its negative. Cumulative hazard is neither a mean event time nor
an event probability. If it equals two at the chosen horizon, the corresponding
event probability is $1-e^{-2}\simeq0.865$. With an assumed USD~100 loss on
that event, expected loss is USD~86.47, not USD~200. The no-time prediction
also evaluates each training subject at its own observed time, whereas a
portfolio decision generally requires a common future horizon. The adapter
must carry target, units, population and horizon with each array rather than
infer them from a method name. \citep{rel22survcaller,rel22survwitness}

These findings support a conditional comparison, with distinct admission
decisions for right-censored parametric fitting, a piecewise hazard
representation and a general-hazard coefficient model. Preserve the useful
formula and distribution interfaces while testing entry/censoring meaning,
parameter domains, common-horizon prediction, likelihood rank, tail inversion
and time-unit changes against independent laws. Include optimizer convergence
and uncertainty failures in the result: the inspected fit stores the minimizer
and objective without retaining a convergence flag in its model fields.
Native qualification and transitive dependency review remain to be done.
The direct MIT notices identify the selected components' declared terms;
they do not establish scientific calibration or clear every dependency.
For the banking decision, choose the least elaborate model whose assumptions
and identified outputs answer the question, and retain uncertainty about the
causal interpretation separately from uncertainty within a fitted survival law.
\citep{rel22survbase,rel22survdist,rel22survcaller}


\subsection{Rust causal labels require method-level inspection}
~~~

## Edit 100

~~~text
longitudinal reference while keeping adoption and coverage as later claims.

~~~
After:
~~~text
longitudinal reference while keeping adoption and coverage as later claims.

\subsection{Centering the influence function before weighting and clustering}
\label{rel23:ife-centering}

The selected uncertainty delegate sharpens the distinction between a policy
value and the uncertainty of that value. The inspected \texttt{ife} revision
declares version 0.2.5 in July 2026 and GPL version 3 or later. It stores a
point estimate, a vector called the influence function, weights, cluster
identifiers and a critical value. Arithmetic operations propagate derivatives
through the vector. This is a useful interface for retaining the dependence
between two policies evaluated on the same observations. It does not construct
the correct influence function from an arbitrary prediction vector. The
separately inspected lmtp revision permits \texttt{ife} versions from 0.2.0;
its resolved dependency is not observed here. The delegate's own change log
records changes to cluster handling in 0.2.1 and 0.2.5, so the lower version
bound cannot identify the behavior of a particular installation.
\citep{rel23ife,exec22_lmtp_reuse}

The boundary matters because the retained lmtp caller supplies an
\emph{uncentered} sequential pseudo-outcome: weighted residual contributions
plus an initial shifted prediction. It rescales that vector and its point
estimate to the outcome's original units, then forwards both to \texttt{ife}.
The delegate stores the supplied vector without subtracting the estimate.
For independent, equally weighted rows, sample variance removes a common
constant. That familiar simplification fails when multiplication by unequal
weights or aggregation over unequal cluster sizes occurs first.
\citep{exec22_lmtp_reuse,rel23ifecaller,rel23ife}

To see the statistical issue without fitting a nuisance model, let $Z_i$ be
a supplied pseudo-outcome and $w_i$ a nonnegative sampling weight. Suppose
the target is the ratio of weighted population moments, estimated by
\begin{equation}
 \widehat\theta=\frac{\sum_i w_iZ_i}{\sum_i w_i},\qquad
 \widetilde w_i=\frac{w_i}{n^{-1}\sum_j w_j},\qquad
 D_i=\widetilde w_i(Z_i-\widehat\theta).
 \label{rel23:weighted-influence}
\end{equation}
Here $n$ is the number of independent observations and $D_i$ is the empirical
influence contribution for this ratio. Differentiating the numerator divided
by the denominator gives the subtraction in the last expression; it accounts
for variation in the denominator as well as the weighted outcome. With
positive finite mean weight, finite second moments and an appropriate
independent sampling law, the usual first-order variance estimate is the
sample variance of these contributions divided by $n$. Fitted longitudinal
pseudo-outcomes additionally require the identifying and nuisance-estimation
conditions developed earlier. This elementary ratio calculation does not
supply those conditions. \citep{rel23ifewitness}

The lmtp task mean-normalizes supplied weights. The selected delegate,
however, first multiplies its supplied vector by those weights. In an exact
authored example, take pseudo-outcomes $(1,2,4,5)$ dollars and normalized
weights $(1/2,1/2,3/2,3/2)$. The weighted mean is USD~3.75. Applying the
displayed centered formula gives a variance estimate of $101/192$ squared
dollars and standard error USD~0.725. The uncentered weighted vector instead
gives $149/48$ and standard error USD~1.762 under the documented sample-variance
convention. Add a known USD~100 accounting offset to every pseudo-outcome.
The centered contributions are unchanged, as uncertainty about an unknown
mean should be under a known shift. The uncentered formula now gives
$14983/16$ and standard error USD~30.601. These are exact source-formula
calculations, not executed R results or estimates of actual bank uncertainty.
\citep{rel23ife,rel23ifecaller,rel23ifewitness}

The same defect can survive a contrast. Consider a stipulated second reward
equal to the first reward plus a known USD~2 for every account, using the
same weights. Their estimated weighted-mean difference is identically two;
its influence contribution is zero. Subtracting the two uncentered vectors
instead yields a constant two, which becomes the unequal vector of twice
the weights when the delegate calculates uncertainty. Its sample-variance
estimate divided by four is $1/3$ squared dollars. This example is a known
reward transformation, not evidence of a constant causal policy effect. It
shows why subtracting point estimates and retaining paired rows still does
not repair an incorrectly centered uncertainty representation.
\citep{rel23ifewitness}

For several independent clusters, the inspected delegate sums weighted
contributions within each cluster and multiplies each sum by the number of
clusters divided by the number of rows. With correctly centered individual
contributions, this gives the empirical cluster influence
\begin{equation}
 G_k=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in C_k}D_i,\qquad
 \widehat{\operatorname{Var}}(\widehat\theta)
       =\frac{s_G^2}{K},
 \label{rel23:cluster-influence}
\end{equation}
where $C_k$ is cluster $k$, $K$ is the number of independent clusters and
$s_G^2$ is their sample variance. Cluster-level inference needs a justified
sampling law and enough independent clusters without a dominating cluster.
The target here remains an individual-weighted mean; averaging branch means
with equal branch weight would define a different target. The presence of an ID column
cannot provide them. As an algebraic check, use four unweighted outcomes
$(0,0,2,2)$, with the first account in one cluster and the remaining three in
another. Centering at their mean gives cluster contributions $(-1/2,1/2)$
and variance estimate $1/4$. The uncentered calculation uses $(0,2)$ and gives
one. Adding the same known USD~100 offset changes these latter values to
$(50,152)$ and the variance estimate to 2,601. Two clusters are sufficient to
expose this origin dependence; they are not a calibration study. The source
also deliberately treats exactly one cluster as independent rows, a choice
its test asserts. An adapter requiring cluster-robust uncertainty should
return that uncertainty as unavailable when only one independent cluster
exists, instead of silently substituting the independent-row sampling law.
\citep{rel23ife,rel23ifewitness}

\subsection{A paired contrast must retain its scale and sampling identities}
\label{rel23:ife-contrasts}

The delegate's arithmetic is useful once the incoming influence representation
is correct. A difference subtracts corresponding contributions; a ratio uses
the numerator derivative and the denominator derivative jointly. This retains
their covariance. Before combining two objects, the inspected code requires
equal vector lengths and identical weight and ID arrays. Those checks help,
but cluster labels do not uniquely identify the individuals within a cluster.
The delivery contract must bind each influence contribution to the same
original observation across fits, as well as to its cluster and weight.
Reordering records while regenerating positional IDs can pass an array-level
check while changing the pairing. Such a change alters uncertainty without
changing either marginal point estimate. \citep{rel23ife}

The newly inspected lmtp contrast path computes relative-risk and odds-ratio
contrasts on the log scale, obtains a symmetric interval there and then
exponentiates its endpoints. It also exponentiates the point estimate, but
leaves the returned \texttt{std.error} column on the log scale. That is usable
when documented; interpreting every numeric column as being on the reported
ratio scale is a conversion error. For a ratio of 1.5 and log-scale standard
error 0.2, the delta-method ratio-scale standard error is 0.3. A conventional
normal critical value gives an exponentiated log interval approximately
$[1.014,2.220]$, compared with $[0.912,2.088]$ for a symmetric interval formed
on the ratio scale. Neither arithmetic construction proves finite-sample
coverage, especially near a weak denominator. The delegate's rejection of
denominators smaller than machine epsilon is a numerical guard, not a test
that a denominator is statistically separated from zero.
\citep{rel23ifecaller,rel23ife,rel23ifewitness}

The caller warns when a numeric reference is used because an estimated
reference treated as known loses its uncertainty. Its print method warns
about unadjusted multiple comparisons. The delegate permits a configurable
critical value but prints a fixed 95\% interval label. A bank-facing result
therefore needs the actual contrast, scale of every uncertainty field,
critical value, sampling unit and comparison family. A method name or a
formatted confidence label should not supply these facts by implication.
\citep{rel23ifecaller,rel23ife}

The recommendation is conditional reuse of the arithmetic interface after
qualifying the incoming centered influence function, mean-weight normalization,
individual pairing and independent clusters. Compare a known outcome shift,
a deterministic reward increment, unequal cluster sizes, a single-cluster
request and log-versus-ratio output against independent laws before relying
on a standard error. A simpler independent implementation of the declared
influence calculation is a serious alternative for a narrow initial worker;
it must meet the same statistical requirements. Native dependency resolution,
the numerical variance delegate, full survival handling, learner behavior and
random-number coordination remain separate qualification obligations. These
source findings strengthen the admission conditions for the longitudinal
reference; they do not establish an executed package failure rate, calibrated
bank uncertainty or an observed business benefit.
\citep{rel23ife,rel23ifecaller,rel23ifewitness}

~~~
