## Edit 104
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/0cdefa558bd74864.tex

Develop selected fitting-population and observation-clock contracts with exact banking consequences.

Substantive destination: Two additive subsections inside the Python comparison; all inherited zEpid/pygformula derivations, uncertainty caveats and alternatives remain.

Before:
~~~text
\subsection{Integration choices and the remaining survey boundary}
~~~
After:
~~~text
\subsection{Deterministic histories must change the fitting population}
A simulator can enforce a structural rule while estimating the stochastic
part of that rule on the wrong records. The selected pygformula documentation
describes a covariate restriction as a known value outside specified histories.
It directs the fitting step to exclude those deterministic histories, then
directs simulation to assign their known value. For a bank, eligibility may
make a service-completion indicator identically zero: the completion model
then describes eligible accounts, rather than an average that includes
accounts unable to receive the service. This is a distinction between two
conditional distributions, even before any causal effect is considered.
\citep{rel25pygfit}

The documented input is a list of restrictions, each beginning with the
covariate's name. In the selected fitting helper, the list used to identify
restricted covariates repeats the whole first restriction instead of extracting
each name. Its subsequent membership test compares a name with those lists,
so the documented input does not enter the intended filtering branch.
The simulation helper does extract the names and assigns deterministic
values on the corresponding histories. The constructor forwards the supplied
restrictions directly. Thus the two steps implement different populations
on this selected path; this conclusion follows from inspected source, not
from an executed package experiment. It is specific to this revision and
schema, and does not assert that every fitted model changes numerically.
\citep{rel25pygfit}

An exact constructed likelihood shows when the distinction matters. Let
$E$ indicate eligibility, $Z$ completion and $X$ a binary predictor. At a
post-baseline fitting time, each value of $X$ has ten eligible records,
eight with $Z=1$ and two with $Z=0$, and ten ineligible records with the known
value $Z=0$. Use the logistic model
\[
 \operatorname{logit}\Pr(Z=1\mid X,E=1)=\beta_0+\beta_1X.
\]
Restricting estimation to eligible records gives fitted probability $4/5$
in both predictor cells. Pooling the deterministic zeros gives $2/5$ in both.
For either fit, the intercept and slope scores vanish at the corresponding
constant probability; both cells contain successes and failures, and the
information matrix is positive definite. These are finite, unique likelihood
solutions, not an appeal to an intercept-only formula that the input parser
might handle differently. The predictor is deliberately uninformative within
eligibility; no claim is made about a model that separately represents every
eligibility history.~\citep{rel25pygwitness}

If half the simulated accounts are eligible and the other half are correctly
set to zero, the completion mean is $2/5$ under restricted estimation but
$1/5$ under the pooled fit. A stipulated USD 100 receipt contingent on completion
and USD 30 cost per account give expected margins of USD 10 and USD $-10$.
This is a contract-payoff calculation under an invented law, not an estimated
causal service benefit. Increasing simulation size converges more precisely
to the respective fitted laws; it cannot repair their different fitting
populations. The proposed worker should therefore retain the exact fitting
row set for each transition, verify deterministic assignments separately, and
withhold this restriction profile until a corrected native path reproduces
the declared law. An explicitly fitted stochastic-subpopulation model is a
substantive alternative, provided that its conditioning histories and
standardization population remain visible.~\citep{rel25pygwitness,rel25pygfit}

\subsection{Censoring labels and competing risks define different denominators}
The input clock places covariates on interval $k$ and the outcome on
interval $k+1$. The selected outcome helper uses nonmissing outcomes at
nonnegative times; with explicit competing events it also requires no current
competing event. A censoring-name argument is not passed to that helper.
The censoring model instead supports the observed natural-course inverse
probability weighted comparator. Supplying that model therefore does not
automatically select the outcome likelihood rows. The documentation permits
missing or zero outcomes on a survival censoring row under its stated
fine-interval qualification; for an end-of-follow-up outcome it requests a
missing value. These conventions concern which event information is observed,
not merely how a missing field is displayed.~\citep{rel25pygfit}

For a deliberately coarse interval, suppose 100 accounts supply eight observed
defaults, 72 observed nondefaults and 20 outcomes unknown after censoring.
A constant outcome likelihood over observed outcomes gives $8/80=1/10$;
coding the unknown outcomes zero gives $8/100=2/25$. A stipulated contract
paying USD 10,000 on default and charging USD 900 has model-based margins
USD 100 and USD $-100$, respectively. Neither choice alone identifies the
complete finite-population risk: the 20 unknown outcomes permit a proportion
between $2/25$ and $7/25$. Interpreting the observed-outcome fit as a target
risk additionally requires an appropriate conditional independent-censoring
assumption, support and model specification. This construction does not
contradict a small-interval approximation; it shows why that approximation
must be justified before applying the convention to coarse monthly data.
\citep{rel25pygwitness,rel25pygfit}

A competing event supplies different information. Consider 100 accounts with
20 prepayments, eight defaults and 72 accounts experiencing neither event.
With prepayment ordered first, its probability is $q=1/5$ and the conditional
default probability among the 80 nonprepaying accounts is $p=1/10$.
The factorization gives
\[
 \Pr(\text{default in the interval})=(1-q)p=\frac{2}{25}.
\]
This matches the selected outcome-fitting restriction and the simulator's
current competing-survival multiplier. Substituting the marginal default
frequency $2/25$ for the conditional hazard would apply that multiplier twice
and yield $8/125$. Consequently, the explicit competing-event branch is
coherent on this clock; it should not be repaired by indiscriminately putting
prepayment rows into the default denominator. The earlier two-period
comparison, $86/625$ against $19/100$ when prepayment is eliminated, retains
its distinct hypothetical targets. Exact records, event order and risk-set
denominators are the common basis for comparing Python and R implementations;
matching a method name or a plotted curve is insufficient.
\citep{rel25pygwitness,rel25pygfit}


\subsection{Integration choices and the remaining survey boundary}
~~~

~~~text
an empirical trial. It does not justify rejecting every method in the same
library, nor does a corrected formula alone establish native qualification.
The next comparison must reproduce the intended fitting population, output
scale and sampling unit, including adverse cases that expose those differences.
That requirement makes conditional reuse more precise: retain a useful original
method where its contract holds, repair and verify a specific mismatch, or
choose a substantively matched alternative.
~~~

## Edit 117
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/7ccd8624e4c82556.tex

Explain the signed nature of an inverse treatment bridge and the consequence of generic positive-weight clipping.

Substantive destination: All existing identification, outcome-bridge, remainder, rate and minimax material remains; the new subsection extends the same finite example with its treatment bridge and exact clipping counterexample.

Before:
~~~text
\section{Learning the bridge is a conditional-moment problem}
~~~
After:
~~~text
\subsection{A treatment bridge need not be a positive weight}
\label{rel29:signed-proxy-bridge}
Equation~\eqref{r3:treatment-bridge} constrains a conditional average of
the bridge, not its pointwise sign. It is an inverse equation, rather
than the definition of an inverse propensity. This distinction follows
from the source's treatment-bridge condition
\citep[Section 4, Assumption 7 and equation (9)]{ghassami2022}.
The finite banking construction above makes the consequence explicit.
Solving its two conditional equations for each action gives
\[
 q(z,0)=-\frac{25}{16}+\frac{75}{8}z,\qquad
 q(z,1)=\frac{125}{16}-\frac{75}{8}z.
\]
For example, among untreated units the probabilities of $Z=1$ given
$W=0$ and $W=1$ are $23/74$ and $15/26$, respectively. Substituting
them gives conditional bridge means $50/37$ and $50/13$. These are
exactly the reciprocals of the untreated probabilities $37/50$ and
$13/50$ given those proxy values. Thus a bridge can take the negative
value $-25/16$ while satisfying both positive conditional targets.
The treated equations give the symmetric construction. These are
project calculations under the already stated finite law, not an
additional empirical finding or a theorem that all bridges are signed.

With these bridges, direct enumeration gives
\[
 \E[\ind\{A=0\}q(Z,0)Y]=\frac32,\qquad
 \E[\ind\{A=1\}q(Z,1)Y]=\frac72.
\]
Their difference is the causal effect $2$. If a generic weighting
routine replaces every negative bridge value by zero, the corresponding
means become $105/64$ and $185/32$, producing the incorrect contrast
$265/64$. The total clipped weight is $95/64$ in each arm, so
normalizing the clipped weights does not repair the result: it gives
arm means $21/19$ and $74/19$, and contrast $53/19$. Positivity of the
action mechanism has not failed; clipping has broken the bridge
equations. If the outcome bridge were also correct, augmentation
would still have its separate outcome-bridge robustness, but the
clipped treatment bridge could no longer justify the other branch.

This example changes the implementation choice. A proxy-bridge solver
must preserve the signed solution and evaluate its conditional moments,
second moments and regularization sensitivity. Large signed values can
signal an unstable inverse problem. Restricting or clipping them is a
new approximation whose bias must be analyzed; calling the operation
ordinary weight stabilization would conceal the changed estimating
equation and potentially change a cost-based decision.

\section{Learning the bridge is a conditional-moment problem}
~~~

## Edit 118

After:
~~~text
\subsection{A variance correction does not fix the target of invalid moments}
\label{rel32:gmm-variance-target}
The follow-up by \citet[Sections 2--4]{rel32hkl} supplies a primary
comparison of the two corrections. The inspected text is the May 2020
preprint, whose title page is dated 1 June; the later journal version
and the Windmeijer original are distinct texts. The follow-up derives
both the uncertainty from estimating the first-stage weight and the
additional variation carried by a nonzero overidentified moment vector.
Under correct specification, those residual-dependent contributions
vanish from the leading asymptotic variance. When no parameter makes
all population moments zero, they can remain first-order terms.

Continue with $g_i(\theta)=b_i-G_i\theta$ and independent entities.
For clarity, take the initial weight to be the identity and distinguish
the uncentered second moment from the covariance:
\[
 \theta_1=\arg\min_\theta\|\E g_i(\theta)\|^2,\qquad
 \Omega_1=\E[g_i(\theta_1)g_i(\theta_1)'],\qquad W=\Omega_1^{-1},
\]
\[
 \theta_2=\arg\min_\theta
       \{\E g_i(\theta)\}'W\{\E g_i(\theta)\},\qquad
 r_j=\E g_i(\theta_j),\quad H_1=G'G,\quad H_2=G'WG.
\]
The weight in the second criterion is evaluated at the first-stage
limit and held fixed during minimization. In particular, this is not
continuous updating. The first-order conditions give $G'r_1=0$ and
$G'Wr_2=0$; they do not imply that either residual vector vanishes.
Assume fixed moment and parameter dimensions, unique interior minima,
full column rank of $G$, nonsingular $\Omega_1$, and the finite moments
needed for the joint central limit theorem. The source's Assumption 1
states fourth-moment conditions for the first-stage moments and
Jacobians, and a second-moment condition at the second-stage target.
These are substantive regularity requirements, not a guarantee for
weak or proliferating instruments.

A direct differentiation of the two first-order conditions gives an
interpretable form of the source's equations (29)--(40). The first-stage
influence is
\[
 \psi_{1i}=H_1^{-1}
 \{G'[g_i(\theta_1)-r_1]+(G_i-G)'r_1\}.
\]
Let $\Omega_{1,j}$ denote the derivative of
$\E[g_i(\theta)g_i(\theta)']$ in coordinate $j$, evaluated at
$\theta_1$. Define the columns of $D$ and the second-stage influence
with the first-stage argument held fixed by
\[
 D_{\cdot j}=-H_2^{-1}G'W\Omega_{1,j}Wr_2,
\]
\[
 \begin{split}
 \kappa_i=H_2^{-1}\bigl\{&(G_i-G)'Wr_2
                +G'W[g_i(\theta_2)-r_2]\\
                &-G'W[g_i(\theta_1)g_i(\theta_1)'-\Omega_1]Wr_2
                    \bigr\}.
 \end{split}
\]
Then $\psi_{2i}=\kappa_i+D\psi_{1i}$ and the asymptotic covariance
of $\sqrt N(\hat\theta_2-\theta_2)$ is
\[
 \begin{split}
 V_2={}&\Var(\kappa_i)+D\Var(\psi_{1i})D'\\
       &+D\Cov(\psi_{1i},\kappa_i)
          +\Cov(\kappa_i,\psi_{1i})D'.
 \end{split}
\]
The covariance of the estimator itself is $V_2/N$. Each term has a
separate origin: moment variation, the estimated Jacobian, the estimated
second-moment matrix and the first-stage fit. Under correct specification,
$r_1=r_2=0$, so the residual-dependent pieces and $D$ disappear from
this population influence. Their fitted counterparts need not vanish
in a finite overidentified sample. Keeping them leads to the corrected
formulas in the source. If the first-stage weight is also estimated,
its influence supplies a further term; the identity-weight display here
must not be reused unchanged for an arbitrary panel adapter.

\subsection{An exact misspecified law distinguishes uncertainty from validity}
Consider the following project construction, chosen for transparent
arithmetic rather than as a fitted bank model. Let $U$ and $V$ be independent
fair signs, and set
\[
 G_i=\begin{pmatrix}1\\1+U/2\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 b_i=\begin{pmatrix}1+U\\3+2V\end{pmatrix}.
\]
The two population moments are $1-\theta$ and $3-\theta$; no common
zero exists. The first-stage target is $\theta_1=2$. Its uncentered
second moment and the resulting second-stage target are
\[
 \Omega_1=\begin{pmatrix}2&-2\\-2&6\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \theta_2=\frac53,\qquad D=\frac{11}{36}.
\]
In the order $(U,V)=(-1,-1),(-1,1),(1,-1),(1,1)$, exact substitution gives
\[
 \psi_1=\left(-\frac54,\frac34,-\frac34,\frac54\right),\qquad
 \psi_2=\left(-\frac{151}{144},\frac{65}{144},
                     -\frac{97}{144},\frac{61}{48}\right).
\]
Both have mean zero. The full second-stage asymptotic variance is
$5827/6912$, approximately $.8430$. Retaining only variation in the
moment vector while fixing the Jacobian and weight gives $193/324$,
approximately $.5957$; the efficient correctly specified formula
$H_2^{-1}$ gives $2/3$. Neither is the full variance for this law.
Independent differentiation of the estimator functional with respect to
all four cell probabilities reproduces the displayed influences.
This checks the calculation, not finite-sample coverage.

Centering the weight also changes the target under misspecification.
Here $r_1=(-1,1)'$, so replacing $\Omega_1$ by
$\Omega_1-r_1r_1'$ gives the different second-stage target $3/2$.
This is why the source's centered-weight alternative changes both
its derivative and its pseudo-true estimand
\citep[Section 4, Remark 2]{rel32hkl}. It is not merely a different
standard-error label on an unchanged population object.

The banking consequence precedes any choice of confidence interval.
Suppose an analyst attaches the illustrative contribution rule
$100\theta-160$ dollars per account to these coefficients. The one-step,
uncentered two-step and centered two-step targets would produce
\USD{40}, \USD{6.67} and $-\USD{10}$, respectively. These are three
values of an assumed reporting map. None is an identified intervention
value, because the construction supplies inconsistent moment restrictions
and no causal response model. A more accurate standard error around one
pseudo-true target cannot justify treating it as the bank's policy effect.
The valid conclusion is to revisit the information and exclusion argument,
or explicitly defend an approximation target and its economic use.

\subsection{What this comparison permits an implementation to claim}
The follow-up's Theorem 1 concerns fixed-dimensional, full-rank linear
GMM with the stated sampling and moment conditions. Its correction is
not a general weak-instrument or many-instrument remedy. The paper also
distinguishes iterated GMM from continuous updating and explains why the
same finite-sample argument does not automatically extend to nonlinear
moment models \citep[Sections 1, 4--6]{rel32hkl}. The score construction
of \citet{kleibergen2021}, discussed below, answers a different question:
its robustness to identification and moment misspecification should not
be conflated with this variance correction.

A detail in the selected preprint's proof requires care. After equation
(94), it infers that a fifth derivative of an inverse-weight functional
vanishes because the underlying second-moment matrix is quadratic.
Matrix inversion does not preserve polynomial degree. In the finite
construction above, put
\[
 Q(\phi)=\{G'\Omega(\phi)^{-1}G\}^{-1}
              G'\Omega(\phi)^{-1}r_2
       =-\frac{2(\phi-24)(\phi-2)}{3(\phi^2+4\phi+36)}.
\]
Although $\Omega(\phi)$ is quadratic, $Q^{(5)}(2)=-725/31104\ne0$.
This disproves that proof step, not the asymptotic covariance conclusion.
The local Taylor argument instead uses smooth inversion on a neighborhood
where the weight and curvature stay nonsingular. A bounded second
derivative and a root-$N$ first-stage error give an unscaled quadratic
remainder of order $O_p(N^{-1})$, hence $o_p(1)$ after multiplication
by $\sqrt N$. The present derivation relies on this local argument;
it does not certify every claim or the uninspected journal version.

For the panel comparison that follows, the historical point estimates,
first-order sandwiches and all adverse coverage results remain exactly
as reported. This primary comparison supports a distinct implementation
candidate: preserve entity-level moment vectors, expose the weight
convention and target, include the required derivative and cross-covariance
terms, and then qualify the complete estimator on a declared design.
No corrected native panel result has been produced here. Replacing the
reported standard errors without rerunning that design would misstate
the evidence; treating misspecification robustness as instrument validity
would misstate the scientific question.

\subsection{A paired comparison and a banking decision rule}
~~~

## Edit 138
docs/release/2026-10-29/source/docs/writing-repair/2026-10-29/src/9334e60d9efb633b.tex

The21 sentence-final citations in the pgmpy comparison render as unpunctuated narrative author names. Use parenthetical citation commands consistently with their grammatical position and surrounding scholarly prose.

Substantive destination: All21 citation keys/groups, complete prose, equations, labels, alternatives, numeric facts and adverse findings remain in this same pgmpy section; only cite becomes citep. Exact reverse normalization recovers the preceding file.

Before:
~~~text
\section{A restricted pgmpy integration: verify the candidate, preserve the question}
\label{exec13:pgmpy-reuse}

The proposal selects a narrow pgmpy integration for generating an adjustment
candidate from a supplied, fully observed causal DAG. The project must then
verify that candidate independently before reporting it. This choice reuses
a permissively licensed graph library while preserving a scientific boundary:
a successful method call does not prove that the supplied graph describes
the bank, that the requested adjustment identifies the intended effect, or
that a numerical estimator has valid uncertainty. The selection authorizes
development of this limited prototype path; it is not evidence that an
adapter has been implemented or executed
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary,exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The source is pinned to the repository revision beginning
\texttt{1f6296fc2606}, committed on 5 October 2026 and selected by the
cutoff-bounded history query. Its project metadata declares version 1.1.2.
Fifteen inspected source, guide, test and licensing files match the selected
Git tree, in addition to their local SHA-256 identities. Source acquisition
occurred after the 7 October cutoff. The pin establishes which implementation
is discussed; neither a late repository snapshot nor a recent commit measures
maintainer responsiveness or long-term support
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

\subsection{A guide, a graph type and an implemented method are separate evidence}

The identification guide presents minimal, variance-optimal and all-set
adjustment routes. The pinned implementation gives a more qualified picture.
Its minimal-variance branch raises \texttt{NotImplementedError}. The minimal
branch explicitly requires a single exposure and outcome, constructs a
proper backdoor graph, then obtains a minimal separator. The all-set branch
enumerates subsets of an ancestor candidate set after removing specified
causal-path and latent variables. These operations cannot be combined into
a claim that every documented graph class and every advertised adjustment
variant is implemented with the same semantics
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

The distinction between minimal and minimum matters even without statistical
estimation. A set is inclusion-minimal when no proper subset remains valid.
It is minimum-cardinality when no valid set has fewer elements. The inspected
separator routine greedily removes dispensable members of an initial set;
that does not establish a global cardinality minimum. Nor does cardinality
alone determine estimator variance, data acquisition cost or missingness.
A front end should not translate the option name into the promise
``the cheapest or most precise set of controls''
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

An exact graph illustrates the difference. Let $C$ cause $A$, $B$ and $Y$;
let $A$ and $B$ cause $X$; and include $X\to Y$. The two inclusion-minimal
backdoor sets are $\{C\}$ and $\{A,B\}$. Conditioning on $C$ blocks both
forks; without $C$, both $A$ and $B$ are needed. A project reference that
deletes dispensable variables from $\{A,B,C\}$ obtains either answer
depending on deletion order. That computation describes the semantics of
greedy minimality; it is not a measured pgmpy output. For a lending example,
one verified common driver may be cheaper to collect than two separate
proxies, but its actual measurement quality and availability still determine
whether the smaller set is useful \cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The package's all-set path is also a bounded algorithmic choice. Enumerating
subsets can require work exponential in the candidate count. Restricting
the candidates to ancestors is meaningful, but a consumer must retain that
domain rather than describe the output as all imaginable observed-variable
sets. Variables irrelevant to the target, descendant restrictions and
generalized adjustment criteria introduce different enumeration questions.
The first proposed wrapper therefore excludes both all-set enumeration and
the unimplemented minimum-variance option. It asks for one candidate and
checks its stated sufficient criterion
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

\subsection{A total-effect validator must reject the wrong question}

The inspected public validation call first checks the supported graph type
and invokes a graph-role check. The latter checks the presence of exposure
and outcome roles. The adjustment validator then tests whether parents of
the exposure remain connected to the outcome conditional on the exposure
and proposed adjustment variables. This particular call path does not
independently exclude descendants of the exposure
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

Consider the supplied DAG $X\to M\to Y$ with $X$ a root. Its exposure
has no parents, so the inspected parent's-connection predicate is an empty
conjunction and evaluates to true, even if the proposed adjustment variable
is the mediator $M$. The role-presence check does not reject that assignment.
This is a static trace through authentic source, not an installed-engine
experiment. It suffices to show why the project cannot adopt that validation
call alone as its total-effect admission rule. The finding concerns this
specific validator and pin; it does not assert that every identification
method returns an invalid set
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary,exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The financial significance can be derived exactly. Let independent,
zero-mean, unit-variance errors generate
\[
 X=U_X,\qquad M=2X+U_M,\qquad Y=3M+U_Y.
\]
Under an intervention on $X$, the derivative of expected $Y$ is $6$.
The covariance entries are
\[
 \operatorname{Var}(X)=1,\quad \operatorname{Var}(M)=5,\quad
 \operatorname{Cov}(X,M)=2,\quad
 \operatorname{Cov}(X,Y)=6,\quad \operatorname{Cov}(M,Y)=15.
\]
The coefficient of $X$ in the population regression on $(X,M)$ is
\[
 \frac{6\cdot5-2\cdot15}{1\cdot5-2^2}=0.
\]
Conditioning on the mediator has removed the mediated path. Reporting that
coefficient as the total effect would change the causal question. If $X$
is a one-unit service intensity, $Y$ is dollars over the agreed horizon and
the common linear law is stipulated for $1{,}000$ accounts, the authored
gross response scenario is $6{,}000$ dollars, compared with zero under the
misreported coefficient. This is generated arithmetic, not bank ROI, a
binary-treatment guarantee or a measured customer response
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The proposed first path consequently requires a conservative pre-action
backdoor contract. A candidate set must contain observed variables distinct
from exposure and outcome, include no descendants of the exposure, and
d-separate exposure and outcome in the graph with outgoing exposure edges
removed. This is a sufficient contract for the selected DAG setting. It
is intentionally narrower than generalized adjustment over all supported
graph classes. An otherwise useful generalized set outside the contract
receives an unsupported-scope disposition; it is not declared scientifically
impossible. The separation between a restricted sufficient criterion and
complete identification is part of the result's meaning.

Two project-owned graph calculations support the proposed verification
boundary. One tests separation after ancestral moralization. The other
enumerates simple skeleton paths, checks noncollider conditioning and checks
whether colliders have conditioned descendants. They agree on $26{,}064$
queries across all $543$ labeled DAGs on four nodes, including every ordered
exposure--outcome pair and every subset of the remaining nodes. The check
helps detect mistakes in the small reference implementation; it is not a
proof for arbitrary graph size, an upstream pgmpy test run or a statement
about statistical discovery from finite samples
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

\subsection{A selected DAG can conceal unresolved direction or a failed extension}

Static discovery remains a separate reuse decision. The pinned PC class
supports original, stable and parallel variants; its defaults include
parallel execution, test level $.01$, a maximum conditioning-set size of
five and a partially directed return. In the fitted method, the names
\texttt{pdag} and \texttt{cpdag} follow the same return branch.
The \texttt{dag} option calls a conversion selecting one orientation.
The option spelling alone cannot establish a complete equivalence class,
whole-graph confidence or causal identification under hidden confounding.
The source declares causal sufficiency, acyclicity and faithfulness;
particular conditional-independence tests add their own assumptions
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

The default conditioning cap can stop search before all possible separating
sets have been considered. Test selection, data types and expert constraints
must therefore be retained alongside the graph. A per-test threshold of
$.01$ is not a $.99$ confidence level for every edge or the downstream
effect. Required and forbidden edges introduce external information;
their provenance must survive any business-language or agent-assisted
formulation step. A graph's attractive rendering cannot supply that
provenance \cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

The PDAG implementation contains a particularly clear conversion boundary.
Its \texttt{to\_dag} routine warns when it cannot find a consistent
extension preserving directed edges and v-structures, then orients remaining
edges arbitrarily while avoiding cycles. The result can therefore be an
acyclic graph with different v-structures. The \texttt{to\_cpdag} route
first obtains an extension when undirected edges exist, so it cannot be
used as an independent certificate that the input represented a valid
equivalence class \cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

A four-node chordless undirected cycle supplies an exact refusal fixture.
There are sixteen orientations of its four edges; two form directed cycles
and fourteen are acyclic. Every acyclic orientation has a sink with two
nonadjacent parents and therefore introduces an unshielded collider.
Consequently none preserves the undirected graph's absence of specified
colliders. The project enumerates all fourteen acyclic cases to check this
small argument. An arbitrary DAG returned after a warning would answer a
different graphical question; simply confirming acyclicity would miss the
change \cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The initial integration therefore consumes an already supplied DAG rather
than an automatically completed discovered graph. Future discovery
integration must preserve typed directed and undirected edges, isolates,
separating sets, constraints, warnings and the exact selection procedure.
Named-edge conversion is preferable to guessing direction from an unlabeled
binary matrix. An incomplete or nonextendible graph must remain an explicit
scientific or computational limitation, with no fallback to a conveniently
oriented causal story.

\subsection{The permitted prototype path and its failure contract}

The proposed operation accepts one exposure and one outcome in a fully
observed acyclic graph, with unique string node identifiers and an explicit
node list. It initially caps the graph at twelve nodes as a conservative
wrapper design choice; that number is not measured engine capacity.
Latent, bidirected, selection, undirected and ambiguous-edge inputs are
outside this path. It constructs a fresh pinned DAG with explicit roles
and requests the minimal adjustment candidate. The original graph remains
available for comparison \cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

After a successful return, the wrapper must verify unchanged nodes, edges,
exposure and outcome roles; validate candidate membership and timing;
exclude exposure descendants; and run the independent graphical check.
Success then means a candidate satisfying the specified criterion relative
to the supplied graph. It carries no numerical effect, confidence interval
or certification of the graph's real-world truth. An empty valid set is
a meaningful result, whereas a null or failed return is a different
disposition. A failure to find a set by this method must not become a
claim of global causal nonidentifiability
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

Exceptions, warnings, unsupported variants, altered graph structure and
resource limits are retained with their causes. They do not become an
empty adjustment set or zero effect. The same graph, target identity and
disposition must cross library, CLI and MCP boundaries. Implementation
acceptance will require actual pinned-engine calls on named confounder,
mediator, collider, isolate and permutation fixtures, together with invalid
graph and failure cases. The exact references developed here provide
oracles for that work; no pgmpy runtime or adapter call was performed
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The minimal path's attraction is limited but practical: pgmpy can propose
a graph-based candidate while the project's independent sufficient check
guards its meaning. It is not necessary to copy the whole library or
port its graph stack to another language to obtain this benefit. A
reference-only role remains appropriate for presently unverified discovery
and estimation branches. Minimum-variance adjustment is rejected at this
pin because the branch is unimplemented. Generalized adjustment, latent
graphs and data-driven direction selection are deferred to separate
source and semantic reviews \cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

\subsection{Licensing, dependencies and ownership make reuse concrete}

The pinned MIT license permits use, modification and distribution subject
to preservation of its copyright and permission notice and contains a
warranty disclaimer. That permission does not replace dependency-license
review. Project metadata requires Python from 3.10 up to but excluding
3.15 and declares twelve mandatory dependencies, including NumPy,
SciPy, NetworkX, pandas, scikit-learn, statsmodels, scikit-base and
Hugging Face Hub. Optional groups separately include Torch, graphical
tools and LiteLLM. Declared version floors are not a resolved reproducible
environment \cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

The proposed graph service should use an isolated, locked environment and
only the necessary path. Owners must retain source and dependency identities,
notices, conversion fixtures and resource measurements. An upgrade requires
rechecking API names, graph representations and failure behavior as well as
rerunning accepted fixtures. The inspected revision includes adjustment
tests for observed, latent and M-graph cases, which are useful maintenance
evidence; their presence does not imply that those tests were executed here
or that all targets and graph classes are qualified
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

The LLM pairwise class is also a separate capability within the same
repository. Its description states that it uses variable names and text
descriptions, not the observed data values, and its source includes an
optional provider-call path. Such suggestions can inform a reviewable
business formulation but do not inherit the evidence of a CI-based
discovery algorithm. This selected graph operation has no live-model
dependency. A future language-model path would need its own custody,
ambiguity, source-grounding and held-out evaluation contract
\cite{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

Costs therefore include graph elicitation, controls and timing review,
dependency maintenance, integration fixtures and adjudication of failures,
even though the selected source license has no purchase price. No monetary
maintenance estimate is supplied without workload evidence. Research owners
must justify the assumed graph and target; adapter owners must enforce
the conversion and failure contract; reviewers must verify executed
results before runtime qualification. The concrete revisit trigger is
a new pin, graph class or method path, followed by source inspection and
matched behavioral tests. This division gives pgmpy a useful, limited
place in the proposal while keeping the unimplemented and scientifically
unsupported extensions visible.
\label{exec13:pgmpy-reuse-end}
~~~

~~~text
\section{A restricted pgmpy integration: verify the candidate, preserve the question}
\label{exec13:pgmpy-reuse}

The proposal selects a narrow pgmpy integration for generating an adjustment
candidate from a supplied, fully observed causal DAG. The project must then
verify that candidate independently before reporting it. This choice reuses
a permissively licensed graph library while preserving a scientific boundary:
a successful method call does not prove that the supplied graph describes
the bank, that the requested adjustment identifies the intended effect, or
that a numerical estimator has valid uncertainty. The selection authorizes
development of this limited prototype path; it is not evidence that an
adapter has been implemented or executed
\citep{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary,exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The source is pinned to the repository revision beginning
\texttt{1f6296fc2606}, committed on 5 October 2026 and selected by the
cutoff-bounded history query. Its project metadata declares version 1.1.2.
Fifteen inspected source, guide, test and licensing files match the selected
Git tree, in addition to their local SHA-256 identities. Source acquisition
occurred after the 7 October cutoff. The pin establishes which implementation
is discussed; neither a late repository snapshot nor a recent commit measures
maintainer responsiveness or long-term support
\citep{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

\subsection{A guide, a graph type and an implemented method are separate evidence}

The identification guide presents minimal, variance-optimal and all-set
adjustment routes. The pinned implementation gives a more qualified picture.
Its minimal-variance branch raises \texttt{NotImplementedError}. The minimal
branch explicitly requires a single exposure and outcome, constructs a
proper backdoor graph, then obtains a minimal separator. The all-set branch
enumerates subsets of an ancestor candidate set after removing specified
causal-path and latent variables. These operations cannot be combined into
a claim that every documented graph class and every advertised adjustment
variant is implemented with the same semantics
\citep{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

The distinction between minimal and minimum matters even without statistical
estimation. A set is inclusion-minimal when no proper subset remains valid.
It is minimum-cardinality when no valid set has fewer elements. The inspected
separator routine greedily removes dispensable members of an initial set;
that does not establish a global cardinality minimum. Nor does cardinality
alone determine estimator variance, data acquisition cost or missingness.
A front end should not translate the option name into the promise
``the cheapest or most precise set of controls''
\citep{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_primary}.

An exact graph illustrates the difference. Let $C$ cause $A$, $B$ and $Y$;
let $A$ and $B$ cause $X$; and include $X\to Y$. The two inclusion-minimal
backdoor sets are $\{C\}$ and $\{A,B\}$. Conditioning on $C$ blocks both
forks; without $C$, both $A$ and $B$ are needed. A project reference that
deletes dispensable variables from $\{A,B,C\}$ obtains either answer
depending on deletion order. That computation describes the semantics of
greedy minimality; it is not a measured pgmpy output. For a lending example,
one verified common driver may be cheaper to collect than two separate
proxies, but its actual measurement quality and availability still determine
whether the smaller set is useful \citep{exec13_pgmpy_reuse_oracle}.

The package's all-set path is also a bounded algorithmic choice. Enumerating

domain rather than describe the output as all imaginable observed-variable
sets. Variables irrelevant to the target, descendant restrictions and
generalized adjustment criteria introduce different enumeration questions.
The first proposed wrapper therefore excludes both all-set enumeration and

checks its stated sufficient criterion
~~~

~~~text
\section{Banjo: temporal network search, observation clocks and a bounded reference choice}
\label{sec:exec24-banjo-reuse}

A bank may seek a temporal graph linking servicing actions, balances and later
repayment. The useful question is whether changing an action changes repayment,
not merely whether yesterday's recorded action predicts tomorrow's label.
Banjo supplies a concrete reference for discrete Bayesian-network search with
lag restrictions. The acquired implementation is a gene-network application
derivative, however, and its inferred cellular pseudotime is not a bank's
physical decision clock. Its selected source can inform a proposed worker
contract without establishing an operational banking implementation.
This comparison preserves the earlier Java and temporal surveys and extends
their analysis of identity, preprocessing, search and graph output
\cite{exec13_java_banjo,exec15_javatemporal_banjo,exec24_banjo_source}.

\subsection{Which artifact, which permission, which proposed use?}
The selected repository is \texttt{daniel-315/slingshot-modified-banjo}, pinned
to its 13 August 2026 commit. Twenty-one selected original files authenticate
against the complete pinned tree through Git blob identity and full-byte
SHA-256. They include application settings, the score and observation
implementations, graph management, search, proposal and cycle checks,
discretization, and the graph comparator. Source constants identify Banjo
2.2.0 with the date 15 April 2008; that declaration does not authenticate an
official distribution, describe all derivative changes or establish current
maintenance. The selected compile script targets Java 8 bytecode; the script
has been inspected but not executed. Neither successful native compilation nor
the identity of a distributed binary is claimed \cite{exec24_banjo_source}.

The source notice reserves rights to Duke University and Alexander Hartemink.
It says that noncommercial use may be licensed and directs commercial users
to contact the rights holder. A possible license is not a granted license.
The official-source identity attempt was refused by the fixed collector's
host policy before an HTTP response, so the evidence does not show that the
official site is unavailable. The actual grant, complete terms, derivative
permissions and dependency inventory remain unresolved. A banking product
cannot infer commercial reuse permission from public source visibility
\cite{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

Reference use is therefore the present proposal decision. Direct adoption and
porting are deferred pending authentic rights and a measured benefit.
A separate Java worker is a candidate integration route after rights, artifact
identity and public fixtures are qualified. Rewriting the search is an
alternative only if its scientific and maintenance costs justify a separate
implementation. Rejecting automatic causal interpretations or silent
conversion loss does not require rejecting every predictive comparison.
The source review establishes this bounded choice, not an admitted native
capability \cite{exec24_banjo_choice}.

\subsection{Lag windows, discretization and support must share a clock}
Let $X_{i,t}$ be variable $i$ in an observation file and let $L$ be the maximum
lag. Each scored row pairs current variables with their preceding $L$ rows.
For valid file lengths $T_f>L$, the number of available windows is
\[
 N=\sum_f(T_f-L).
\]
The selected observation implementation constructs windows within each file,
so it avoids a transition from the end of one file to the beginning of the
next. Two files of lengths four and three with $L=1$ supply five windows.
Concatenating them first supplies six and creates one false cross-file
transition. The application setting of 959 observations with lag one yields
958 available windows in one file; the requested count is capped at the
available count. A file boundary is consequently part of the statistical
input, not merely a storage optimization. A proposed bank adapter must retain
entity boundaries, calendars, missing intervals and the meaning of a row
\cite{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

By contrast, the discretization map is fitted to all combined loaded raw
observations before those observations are mapped back to their files.
The selected interval discretizer uses the observed range and a requested
number of bins; when the number of distinct values does not exceed the bin
count, it assigns successive category labels. It is not an equal-frequency
rule. In the authored two-bin witness, training values $0,1,2$ map to
$0,1,1$. Adding a held-out value of $100$ changes their mapping to $0,0,0$.
This is a precise reason to declare the fit split before preprocessing.
It is not evidence that every exploratory use of combined observations is
leakage. A future predictive evaluation requires training-only fit, retained
cutpoints and a declared out-of-range policy. The selected value-lookup path
throws when a value has no stored mapping; an adapter must not assume that
an arbitrary new observation is automatically classified by a frozen
threshold model \cite{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

A second boundary concerns a category present only in the initial lag rows.
The selected cardinality routine scans current slices of usable windows and
takes one plus each variable's largest code. For a coded sequence $(1,0,0)$
with lag one, current slices contain only zero but the first lagged parent is
one. The authored witness compares a current-only cardinality of one with a
lag-inclusive cardinality of two. The score's parent-configuration count uses
these declared cardinalities, while its indexing reads lagged values. This
is a conditional inconsistency requiring an end-to-end fixture, not an
observed Java crash. Earlier validation or a different runtime path could
refuse the input. The product contract should verify every current and lagged
code against its declared domain before search, and preserve a refusal rather
than quietly renumbering the data \cite{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

\subsection{What the score establishes, and what it does not}
For node $i$, let $r_i$ be its number of states, $q_i$ the product of its
parents' state counts, $N_{ijk}$ the count for parent configuration $j$ and
child state $k$, and $N_{ij}=\sum_kN_{ijk}$. With positive equivalent sample
size $\alpha$, the inspected evaluator implements the local log score
\begin{align*}
 s_i={}&\sum_{j=1}^{q_i}\left[\log\Gamma(\alpha/q_i)
                  -\log\Gamma(\alpha/q_i+N_{ij})\right]\\
 &+\sum_{j=1}^{q_i}\sum_{k=1}^{r_i}
       \left[\log\Gamma(\alpha/(q_ir_i)+N_{ijk})
                  -\log\Gamma(\alpha/(q_ir_i))\right].
\end{align*}
Zero-count terms cancel. The source has compact-array and legacy observation
paths, increments integer counts for the selected rows and can precompute
log-gamma values. Its input validation requires a positive equivalent sample
size. These facts identify the selected computation; they do not prove that
successive bank windows are independent or that a chosen discrete model is
correct \cite{exec24_banjo_source}.

The exact authored oracle evaluates the same finite rising-factorial
expression without executing Banjo. For binary parent and child, counts
$(2,0)$ and $(0,2)$ and $\alpha=2$ give a conditional marginal factor $9/64$.
The no-parent counts $(2,2)$ give $1/30$, so their ratio is $135/32$.
Repeating each observation twice changes that ratio to $385875/8192$.
The extra apparent strength is produced by count duplication; it does not
manufacture independent information. The local ratio conditions on the
specified domains, prior and models, with common terms held fixed. It is
not a posterior probability of a causal edge. The oracle also checks the
log-gamma representation numerically against the exact rational results
\cite{exec24_banjo_choice}.

Lag order alone does not resolve this causal distinction. Consider two
authored mechanisms for an action $A_{t-1}$ and outcome $Y_t$. In the first,
a hidden fair binary variable $U_{t-1}$ determines both
$A_{t-1}=U_{t-1}$ and $Y_t=U_{t-1}$. In the second,
$A_{t-1}$ is fair binary and $Y_t=A_{t-1}$. Both observational laws place
probability one half on $(0,0)$ and $(1,1)$, yet their intervention contrasts
are respectively zero and one. Time direction excludes a future-to-past
arrow but does not exclude the hidden common cause. A hypothetical campaign
covering 1,000 accounts, valuing a successful outcome at 50 USD and costing
10,000 USD therefore has net value of either $-10{,}000$ or $40{,}000$ USD
under these two mechanisms. Neither number is a bank forecast. Additional
assignment knowledge, measured confounders, interventions or a justified
restricted causal model is needed to decide between them
\cite{exec24_banjo_choice}.

\subsection{Constraints and retained graphs are part of the result}
The application configuration fixes lag one, mandatory self-lag, at most five
parents, two-bin discretization, greedy search, all local proposals and a
five-minute time budget. Those choices define a constrained search space.
Mandatory self-lag consumes a parent slot and cannot be advertised as a
discovered relationship. The inspected proposal implementation respects
addable and deletable parent sets and the parent limit; reversal proposals
are restricted to contemporaneous edges. Cycle checks exempt deletions and
positive-lag changes, consistently with a time-unrolled graph. Two-way lagged
feedback is consequently distinct from a forbidden contemporaneous directed
cycle. One cycle-check strategy validates before applying a move; another
applies then undoes a rejected cyclic move. A future worker must test rollback
as well as the accepted output \cite{exec24_banjo_source}.

Greedy acceptance permits equal scores, allowing moves across a score
plateau. Search termination can depend on elapsed time, proposed/visited
networks and restart limits. Settings derive per-thread seeds from a base
seed plus a thread index; the base may be supplied or time based. A fixed
seed alone therefore does not specify a run terminated by a machine-dependent
wall-clock limit. Preserve seed, thread configuration, counts, termination
reason and intermediate failures. Compare deterministic work budgets where
possible and record time-budget sensitivity separately
\cite{exec24_banjo_source}.

The requested five best networks are configured as nonidentical. This takes
the equivalence-skip branch, whose predicate returns false; it is distinct
from the source's non-equivalent-network option. Neither option turns the
retained list into a calibrated sample from a graph posterior. Even if four
graphs have equal probability and an edge appears in two of them, retaining
only those two makes its retained-list frequency one rather than its full
probability of one half. A graph rank, score, retained-list frequency and
intervention probability must therefore have different output fields
\cite{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

The selected graph comparator also needs a tied-score fixture. After matching
score and per-node parent counts, it returns zero for identical edge entries
and positive one for distinct entries. For two distinct graphs meeting those
conditions, the authored control-flow witness obtains positive one in both
comparison directions. This violates the antisymmetry expected of an ordered
comparison. It does not establish that a particular recorded Banjo run lost
or duplicated a graph. A qualified worker must test insertion permutations,
exact identity, ties and removal against an independent canonical edge set;
porting this branch without examining the contract would preserve the
problem \cite{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

\subsection{Public qualification before a bank experiment}
The application README describes selection of top-ten and top-fifteen genes
using gold-standard density and analysis under different inferred
trajectories. A reported score on a graph used to select the variables cannot
be treated as an untouched discovery benchmark. The source supports that
description of the workflow; its reported scientific scores and edge signs
have not been reproduced here. Public bank-oriented fixtures should instead
freeze variable selection, temporal ordering, fit splits and scientific
oracles before search. Physical time, inferred pseudotime and a business
event ordering must remain explicit alternatives
\cite{exec24_banjo_source}.

The known route is a small, preregistered fixture family: independent files
and irregular clocks; frozen and leaked discretization; lag-only categories;
exact score tables and duplicated windows; required/forbidden edges and
lag-zero cycles; tied-score graph ordering; cancellation, rollback and failed
requests. A generated latent-confounding pair should require the same
observational fit to produce an identification refusal. A search budget
study can assess stability without renaming it uncertainty calibration.
Only a rights-cleared artifact in an isolated Java environment can supply
native evidence. No downloaded source or compilation script has been run
for this review \cite{exec24_banjo_choice}.

Scientific ownership covers the target, clock, identification and support;
adapter ownership covers domains, windows, graph identity and refusal;
rights/platform ownership covers distribution, dependencies and maintenance;
evaluation ownership covers predeclared oracles, every request and costs.
For illustration only, 9,000 USD over 300 submitted, 225 completed and
150 qualified requests gives respectively 30, 40 and 60 USD per request.
Source review, environment creation, conversions, failures and maintenance
belong in that numerator. Prices and performance remain unmeasured.
Official source identity and terms, executable comparisons, complete capability coverage and independent review remain unresolved
\cite{exec24_banjo_choice}.
~~~

~~~text
\section{Banjo: temporal network search, observation clocks and a bounded reference choice}
\label{sec:exec24-banjo-reuse}

A bank may seek a temporal graph linking servicing actions, balances and later
repayment. The useful question is whether changing an action changes repayment,
not merely whether yesterday's recorded action predicts tomorrow's label.
Banjo supplies a concrete reference for discrete Bayesian-network search with
lag restrictions. The acquired implementation is a gene-network application
derivative, however, and its inferred cellular pseudotime is not a bank's
physical decision clock. Its selected source can inform a proposed worker
contract without establishing an operational banking implementation.
This comparison preserves the earlier Java and temporal surveys and extends
their analysis of identity, preprocessing, search and graph output
\citep{exec13_java_banjo,exec15_javatemporal_banjo,exec24_banjo_source}.

\subsection{Which artifact, which permission, which proposed use?}
The selected repository is \texttt{daniel-315/slingshot-modified-banjo}, pinned
to its 13 August 2026 commit. Twenty-one selected original files authenticate
against the complete pinned tree through Git blob identity and full-byte
SHA-256. They include application settings, the score and observation
implementations, graph management, search, proposal and cycle checks,
discretization, and the graph comparator. Source constants identify Banjo
2.2.0 with the date 15 April 2008; that declaration does not authenticate an
official distribution, describe all derivative changes or establish current
maintenance. The selected compile script targets Java 8 bytecode; the script
has been inspected but not executed. Neither successful native compilation nor
the identity of a distributed binary is claimed \citep{exec24_banjo_source}.

The source notice reserves rights to Duke University and Alexander Hartemink.
It says that noncommercial use may be licensed and directs commercial users
to contact the rights holder. A possible license is not a granted license.
The official-source identity attempt was refused by the fixed collector's
host policy before an HTTP response, so the evidence does not show that the
official site is unavailable. The actual grant, complete terms, derivative
permissions and dependency inventory remain unresolved. A banking product
cannot infer commercial reuse permission from public source visibility
\citep{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

Reference use is therefore the present proposal decision. Direct adoption and
porting are deferred pending authentic rights and a measured benefit.
A separate Java worker is a candidate integration route after rights, artifact
identity and public fixtures are qualified. Rewriting the search is an
alternative only if its scientific and maintenance costs justify a separate
implementation. Rejecting automatic causal interpretations or silent
conversion loss does not require rejecting every predictive comparison.
The source review establishes this bounded choice, not an admitted native
capability \citep{exec24_banjo_choice}.

\subsection{Lag windows, discretization and support must share a clock}
Let $X_{i,t}$ be variable $i$ in an observation file and let $L$ be the maximum
lag. Each scored row pairs current variables with their preceding $L$ rows.
For valid file lengths $T_f>L$, the number of available windows is
\[
 N=\sum_f(T_f-L).
\]
The selected observation implementation constructs windows within each file,
so it avoids a transition from the end of one file to the beginning of the
next. Two files of lengths four and three with $L=1$ supply five windows.
Concatenating them first supplies six and creates one false cross-file
transition. The application setting of 959 observations with lag one yields
958 available windows in one file; the requested count is capped at the
available count. A file boundary is consequently part of the statistical
input, not merely a storage optimization. A proposed bank adapter must retain
entity boundaries, calendars, missing intervals and the meaning of a row
\citep{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

By contrast, the discretization map is fitted to all combined loaded raw
observations before those observations are mapped back to their files.
The selected interval discretizer uses the observed range and a requested
number of bins; when the number of distinct values does not exceed the bin
count, it assigns successive category labels. It is not an equal-frequency
rule. In the authored two-bin witness, training values $0,1,2$ map to
$0,1,1$. Adding a held-out value of $100$ changes their mapping to $0,0,0$.
This is a precise reason to declare the fit split before preprocessing.
It is not evidence that every exploratory use of combined observations is
leakage. A future predictive evaluation requires training-only fit, retained
cutpoints and a declared out-of-range policy. The selected value-lookup path
throws when a value has no stored mapping; an adapter must not assume that
an arbitrary new observation is automatically classified by a frozen
threshold model \citep{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

A second boundary concerns a category present only in the initial lag rows.
The selected cardinality routine scans current slices of usable windows and
takes one plus each variable's largest code. For a coded sequence $(1,0,0)$
with lag one, current slices contain only zero but the first lagged parent is
one. The authored witness compares a current-only cardinality of one with a
lag-inclusive cardinality of two. The score's parent-configuration count uses
these declared cardinalities, while its indexing reads lagged values. This
is a conditional inconsistency requiring an end-to-end fixture, not an
observed Java crash. Earlier validation or a different runtime path could
refuse the input. The product contract should verify every current and lagged
code against its declared domain before search, and preserve a refusal rather
than quietly renumbering the data \citep{exec24_banjo_source,exec24_banjo_choice}.

\subsection{What the score establishes, and what it does not}
For node $i$, let $r_i$ be its number of states, $q_i$ the product of its
parents' state counts, $N_{ijk}$ the count for parent configuration $j$ and
child state $k$, and $N_{ij}=\sum_kN_{ijk}$. With positive equivalent sample
size $\alpha$, the inspected evaluator implements the local log score
\begin{align*}
 s_i={}&\sum_{j=1}^{q_i}\left[\log\Gamma(\alpha/q_i)
                  -\log\Gamma(\alpha/q_i+N_{ij})\right]\\
 &+\sum_{j=1}^{q_i}\sum_{k=1}^{r_i}
       \left[\log\Gamma(\alpha/(q_ir_i)+N_{ijk})
                  -\log\Gamma(\alpha/(q_ir_i))\right].
\end{align*}
Zero-count terms cancel. The source has compact-array and legacy observation
paths, increments integer counts for the selected rows and can precompute
log-gamma values. Its input validation requires a positive equivalent sample
size. These facts identify the selected computation; they do not prove that
successive bank windows are independent or that a chosen discrete model is
correct \citep{exec24_banjo_source}.

The exact authored oracle evaluates the same finite rising-factorial
expression without executing Banjo. For binary parent and child, counts

The no-parent counts $(2,2)$ give $1/30$, so their ratio is $135/32$.

The extra apparent strength is produced by count duplication; it does not
manufacture independent information. The local ratio conditions on the
specified domains, prior and models, with common terms held fixed. It is
not a posterior probability of a causal edge. The oracle also checks the
log-gamma representation numerically against the exact rational results
\citep{exec24_banjo_choice}.

Lag order alone does not resolve this causal distinction. Consider two
authored mechanisms for an action $A_{t-1}$ and outcome $Y_t$. In the first,
a hidden fair binary variable $U_{t-1}$ determines both
$A_{t-1}=U_{t-1}$ and $Y_t=U_{t-1}$. In the second,
$A_{t-1}$ is fair binary and $Y_t=A_{t-1}$. Both observational laws place
probability one half on $(0,0)$ and $(1,1)$, yet their intervention contrasts
are respectively zero and one. Time direction excludes a future-to-past
arrow but does not exclude the hidden common cause. A hypothetical campaign
covering 1,000 accounts, valuing a successful outcome at 50 USD and costing
10,000 USD therefore has net value of either $-10{,}000$ or $40{,}000$ USD
under these two mechanisms. Neither number is a bank forecast. Additional
assignment knowledge, measured confounders, interventions or a justified
restricted causal model is needed to decide between them
\citep{exec24_banjo_choice}.

\subsection{Constraints and retained graphs are part of the result}
The application configuration fixes lag one, mandatory self-lag, at most five
~~~

~~~text
\section{libDAI: exact factor meaning before approximate computation}
\label{sec:exec24-libdai-reuse}

A bank may need probabilities from an already specified causal model before
it compares interventions. A discrete inference engine can compute those
probabilities, but it cannot supply missing causal assumptions merely by
returning normalized beliefs. libDAI is a useful reference for that boundary:
its factor representation, exact enumeration, junction-tree computation and
belief propagation expose different mathematical and operational contracts.
The earlier combined C++ comparison remains intact; this review separates
libDAI's concrete reuse decision from OpenGM and examines the selected
implementation rather than treating a feature list as qualification
\cite{exec13_cxx_inference,exec24_libdai_source}.

\subsection{A historical paper and a later source revision}
Mooij's five-page JMLR paper describes version 0.2.7, released under GPL v2 or
later. Its factor-graph formulation, available inference tasks and interface
scope are primary technical evidence. Its comparison table is historical,
and its performance statements are not measurements of the proposed banking
worker. The genuine publisher PDF is retained and inspected, including its
distinction among marginals, partition functions and maximum-probability
configurations. The paper says that not every method implements every task;
it does not promise a general causal-discovery or identification service
\cite{exec24_libdai_paper}.

The selected source is the \texttt{dbtsai/libDAI} mirror at its 16 May 2012
revision. Its README declares version 0.3.0, dated 12 July 2011, and its
license grants redistribution under two-clause BSD conditions. Those later
terms must not be projected backwards onto the paper's version. Nineteen
selected files authenticate against the complete pinned tree, including
factor and probability primitives, indexing, factor-graph operations, exact
inference, belief propagation, junction-tree construction and build settings.
The mirror is not authenticated as an official current distribution. The
official-source attempt was rejected by the collector's fixed host policy,
which is not evidence of an unavailable server
\cite{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

The Linux template links GMP and GMPXX and names Boost libraries. Its SWIG
example points to Python 2.7 headers. Those are concrete porting questions,
not proof that a modern Python package is unusable or already supported.
The platform-independent configuration enables debug checks, while the
makefile permits another configuration. A qualified artifact must record
the actual build options and complete dependency notices. The inspected BSD
notice is evidence about the selected source, not complete clearance of a
future binary and its bindings. No downloaded build script, source or native
library has been executed for this review \cite{exec24_libdai_source}.

The preceding bridge and discovery examples concern the interpretation of a
graph inferred or supplied by an analyst. Even after a causal model is
accepted for a particular calculation, its probability representation can
change at the next boundary. Factor ordering, normalization and the operation
used to encode an intervention therefore require their own comparison.

\subsection{Factor meaning is a typed conversion contract}
Let the finite variables be $X_V=(X_i:i\in V)$ and let each nonnegative factor
$f_I$ depend on variables $X_{N_I}$. The represented probability law is
\[
 p(x_V)=\frac{\prod_I f_I(x_{N_I})}{Z},\qquad
 Z=\sum_{x_V}\prod_I f_I(x_{N_I}).
\]
This requires finite nonnegative entries and $0<Z<\infty$. A Bayesian network
can be represented using one conditional-probability factor per variable.
That conversion preserves a probability law only if category meaning,
variable identity, dimensions and entry ordering are also preserved
\cite{exec24_libdai_paper,exec24_libdai_source}.

The source orders variables by ascending numeric label and uses the first
variable as the fastest-changing index. With binary $X$ followed by
three-state $Y$, the index is $x+2y$. Assigning weights $(1,2,3,4,5,6)$ in
that order yields $p(X=1)=4/7$. Reading the same vector as a row-major table
with index $3x+y$ instead yields $5/7$. Both outputs normalize and can look
plausible; a probability-simplex check cannot detect this error. One factor
constructor accepts a variable set and copies entries, whereas the ordered
variable-vector constructor explicitly permutes entries. A proposed adapter
must select the correct contract and retain category labels and the
permutation as auditable input \cite{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

The variable comparison operators use labels for identity; matching labels
with different state counts trigger checks only under the selected debug
conditional. The adapter should always reject conflicting domains before
construction, regardless of the native build. The linear-index routine also
defaults unspecified variables to state zero. A missing business observation
must therefore be represented as missing evidence, not passed as an omitted
state that silently becomes the first category. The marginal routine
intersects the requested variable set with the factor's domain; the caller
must verify that every requested variable was actually represented. These
are distinct identity, missingness and query-completeness tests
\cite{exec24_libdai_source}.

\subsection{Clamping evidence does not perform a causal intervention}
The inspected factor-graph clamp multiplies neighboring factors by a
single-state indicator. This conditions the original distribution on an
observation. In a causally justified Bayesian network, an intervention
instead replaces the factor governing the action by the specified assignment
mechanism, while retaining the other mechanisms. The factor graph alone
does not say which table was an action's causal mechanism. That provenance
must survive compilation \cite{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

An exact authored example makes the distinction observable. Let $U$ be fair
binary, let $P(A=U\mid U)=4/5$, and let $Y=U$. Then
$P(Y=1\mid A=1)=4/5$, while
$P(Y=1\mid\operatorname{do}(A=1))=1/2$ and the intervention contrast is zero.
The conditioning calculation retains $p(A\mid U)$; the intervention
calculation replaces it. This example supplies the causal model explicitly.
It does not infer that model from the observed distribution
\cite{exec24_libdai_choice}.

Nor is making a cavity a generic intervention implementation: the selected
method replaces every factor touching a variable with ones, which can erase
outcome mechanisms as well as the assignment mechanism. Backup and restore
operations can retain earlier factors, but the worker must verify recovery
under failed or cancelled requests. In the selected clamp, the initial
guard permits an index equal to the number of states, whereas valid indices
are strictly smaller. The subsequent setter has a debug-only bounds check.
This is a conditional source boundary requiring a native fixture, not an
observed memory fault. The proposed API rejects every index outside
$0\leq x<r_i$ before calling the library
\cite{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

\subsection{Exact inference has numerical and resource conditions}
The selected exact enumerator multiplies factors into a full joint table,
computes its sum and normalizes marginals. Its algorithmic exactness is
conditional on the supplied finite model; it does not imply exact arithmetic.
For two singleton factors with entries $(10^{-200},2\cdot10^{-200})$, the
mathematically normalized product is $(1/5,4/5)$, but both binary64 products
underflow to zero in the independent witness. A log-scale calculation with
maximum subtraction recovers the intended normalized ratio. An exactly zero
normalizer instead denotes an impossible or invalid query and must be
refused. Scaling factors and tracking their scales can preserve normalized
beliefs, but the scales must also be restored when reporting $\log Z$.
The inspected probability primitive explicitly refuses a zero norm;
finite, nonnegative inputs and finite outputs require additional checks
\cite{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

Resource use is another part of the result. A full joint table on thirty
binary variables has $2^{30}$ entries: eight-byte entries alone require
8 GiB, before containers and intermediate products. Junction-tree inference
can exploit structure, but its clique tables may still be large. The
selected constructor chooses an elimination heuristic and applies a memory
estimate with a documented heuristic multiplier. An estimate is not an
operating-system resource limit. A proposed worker needs its own memory,
time, cancellation and output limits, and must report when a requested
exact calculation was refused or replaced by an explicitly approved
approximation \cite{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

\subsection{A zero convergence residual can coexist with a wrong marginal}
Belief propagation iterates messages, with configured update order, damping,
log-domain arithmetic, tolerance and iteration/time bounds. The selected
implementation returns the largest change in beliefs; a nonconvergence
warning is conditional on verbosity. It explicitly omits NaN checks in the
iteration loop. A worker must inspect residuals, iteration counts, finite
values and termination conditions independently of textual logs. Even a
finite residual below tolerance does not bound marginal error
\cite{exec24_libdai_source}.

Consider an authored triangle of binary variables with edge factor three
when neighboring states agree and one when they disagree. Uniform messages
remain uniform after a belief-propagation update, giving zero residual.
The resulting edge belief assigns agreement probability $3/4$. Exact
enumeration gives two all-equal states of weight 27 each and six other
states of weight three each, hence $Z=72$. For a fixed edge, the agreeing
weight is 60, so its true agreement probability is $5/6$. The error is
$1/12$ despite the fixed point. Single-variable marginals are one half in
both calculations, so inspecting only those marginals would miss the
discrepancy \cite{exec24_libdai_choice}.

This is a mathematical witness for the proposed diagnostic, not a native
libDAI benchmark. It distinguishes iterative stability, approximation error
and statistical uncertainty. Under a hypothetical decision coefficient of
120,000 USD, an error of $1/12$ translates into 10,000 USD of value error.
A small residual cannot justify that monetary exposure. Public qualification
should compare exact and approximate results on declared finite regimes,
measure query-specific errors and refuse unsupported accuracy claims.
Sampling uncertainty and uncertainty about the causal model remain separate
\cite{exec24_libdai_choice}.

\subsection{A reference choice with a concrete integration route}
The current decision is to retain libDAI as a historical numerical reference
and conditional candidate for an isolated C++ inference worker. Direct
adoption of a general causal platform is rejected because the selected
surface does not supply identification. A port is deferred until a measured
need justifies a new correctness and maintenance burden. Reuse through a
rights-reviewed worker can be preferable to porting when exact artifact
identity, factor semantics and error contracts are demonstrably preserved.
Another current inference implementation is an alternative comparator,
but it must face the same typed queries and public oracles; this review does
not confer approval on an uninspected substitute \cite{exec24_libdai_choice}.

The known route is to preregister finite fixtures for variable permutations,
conflicting cardinalities, unspecified states and absent query variables;
conditioning versus intervention and restoration after faults; zero mass,
nonfinite entries and underflow; exact enumeration versus junction trees;
tree and loopy-message fixed points, truncation and resource refusal.
The numerical owner supplies exact oracles and error budgets; the causal
owner supplies mechanism provenance and identifying assumptions; the
adapter owner supplies types and refusal behavior; the platform/rights owner
supplies artifact and dependency qualification. Every submitted request,
including conversion and runtime failures, remains in the cost denominator.
A purely illustrative 12,000 USD over 400 submitted, 300 completed and
200 qualified requests produces 30, 40 and 60 USD respectively.
Official provenance, applicable rights, execution, independent review, current support and actual costs remain unresolved \cite{exec24_libdai_choice}.
~~~
