~~~text
\section{libDAI: exact factor meaning before approximate computation}
\label{sec:exec24-libdai-reuse}

A bank may need probabilities from an already specified causal model before
it compares interventions. A discrete inference engine can compute those
probabilities, but it cannot supply missing causal assumptions merely by
returning normalized beliefs. libDAI is a useful reference for that boundary:
its factor representation, exact enumeration, junction-tree computation and
belief propagation expose different mathematical and operational contracts.
The earlier combined C++ comparison remains intact; this review separates
libDAI's concrete reuse decision from OpenGM and examines the selected
implementation rather than treating a feature list as qualification
\citep{exec13_cxx_inference,exec24_libdai_source}.

\subsection{A historical paper and a later source revision}
Mooij's five-page JMLR paper describes version 0.2.7, released under GPL v2 or
later. Its factor-graph formulation, available inference tasks and interface
scope are primary technical evidence. Its comparison table is historical,
and its performance statements are not measurements of the proposed banking
worker. The genuine publisher PDF is retained and inspected, including its
distinction among marginals, partition functions and maximum-probability
configurations. The paper says that not every method implements every task;
it does not promise a general causal-discovery or identification service
\citep{exec24_libdai_paper}.

The selected source is the \texttt{dbtsai/libDAI} mirror at its 16 May 2012
revision. Its README declares version 0.3.0, dated 12 July 2011, and its
license grants redistribution under two-clause BSD conditions. Those later
terms must not be projected backwards onto the paper's version. Nineteen
selected files authenticate against the complete pinned tree, including
factor and probability primitives, indexing, factor-graph operations, exact
inference, belief propagation, junction-tree construction and build settings.
The mirror is not authenticated as an official current distribution. The
official-source attempt was rejected by the collector's fixed host policy,
which is not evidence of an unavailable server
\citep{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

The Linux template links GMP and GMPXX and names Boost libraries. Its SWIG
example points to Python 2.7 headers. Those are concrete porting questions,
not proof that a modern Python package is unusable or already supported.
The platform-independent configuration enables debug checks, while the
makefile permits another configuration. A qualified artifact must record
the actual build options and complete dependency notices. The inspected BSD
notice is evidence about the selected source, not complete clearance of a
future binary and its bindings. No downloaded build script, source or native
library has been executed for this review \citep{exec24_libdai_source}.

The preceding bridge and discovery examples concern the interpretation of a
graph inferred or supplied by an analyst. Even after a causal model is
accepted for a particular calculation, its probability representation can
change at the next boundary. Factor ordering, normalization and the operation
used to encode an intervention therefore require their own comparison.

\subsection{Factor meaning is a typed conversion contract}
Let the finite variables be $X_V=(X_i:i\in V)$ and let each nonnegative factor
$f_I$ depend on variables $X_{N_I}$. The represented probability law is
\[
 p(x_V)=\frac{\prod_I f_I(x_{N_I})}{Z},\qquad
 Z=\sum_{x_V}\prod_I f_I(x_{N_I}).
\]
This requires finite nonnegative entries and $0<Z<\infty$. A Bayesian network
can be represented using one conditional-probability factor per variable.
That conversion preserves a probability law only if category meaning,
variable identity, dimensions and entry ordering are also preserved
\citep{exec24_libdai_paper,exec24_libdai_source}.

The source orders variables by ascending numeric label and uses the first
variable as the fastest-changing index. With binary $X$ followed by
three-state $Y$, the index is $x+2y$. Assigning weights $(1,2,3,4,5,6)$ in
that order yields $p(X=1)=4/7$. Reading the same vector as a row-major table
with index $3x+y$ instead yields $5/7$. Both outputs normalize and can look
plausible; a probability-simplex check cannot detect this error. One factor
constructor accepts a variable set and copies entries, whereas the ordered
variable-vector constructor explicitly permutes entries. A proposed adapter
must select the correct contract and retain category labels and the
permutation as auditable input \citep{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

The variable comparison operators use labels for identity; matching labels
with different state counts trigger checks only under the selected debug
conditional. The adapter should always reject conflicting domains before
construction, regardless of the native build. The linear-index routine also
defaults unspecified variables to state zero. A missing business observation
must therefore be represented as missing evidence, not passed as an omitted
state that silently becomes the first category. The marginal routine
intersects the requested variable set with the factor's domain; the caller
must verify that every requested variable was actually represented. These
are distinct identity, missingness and query-completeness tests
\citep{exec24_libdai_source}.

\subsection{Clamping evidence does not perform a causal intervention}
The inspected factor-graph clamp multiplies neighboring factors by a
single-state indicator. This conditions the original distribution on an
observation. In a causally justified Bayesian network, an intervention
instead replaces the factor governing the action by the specified assignment
mechanism, while retaining the other mechanisms. The factor graph alone
does not say which table was an action's causal mechanism. That provenance
must survive compilation \citep{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

An exact authored example makes the distinction observable. Let $U$ be fair
binary, let $P(A=U\mid U)=4/5$, and let $Y=U$. Then
$P(Y=1\mid A=1)=4/5$, while
$P(Y=1\mid\operatorname{do}(A=1))=1/2$ and the intervention contrast is zero.
The conditioning calculation retains $p(A\mid U)$; the intervention
calculation replaces it. This example supplies the causal model explicitly.
It does not infer that model from the observed distribution
\citep{exec24_libdai_choice}.

Nor is making a cavity a generic intervention implementation: the selected
method replaces every factor touching a variable with ones, which can erase
outcome mechanisms as well as the assignment mechanism. Backup and restore
operations can retain earlier factors, but the worker must verify recovery
under failed or cancelled requests. In the selected clamp, the initial
guard permits an index equal to the number of states, whereas valid indices
are strictly smaller. The subsequent setter has a debug-only bounds check.
This is a conditional source boundary requiring a native fixture, not an
observed memory fault. The proposed API rejects every index outside
$0\leq x<r_i$ before calling the library
\citep{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

\subsection{Exact inference has numerical and resource conditions}
The selected exact enumerator multiplies factors into a full joint table,
computes its sum and normalizes marginals. Its algorithmic exactness is
conditional on the supplied finite model; it does not imply exact arithmetic.
For two singleton factors with entries $(10^{-200},2\cdot10^{-200})$, the
mathematically normalized product is $(1/5,4/5)$, but both binary64 products
underflow to zero in the independent witness. A log-scale calculation with
maximum subtraction recovers the intended normalized ratio. An exactly zero
normalizer instead denotes an impossible or invalid query and must be
refused. Scaling factors and tracking their scales can preserve normalized
beliefs, but the scales must also be restored when reporting $\log Z$.
The inspected probability primitive explicitly refuses a zero norm;
finite, nonnegative inputs and finite outputs require additional checks
\citep{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

Resource use is another part of the result. A full joint table on thirty
binary variables has $2^{30}$ entries: eight-byte entries alone require
8 GiB, before containers and intermediate products. Junction-tree inference
can exploit structure, but its clique tables may still be large. The
selected constructor chooses an elimination heuristic and applies a memory
estimate with a documented heuristic multiplier. An estimate is not an
operating-system resource limit. A proposed worker needs its own memory,
time, cancellation and output limits, and must report when a requested
exact calculation was refused or replaced by an explicitly approved
approximation \citep{exec24_libdai_source,exec24_libdai_choice}.

\subsection{A zero convergence residual can coexist with a wrong marginal}
Belief propagation iterates messages, with configured update order, damping,
log-domain arithmetic, tolerance and iteration/time bounds. The selected
implementation returns the largest change in beliefs; a nonconvergence
warning is conditional on verbosity. It explicitly omits NaN checks in the
iteration loop. A worker must inspect residuals, iteration counts, finite
values and termination conditions independently of textual logs. Even a
finite residual below tolerance does not bound marginal error
\citep{exec24_libdai_source}.

Consider an authored triangle of binary variables with edge factor three
when neighboring states agree and one when they disagree. Uniform messages
remain uniform after a belief-propagation update, giving zero residual.
The resulting edge belief assigns agreement probability $3/4$. Exact
enumeration gives two all-equal states of weight 27 each and six other
states of weight three each, hence $Z=72$. For a fixed edge, the agreeing
weight is 60, so its true agreement probability is $5/6$. The error is
$1/12$ despite the fixed point. Single-variable marginals are one half in
both calculations, so inspecting only those marginals would miss the
discrepancy \citep{exec24_libdai_choice}.

This is a mathematical witness for the proposed diagnostic, not a native
libDAI benchmark. It distinguishes iterative stability, approximation error
and statistical uncertainty. Under a hypothetical decision coefficient of
120,000 USD, an error of $1/12$ translates into 10,000 USD of value error.
A small residual cannot justify that monetary exposure. Public qualification
should compare exact and approximate results on declared finite regimes,
measure query-specific errors and refuse unsupported accuracy claims.
Sampling uncertainty and uncertainty about the causal model remain separate
\citep{exec24_libdai_choice}.

\subsection{A reference choice with a concrete integration route}
The current decision is to retain libDAI as a historical numerical reference
and conditional candidate for an isolated C++ inference worker. Direct
adoption of a general causal platform is rejected because the selected
surface does not supply identification. A port is deferred until a measured
need justifies a new correctness and maintenance burden. Reuse through a
rights-reviewed worker can be preferable to porting when exact artifact
identity, factor semantics and error contracts are demonstrably preserved.
Another current inference implementation is an alternative comparator,
but it must face the same typed queries and public oracles; this review does
not confer approval on an uninspected substitute \citep{exec24_libdai_choice}.

The known route is to preregister finite fixtures for variable permutations,
conflicting cardinalities, unspecified states and absent query variables;
conditioning versus intervention and restoration after faults; zero mass,
nonfinite entries and underflow; exact enumeration versus junction trees;
tree and loopy-message fixed points, truncation and resource refusal.
The numerical owner supplies exact oracles and error budgets; the causal
owner supplies mechanism provenance and identifying assumptions; the
adapter owner supplies types and refusal behavior; the platform/rights owner
supplies artifact and dependency qualification. Every submitted request,
including conversion and runtime failures, remains in the cost denominator.
A purely illustrative 12,000 USD over 400 submitted, 300 completed and
200 qualified requests produces 30, 40 and 60 USD respectively.
Official provenance, applicable rights, execution, independent review, current support and actual costs remain unresolved \citep{exec24_libdai_choice}.
~~~

Before:
~~~text
\section{aGrUM and pyAgrum: a formula is not yet a supported causal answer}
\label{sec:exec24-agrum-reuse}

A bank may obtain a symbolic intervention formula and still lack the support
needed to evaluate it for its target customers. It may also obtain a
counterfactual-looking distribution without preserving the dependence induced
by the factual information. aGrUM and its Python wrapper pyAgrum are important
integration candidates because their selected source spans graphical
learning, causal models, identification and numerical tensors. That breadth
makes it especially important to qualify each layer separately. This
comparison preserves the earlier discovery, latent, temporal and native
identification reviews, and resolves their remaining numerical-support
question at a bounded set of source paths
\cite{exec13_cxx_agrum,exec14_native_agrum,exec16_ct_agrum,
exec19_cxx_identification_source,exec24_agrum_source}.

\subsection{The selected source and the unit of adoption}
The inspected revision is the 6 October 2026 commit of
\texttt{agrumery/aGrUM}, with declared version 3.2.0. Nineteen selected
originals are rebound to the complete pinned tree and full-byte hashes,
including tensor arithmetic, operator dispatch, causal formula evaluation,
counterfactual reconstruction, Python convenience methods and license/build
declarations. Earlier source inspections remain evidence for their own
bounded claims. A repeated source binding does not turn an earlier
inconclusive capability comparison into a native qualification
\cite{exec24_agrum_source,exec24_agrum_choice}.

The root and selected headers declare LGPL-3.0-or-later or MIT. The root
notice also points to third-party code and its separate terms. The inspected
MIT template and root declaration must be read alongside file-specific
copyright notices; a template alone is not a complete binary inventory.
CMake 3.18 and C++20 are declared build requirements, and pyAgrum uses SWIG.
The package README's placeholder badges do not establish a release or test
result. The source identity is pinned independently of those badges.
Complete artifact, dependencies, binding behavior and maintenance remain
qualification work \cite{exec24_agrum_source}.

The current choice is a reference decision with a conditional integration
route through an isolated C++ or Python worker. Direct adoption of all
learning, identification and counterfactual claims is deferred. Porting the
identification and tensor machinery is also deferred until a measured need
justifies independent theorem, implementation and maintenance work.
Rejecting unsupported numerical or counterfactual claims does not require
rejecting the package's entire set of potential uses. Each proposed worker
must declare which graph class, query, data regime and source paths it
actually supports \cite{exec24_agrum_choice}.

\subsection{Graph identification and numeric support are different predicates}
The selected causal formula tests identification by the presence of an
abstract-syntax-tree root. Its evaluation method refuses a missing root and
otherwise evaluates that tree against the observational Bayesian network.
These are useful distinctions, but a present root is not a certificate of
positive observational support for every conditional term. The source also
checks that queried variables are observed and that outcome, intervention
and conditioning sets do not overlap. A caller must preserve those
preconditions rather than reducing every failure to an empty numeric answer
\cite{exec24_agrum_source}.

The selected division node evaluates its children and invokes tensor
division. For dense arrays, the inspected dispatch reaches ordinary
elementwise division; generic dispatch similarly invokes the registered
operator. No support-zero refusal is introduced along those selected
numeric branches. Scalar and empty-tensor branches also divide directly.
This is a bounded source finding: it does not establish that every
high-level query reaches a zero denominator, or that every scalar type and
runtime reports the same result. It defines concrete zero-over-zero,
positive-over-zero and nonfinite fixtures for native evaluation
\cite{exec24_agrum_source,exec24_agrum_choice}.

Ordinary tensor normalization leaves a zero-sum tensor unchanged. By
contrast, conditional-probability-table normalization throws for a zero-sum
conditioning row. These methods therefore have different contracts.
A returned all-zero vector is not a normalized distribution and cannot be
read as zero risk. A proposed wrapper must check finite nonnegative entries,
required support, dimensional completeness and the appropriate normalizer
for its query. It should return a typed refusal or an explicitly justified
identified set; adding a small denominator is a modeling change, not an
innocuous formatting repair \cite{exec24_agrum_source,exec24_agrum_choice}.

For an exact authored example, let a binary covariate $Z$ have probability
one half in each stratum. Suppose action $A=1$ is observed only when $Z=0$,
where the mean outcome under that action is one half. Under the specified
adjustment model, the supported contribution to the population mean is
$1/2\cdot1/2=1/4$. The unobserved mean for $Z=1$ can be any $m\in[0,1]$,
so the compatible intervention means form
\[
 \mu(1)=\frac14+\frac12m\in\left[\frac14,\frac34\right].
\]
The formula's unsupported conditional term corresponds to a zero-over-zero
ratio in the observed distribution. Replacing it by zero selects the lower
endpoint; replacing it by one half selects the midpoint. Neither operation
identifies the missing mean. Publicly generated data can test whether the
worker preserves this refusal or set, but recovering the actual missing
bank response requires additional information or a justified restriction
\cite{exec24_agrum_choice}.

\subsection{Counterfactual abduction must preserve a joint posterior}
The selected counterfactual builder starts from the observational Bayesian
network, identifies parentless variables excluding intervention and declared
latent variables, computes their individual posteriors given the profile,
and fills their separate root tables with those marginals. This preserves
each updated root marginal. It need not preserve dependence induced between
the roots by conditioning on their common descendant. The earlier
identification review already flags this boundary; the present decision
retains it as a prerequisite for a native, query-specific comparison
\cite{exec19_cxx_identification_source,exec24_agrum_source}.

Consider independent fair binary roots $U,V$, an action $A$, a factual
observation $E=\mathbf1\{U=V\}$, and deterministic potential outcome
$W(a)=a\mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}U\mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}V$.
After observing $E=1$, the joint posterior places one half on $(0,0)$ and
one half on $(1,1)$. Both individual root marginals are still fair binary.
Under $\operatorname{do}(A=1)$, preserving the joint posterior gives
$P(W(1)=1\mid E=1)=1$. Replacing it by the product of its marginal
posteriors assigns probability one quarter to all four root pairs and gives
one half instead. This is an exact finite structural example, not a claim
that a particular installed aGrUM build returned the latter value
\cite{exec24_agrum_choice}.

The difference has a concrete decision interpretation. For a hypothetical
1,000-account cohort, 80 USD per successful outcome and a 30,000 USD
campaign cost, the two calculations give 50,000 and 10,000 USD net value.
The 40,000 USD discrepancy comes from discarded posterior coupling, not
from Monte Carlo error. A valid restricted use may prove that the factual
profile leaves relevant roots independent, or may preserve a joint
abduction distribution in another representation. A general counterfactual
claim instead requires structural mechanisms and cross-world coupling that
an observational Bayesian network does not supply automatically
\cite{exec24_agrum_choice}.

Graph identity also matters during reconstruction. The constructor from an
observational network differs from the copy constructor of the full causal
model. The selected counterfactual path uses the former, so added latent
descriptors and causal annotations need an explicit preservation test.
The output adapter maps variables back to the original network by name and
fills the resulting tensor. Domain labels, order and retained annotations
must be checked together; equal displayed names alone are insufficient
\cite{exec24_agrum_source}.

\subsection{Tensor identity and language wrappers are scientific interfaces}
The inspected dense and generic binary-operation templates compare shared
variables through stored variable pointers. A native object identity may
therefore determine whether two axes are shared, independently of a user's
displayed name. Higher-level construction may impose further checks; this
review does not claim that every Python object with a repeated name silently
becomes a new axis. It requires a cross-language fixture with shared objects,
copied objects, changed domains and reordered categories
\cite{exec24_agrum_source}.

An authored probability witness explains why that fixture matters. Two
factors on the same binary variable, $(1/4,3/4)$ and $(4/5,1/5)$, multiply
and normalize to $(4/7,3/7)$. If an adapter instead treats them as distinct
variables, it produces an outer-product joint distribution whose
probability that the two states disagree is $13/20$. That is a different
model, even though both outputs are normalized. Variable identity, not only
numeric values, belongs in the round-trip contract
\cite{exec24_agrum_choice}.

The selected Python convenience method standardizes string queries into
sets, invokes its underlying causal routine, keeps a model reference on the
returned tensor and returns \texttt{None} for an unidentified formula.
The inspected SWIG extension also documents special constructor and lifetime
handling. These source observations do not settle every exception,
temporary-object, serialization or cancellation path. A CLI or MCP response
should distinguish an unidentified query, unsupported numeric support,
invalid domain, runtime failure and successful qualified value. Serializing
each as a generic null would erase information needed for a safe business
decision \cite{exec24_agrum_source,exec24_agrum_choice}.

\subsection{A public qualification route with explicit research limits}
The earlier PC/FCI, temporal and identification inspections supply bounded
source comparisons. They do not establish complete discovery under hidden
confounding, every ID/IDC recursion, or arbitrary counterfactual coupling.
The proposed program retains their concrete conformance questions rather
than declaring them defects from comments or method names alone. Native
qualification should separate discovery endpoint semantics, identified
formulas, numeric support and counterfactual laws into different test groups
\cite{exec13_cxx_agrum,exec14_native_agrum,exec16_ct_agrum,
exec19_cxx_identification_source,exec24_agrum_choice}.

Known next steps are finite generated graphs with manual formula oracles;
zero-support and near-zero-support tables; ordinary versus CPT normalization;
joint-root counterfactual examples with and without induced dependence;
latent-annotation round trips; tensor pointer/name/domain permutations;
and Python/CLI/MCP failure, lifetime and recovery cases. Those experiments
can justify a prototype before authentic bank experiments. No downloaded
code has been run in this review. A successful public fixture would qualify
its declared case, not unrestricted causal inference
\cite{exec24_agrum_choice}.

Scientific ownership covers graph assumptions, support, identification and
coupling. Numerical ownership covers factor domains and invalid mass;
adapter ownership covers types, lifetimes and typed refusals; platform and
rights ownership covers complete binary provenance and dependencies.
An illustrative 15,000 USD over 500 submitted, 375 completed and 250
qualified requests gives 30, 40 and 60 USD respectively. Actual prices and
decision value remain unmeasured. Hidden bank assignment, unsupported
stratum responses and arbitrary cross-world dependence cannot be recovered
by a general package-integration step. Execution of the complete package, independent validation and evaluation in a bank remain outstanding \cite{exec24_agrum_choice}.
~~~

~~~text
becomes a new axis. It requires a cross-language fixture with shared objects,
copied objects, changed domains and reordered categories
\citep{exec24_agrum_source}.

An authored probability witness explains why that fixture matters. Two
factors on the same binary variable, $(1/4,3/4)$ and $(4/5,1/5)$, multiply
and normalize to $(4/7,3/7)$. If an adapter instead treats them as distinct
variables, it produces an outer-product joint distribution whose
probability that the two states disagree is $13/20$. That is a different
model, even though both outputs are normalized. Variable identity, not only
numeric values, belongs in the round-trip contract
\citep{exec24_agrum_choice}.

The selected Python convenience method standardizes string queries into
sets, invokes its underlying causal routine, keeps a model reference on the
returned tensor and returns \texttt{None} for an unidentified formula.
The inspected SWIG extension also documents special constructor and lifetime
handling. These source observations do not settle every exception,
temporary-object, serialization or cancellation path. A CLI or MCP response
should distinguish an unidentified query, unsupported numeric support,
invalid domain, runtime failure and successful qualified value. Serializing
each as a generic null would erase information needed for a safe business
decision \citep{exec24_agrum_source,exec24_agrum_choice}.

\subsection{A public qualification route with explicit research limits}
The earlier PC/FCI, temporal and identification inspections supply bounded
source comparisons. They do not establish complete discovery under hidden
confounding, every ID/IDC recursion, or arbitrary counterfactual coupling.
The proposed program retains their concrete conformance questions rather
than declaring them defects from comments or method names alone. Native
qualification should separate discovery endpoint semantics, identified
formulas, numeric support and counterfactual laws into different test groups
\citep{exec13_cxx_agrum,exec14_native_agrum,exec16_ct_agrum,
exec19_cxx_identification_source,exec24_agrum_choice}.

Known next steps are finite generated graphs with manual formula oracles;
zero-support and near-zero-support tables; ordinary versus CPT normalization;
joint-root counterfactual examples with and without induced dependence;
latent-annotation round trips; tensor pointer/name/domain permutations;
and Python/CLI/MCP failure, lifetime and recovery cases. Those experiments
can justify a prototype before authentic bank experiments. No downloaded
code has been run in this review. A successful public fixture would qualify
its declared case, not unrestricted causal inference
\citep{exec24_agrum_choice}.

Scientific ownership covers graph assumptions, support, identification and
coupling. Numerical ownership covers factor domains and invalid mass;
adapter ownership covers types, lifetimes and typed refusals; platform and
rights ownership covers complete binary provenance and dependencies.
An illustrative 15,000 USD over 500 submitted, 375 completed and 250
qualified requests gives 30, 40 and 60 USD respectively. Actual prices and
decision value remain unmeasured. Hidden bank assignment, unsupported
stratum responses and arbitrary cross-world dependence cannot be recovered
by a general package-integration step. Execution of the complete package, independent validation and evaluation in a bank remain outstanding \citep{exec24_agrum_choice}.
~~~

~~~text
\section{OpenGM: preserving the question across optimization and inference}
\label{sec:exec24-opengm-reuse}

A bank allocating a servicing budget may need the least costly joint action,
the probability that a particular account responds, or the effect of changing
the assignment policy. These are different questions even when their data
can be represented by the same factor graph. OpenGM is a useful historical
reference for separating model representation, algebra and algorithms. Its
generality makes the adapter's declaration of the requested operation more
important, not less. A most probable joint configuration is not a vector of
marginal probabilities, and neither is automatically a causal intervention
\cite{exec24_opengm_paper,exec24_opengm_source,exec24_opengm_choice}.

\subsection{A historical original with a conditional integration route}
The selected repository is \texttt{opengm/opengm}, pinned to its 26 July
2019 revision. Its README explicitly labels the project unmaintained, while
the inspected CMake file declares version 2.3.1. The README links manuals
for other versions; those labels must not be silently treated as the
selected artifact. This is a bounded historical-source comparison, not a
claim that every later fork or commercial support arrangement has been
surveyed. The five-page 2012 library paper is a genuine retained original.
Its design argument is inspected directly, while its historical performance
comparisons are not imported as current measurements
\cite{exec24_opengm_paper,exec24_opengm_source}.

The root license grants MIT-style permissions with notice retention. That
does not determine the complete rights of a binary containing optional
solvers. CMake exposes separate switches for, among others, CPLEX, Gurobi,
libDAI, graph-cut libraries, HDF5 and Python bindings. The inspected defaults
disable the command-line build and Python wrapper. A list of supported
interfaces therefore does not establish what an installed artifact contains.
The selected source's build requirements and declarations are evidence for
a proposed artifact inventory; no downloaded code or build script has been
executed \cite{exec24_opengm_source}.

The proposal retains OpenGM as a reference and leaves a narrowly specified
isolated worker as a conditional integration option. Direct adoption of the
whole solver catalogue is deferred because its algorithms have different
domains, rights and result contracts. Porting is deferred until a measured
need justifies maintaining model encodings, numeric behavior and solver
interfaces. The unmaintained declaration increases the importance of an
identified maintenance owner and a supported build, but is not by itself a
proof that every bounded use is unsuitable \cite{exec24_opengm_choice}.

\subsection{The algebra is part of the business query}
Let $V$ be finite variables, $F$ factors, $x$ a joint labeling, and
$\phi_f(x_{N(f)})$ a factor on its ordered neighborhood. OpenGM separates
the operation that combines factors from the operation that accumulates
over labelings. For a probability model, an unnormalized weight is
\[
 w(x)=\prod_{f\in F}\phi_f(x_{N(f)}),\qquad
 Z=\sum_x w(x),\qquad p(x)=w(x)/Z,
\]
provided the factors give finite nonnegative weights and $0<Z<\infty$.
For an energy model, $E(x)=\sum_f e_f(x_{N(f)})$ may instead be minimized.
The library paper formalizes these as different algebraic choices. It also
explicitly warns that not every algorithm supports every choice. Selected
source implements separate addition, multiplication, minimization and
maximization operations. A type name or successful generic serialization
cannot replace the algorithm-specific compatibility check
\cite{exec24_opengm_paper,exec24_opengm_source}.

An exact authored example makes the distinction operational. Suppose two
binary labels have joint probabilities
\[
 p(0,0)=0.35,\quad p(0,1)=0.25,\quad p(1,0)=0,\quad p(1,1)=0.40.
\]
The most probable joint labeling is $(1,1)$, but $P(X=0)=0.60$. Under
unit loss for an incorrect prediction of $X$, choosing its marginal mode
has expected loss $0.40$, whereas taking the $X$ component of the joint
mode has loss $0.60$. For 10,000 hypothetical cases at 100 USD per error,
that substitution adds 200,000 USD expected loss. The solver could have
correctly solved its stated joint optimization problem while the business
adapter answered the wrong question \cite{exec24_opengm_choice}.

Even correct probability inference does not identify an intervention.
Let an unobserved fair binary $U$ determine $Y=U$, and let the assigned
action equal $U$ with probability $4/5$. Then $P(Y=1\mid A=1)=4/5$, while
both intervention means are $1/2$ and the effect of changing $A$ is zero.
Conditioning a factor graph on $A=1$ preserves the selection association;
it does not remove the assignment mechanism. A causal worker needs an
identified model and a declared mechanism transformation before numerical
inference is meaningful \cite{exec24_opengm_choice}.

\subsection{A normal return does not certify exhaustive search}
The selected brute-force routine enumerates configurations with variable
zero changing fastest. It updates an incumbent through the chosen ordered
accumulator, invokes a visitor, and stops if the visitor requests it. Both
visitor stopping and exhaustion reach a final \texttt{NORMAL} return.
The separate visitor protocol distinguishes, for example, a time-limit stop
from a bound-based stop, but that distinction is not preserved in this
routine's return code. Its \texttt{arg} method supports the first stored
labeling and returns \texttt{UNKNOWN} for a requested higher rank. These
are concrete source contracts, not a report from an installed worker
\cite{exec24_opengm_source}.

For a two-variable authored energy table, let the enumeration order
$(0,0),(1,0),(0,1),(1,1)$ have energies $5,4,3,0$. Stopping after two
visits produces incumbent $4$; complete enumeration produces $0$.
The four-unit difference is known because this tiny example has an exact
oracle. In a larger case, \texttt{NORMAL} alone does not supply either an
exhaustion certificate or a finite optimality gap. The proposed response
must therefore include the visited/requested state counts where meaningful,
visitor stop reason, incumbent, independently valid bound and algorithm
configuration \cite{exec24_opengm_choice}.

The inherited base inference interface further returns an inverse-neutral
value for its default bound when the accumulator has an ordering, and a
not-a-number value when it does not. For floating-point minimization,
the selected inverse neutral is negative infinity. The brute-force class
does not override that bound in the inspected header. Exhaustion may justify
an exact result under valid arithmetic, but merely reading its default bound
does not yield a useful finite certificate. Unsupported marginals return
\texttt{UNKNOWN} in the base interface. A uniform response schema must
preserve those distinctions instead of manufacturing a probability or gap
\cite{exec24_opengm_source}.

\subsection{Hard constraints and shared factors require numerical contracts}
A second boundary appears in the concrete move-making delegate. For a
multiplicative model it recomputes the destination objective. For other
operators it updates the previous value by combining a new factor value
and removing the old one. With finite exact arithmetic this is a useful
incremental calculation. With infinite penalties it can have a different
numeric contract \cite{exec24_opengm_source}.

Consider an additive unary energy whose initial label has positive infinite
penalty and whose other label has zero penalty. The exact constrained
optimum is the feasible second label. Along the inspected incremental
update, the arithmetic expression is $+\infty+0-(+\infty)$, which is
not-a-number under the independently tested binary64 arithmetic. This is a
conditional source witness: no native OpenGM failure was observed, and
other algorithms or encodings may follow different paths. It motivates
fixtures for initially infeasible states, infinite penalties, NaNs and
finite substitutes. Replacing infinity by an arbitrary large constant is
not automatically valid; the constant must dominate a proved objective
range without creating unacceptable numeric error
\cite{exec24_opengm_source,exec24_opengm_choice}.

Function sharing saves representation space but does not remove repeated
factors. An authored unary factor with weights $(2,1)$ gives probability
$2/3$ to its first label after normalization. Adding the same factor twice
multiplies its contribution twice and yields $4/5$, a change of $2/15$.
That may be the intended model, or it may double-count one observation.
The adapter must distinguish shared storage identity from independent
evidence provenance \cite{exec24_opengm_paper,exec24_opengm_choice}.

Variable order is another semantic input. The inspected factor constructor
requires strictly increasing variable indices using runtime checks that
remain active when ordinary debug assertions are disabled. Other label
checks are assertions, and one direct-evaluation label check is commented
out in the selected source. This does not prove every invalid call lacks
validation; it requires entrypoint-specific tests. Sorting variable indices
must also permute factor axes. For a binary table with weights
$w(0,0)=1,w(0,1)=2,w(1,0)=3,w(1,1)=4$, the correct $P(X=1)$ is $7/10$.
Transposing its semantic axes changes the reported event probability to
$3/5$. Both tables normalize, so a sum-to-one check cannot detect the
meaning change \cite{exec24_opengm_source,exec24_opengm_choice}.

\subsection{A bounded public qualification route}
The proposed qualification suite starts with tiny fully enumerated models,
where energy optima, partition functions and marginal probabilities have
separate exact oracles. It varies semiring, solver, factor and variable
order, shared function identity, numeric type, constraint encoding and
early visitor stops. It tests valid inputs and typed refusals separately.
Even 40 binary variables have $2^{40}=1{,}099{,}511{,}627{,}776$ joint
assignments; finite representability is not a practical exhaustive-search
guarantee. Sparse or shared functions can reduce storage while leaving
the search combinatorics difficult \cite{exec24_opengm_choice}.

An isolated CLI or MCP worker should declare its exact artifact, enabled
solver dependencies, model algebra, supported factor class and requested
result. Its output distinguishes model rejection, unsupported algorithm,
timeout, incomplete incumbent, numeric failure and qualified result. A
timeout must preserve request identity and partial-result status; a retry
must not duplicate a business action. These are proposed adapter contracts,
not verified OpenGM integrations \cite{exec24_opengm_choice}.

Scientific ownership covers causal meaning and loss; numerical ownership
covers algebra, domain and valid bounds; adapter ownership covers ordering,
termination and request recovery; platform ownership covers maintenance,
rights and complete cost. A hypothetical 18,000 USD over 600 submitted,
450 completed and 300 qualified requests gives 30, 40 and 60 USD per request
under the respective denominators. Actual current support, compatibility,
prices and decision value remain unknown. Public tests can resolve bounded
implementation questions before expensive bank experiments. Hidden
assignment, unrestricted causal identification and arbitrary uncertainty
cannot be supplied by a general factor-graph solver. Package execution and
independent validation remain to be performed
\cite{exec24_opengm_choice}.

The chapter's comparisons locate several different causes of an apparently
successful but unusable answer. A bridge can change variable meaning, a
predictor can answer a conditional rather than interventional question, and
an inference engine can solve the wrong numerical objective. Even the right
objective may have unsupported probability terms or an inadequate numerical
approximation. The shared service must preserve the question at each of
these steps. The authoritative software choice in
Chapter~\ref{scisyn:software} therefore remains selective reuse with explicit
scientific and numerical boundaries; these examples explain the obligations
behind that choice.

~~~

~~~text
\chapter{Functional discovery, symbolic identification and estimating equations}
\label{v13:sw-functional}

\section{An operation is defined by its mathematical output}

A bank may ask a single software team to discover a graph, identify an effect
and estimate the value of an offer. Those requests have different outputs.
Discovery can return a selected directed graph, an equivalence class or a set
of candidates. Identification can return an expression in the observed law or
a nonidentification result. Estimation can return a number with a sampling
uncertainty claim. A shared product becomes misleading when it places all
three behind a field named ``causal effect'' without preserving these types.

Consider a renewal offer $A$, retained balance $Y$ and customer characteristics
$X$ available before the offer. A point-treatment question might be
\[
 \theta=\mathbb E\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}.
\]
Here the expectation is over a declared eligible population, not automatically
over the customers who accepted an offer. A discovery algorithm can help
propose relationships among $X,A,Y$, but a graph proposal does not by itself
establish that $Y(a)$ is identified. Even after identification, numerical
uncertainty depends on how customers were sampled and whether branch, household
or calendar dependence matters. Software comparison must preserve each stage.

The selected source paths considered here make these differences concrete.
DirectLiNGAM exposes a functional discovery construction; y0 exposes symbolic
identification; delicatessen exposes estimating equations; and statsmodels
exposes fitted treatment-effect operations with a joint GMM representation.
These are useful components of a workflow, with different scientific contracts.
The source inspections establish the specific interfaces and branches described
below. They do not establish execution speed, numerical agreement or empirical
coverage for this bank. \cite{v13_sw_lingam,v13_sw_y0,v13_sw_delicatessen,v13_sw_statsmodels}

\section{DirectLiNGAM: a selected order and the meaning of prior knowledge}

The inspected DirectLiNGAM class accepts a prior-knowledge matrix, an option to
apply it softly, a measure named \texttt{pwling}, \texttt{kernel} or
\texttt{pwling\_fast}, and a choice about adaptive-Lasso pruning. Its fit method
checks the prior matrix shape, selects variables sequentially, residualizes
remaining variables, stores a causal order and calls a separate adjacency
estimation routine. The fast branch refers to an additional GPU implementation;
the inspected dispatch is not a performance measurement. \cite{v13_sw_lingam}

The motivating functional family can be written
\[
 X_j=\sum_{k\in\operatorname{pa}(j)}b_{jk}X_k+\varepsilon_j,
\]
with an acyclic order and assumptions on the disturbances that distinguish this
family from unrestricted latent-confounded systems. A selected order narrows the
model much more than a CPDAG. That narrowing can be appropriate when its
functional assumptions are defensible; it cannot be justified by comparing only
the number of directed edges with a constraint-based method that deliberately
retains ambiguity. The present interface inspection does not verify the base
adjacency estimator or provide a finite confidence statement for the selected
order.

Prior knowledge is particularly consequential. The source describes zero as no
directed path, one as a directed path, and minus one as unspecified knowledge.
A bank statement that ``offer acceptance occurs after invitation'' is a temporal
constraint. It is not necessarily a statement that a directed path must exist.
Likewise, a field absent from the available dataset is not a justified absent
cause. An adapter should retain the original proposition, its variable order,
its path-versus-edge meaning and the evidence supporting it. Transposing an
array or treating an unknown entry as zero can change the scientific problem
without causing a matrix-shape error.

For funding analysis, useful adverse cases include a latent common market shock,
near-Gaussian disturbances and aggregation that mixes several decision times.
A comparison should ask whether the supplied functional model is valid in each
case, what output the package produces when it is not, and whether downstream
reporting preserves that limitation. It should not credit a plausible-looking
order as proof of an identified funding response. Constraint-based CPDAG/PAG
methods, alternative functional models and additional experimental information
are substantive alternatives, each requiring its own declared assumptions.

The appropriate first reuse candidate is an adapter around a reviewed operation.
Chapter~\ref{v131:graph-uncertainty} develops the base-class orientation and
total-effect calculation that this dispatch alone does not establish. A port
still needs matched behavioral references, dependency terms and a maintenance
case. The observed MIT license is evidence for that investigation, not a
completed dependency or maintenance assessment. \cite{v13_sw_lingam}

\section{y0: an expression can fail to exist before estimation begins}

The inspected \texttt{identify\_outcomes} function accepts an acyclic directed
mixed graph, treatment and outcome variables, optional conditions and an optional
ordering. With no conditions it dispatches to an ID routine; otherwise it
dispatches to IDC. It catches a dedicated \texttt{Unidentifiable} exception,
returning \texttt{None} by default and re-raising when \texttt{strict=True}.
This is a useful explicit boundary between a symbolic expression and a failure
to identify the requested query. It is only the dispatcher. The detailed
comparison in Section~\ref{exec18:pyid-packages} excludes the selected native
conditional path pending a separation repair, while the following chapter
traces recursion and graph validation. Recognizing this entry point cannot
override those narrower findings or establish general correctness. \cite{v13_sw_y0}

For example, suppose the graph justifies adjustment for pre-offer $X$. The
corresponding functional, under the relevant causal assumptions, is
\[
 \theta=\int\{m_1(x)-m_0(x)\}\,dP_X(x),
 \qquad m_a(x)=\mathbb E(Y\mid A=a,X=x).
\]
The expression states what must be estimated. It supplies neither fitted
regressions nor a sampling distribution. If instead the graph contains an
unblocked latent common cause of $A$ and $Y$ and no identifying information,
another optimizer cannot manufacture this adjustment functional.

A product therefore needs separate outcomes for an identified expression, a
scientifically nonidentified query, an unsupported query language and an
execution error. Mapping all of them to a numeric missing value loses essential
information. Conversely, turning \texttt{None} into zero would state that the
effect is zero, which is a different scientific claim. The adapter should bind
the graph, query, conditioning variables and expression identities, and retain
the original failure type and diagnostic context.

Counterfactual coverage also needs care. The existence of an ID/IDC entry point
does not establish support for every nested counterfactual, path-specific effect
or transport query. The proper comparison has a query matrix: adjustment,
general intervention identification, conditional interventions and other
counterfactual families. Each cell should be tied to an inspected algorithm and
an independent small-graph reference. Package branding is not evidence for every
cell. A proposed symbolic adapter is justified as a candidate by this source;
general algorithmic correctness and comprehensive query support remain open.

\section{Stacked equations preserve the nuisance contribution}

Delicatessen's inspected \texttt{ee\_aipw} function returns an array of estimating
equations rather than a fitted effect object. Its parameter vector contains the
effect, two potential-outcome means and propensity/outcome coefficients. The
documentation and source identify a $(3+b+c)\times n$ array, where $b$ and $c$
are the numbers of propensity and outcome coefficients. The source selects
logistic or linear outcome regression from the outcome values unless the user
forces a continuous treatment of the outcome. It clips estimated propensity
probabilities only when bounds are supplied. \cite{v13_sw_delicatessen}

Writing $e(x)=P(A=1\mid X=x)$ and $m_a(x)=\mathbb E(Y\mid A=a,X=x)$, the
familiar augmented mean contribution has the form
\[
 \phi_a(O)=m_a(X)+\frac{\mathbf 1\{A=a\}}{p_a(X)}
                   \{Y-m_a(X)\},
 \qquad p_1=e,\quad p_0=1-e.
\]
This display describes the target contribution; its causal interpretation still
requires consistency, exchangeability and positivity for the declared target.
Stacking nuisance equations alongside equations for the two means makes their
joint role explicit. To obtain an estimator one must solve the system; to obtain
uncertainty one must review its Jacobian, covariance construction and sampling
unit. An array of equations is not an already qualified sandwich estimator.

This distinction matters when borrowers are clustered. If $\psi_i(\eta)$ is
an estimating-function contribution, an independent-customer covariance uses
individual outer products. A group construction instead uses sums
$\Psi_g(\eta)=\sum_{i\in g}\psi_i(\eta)$ and outer products of those group
sums, under an appropriate group sampling law. Neither replacing a variable
name nor using a generic robust standard error proves the required result for
shared calendars or persistent audit errors. The solver and covariance paths
must be inspected and qualified separately.

Truncation is also part of the method. Replacing $\widehat e$ by
$\max(\epsilon,\min(1-\epsilon,\widehat e))$ controls numerical weights but
changes the estimating equation. For a bank with almost no offers in a risk
stratum, clipping cannot create the missing counterfactual support. An adapter
must record the requested and applied bounds, how many observations were
affected, and the sensitivity of the answer. A stable answer after clipping is
not evidence that positivity held before clipping.

The same module exposes instrumental-variable and two-stage least-squares
estimating-equation entries. Their presence identifies a useful review route;
it does not establish weak-instrument inference, longitudinal g-estimation or
arbitrary dependent-data support. Reusing a selected equation as an independent
numerical reference may be valuable even when a complete estimator is not yet
an adoption candidate. \cite{v13_sw_delicatessen}

\section{statsmodels: population and covariance must survive the interface}

The inspected \texttt{TreatmentEffect} class describes five methods: IPW,
regression adjustment, AIPW, AIPW with weighted least squares and IPW with
regression adjustment. It states that inference uses a joint GMM representation
of the selection model, outcome model and effect functions. Its current outcome
model is documented as linear OLS. The result class exposes contrasts for an
effect and two potential-outcome means. \cite{v13_sw_statsmodels}

The population option distinguishes all, treated and untreated observations.
These correspond to different questions. With treatment propensity $e(X)$,
the all-population target averages over $P_X$, whereas an effect among treated
customers averages over $P_{X\mid A=1}$. Matching point estimates across packages
requires matching this measure as well as the outcome and treatment coding.
An apparent disagreement can be a population mismatch rather than a numerical
defect; apparent agreement in one synthetic sample can conceal the mismatch.

The class also exposes \texttt{ps\_bounds}, defaulting to $(0.001,0.999)$, and
validates strictly interior, ordered bounds. The selected source says the same
bounds apply to point estimates and GMM moment conditions. That is part of a
reproducible comparison, including a comparison with delicatessen's optional
truncation. Omitting these settings from a bridge specification means the two
programs need not be solving the same estimating equations.

One especially important detail is the internal \texttt{\_cov\_type} argument.
The inspected class states that it affects preliminary estimators but that final
GMM results correspond to HC0. A caller cannot infer clustered or calendar-robust
final inference merely from passing a covariance-related keyword. The early
module header also contains older prose about unavailable standard errors,
while the class and result implementation contain joint-GMM inference. Reviewing
the current operation resolves more than searching for one favorable or
unfavorable sentence. \cite{v13_sw_statsmodels}

For a banking baseline, this makes the candidate scope concrete: a declared
point-treatment population, linear outcome model, explicit propensity bounds
and a reviewed sampling law. Panel or time-series capabilities elsewhere in
statsmodels require separate inspection; they are not established by this one
module. The correct reuse decision is provisional numerical integration and
matched equation review, with refusal of unsupported dependence claims.

\section{A matched comparison must keep the question fixed}

A useful comparison first fixes the eligible population, intervention, observed
history and identification assumptions. It then asks each tool for its actual
output type: an order, symbolic expression, equation array or fitted estimate.
Small independent references can test the transitions. For discovery they
check order/edge conventions and unresolved alternatives. For identification
they check graph-query expression and failure semantics. For estimation they
check the target measure, nuisance fits, truncation and covariance construction.

Numerical disagreements should be classified by cause before they are ranked.
A different estimand is a semantic mismatch. A failed root solve is a numerical
failure. Nonidentification is a scientific outcome. An unsupported covariance
law is a scope limitation. Timing a successful branch while discarding the
others rewards a system for silently avoiding difficult requests. The full
comparison must retain them and distinguish source inspection from executed
behavior. These distinctions make the software survey useful for design and
investment even when it concludes that a component should be deferred.
~~~

~~~text
\chapter{Functional discovery, symbolic identification and estimating equations}
\label{v13:sw-functional}

\section{An operation is defined by its mathematical output}

A bank may ask a single software team to discover a graph, identify an effect
and estimate the value of an offer. Those requests have different outputs.
Discovery can return a selected directed graph, an equivalence class or a set
of candidates. Identification can return an expression in the observed law or
a nonidentification result. Estimation can return a number with a sampling
uncertainty claim. A shared product becomes misleading when it places all
three behind a field named ``causal effect'' without preserving these types.

Consider a renewal offer $A$, retained balance $Y$ and customer characteristics
$X$ available before the offer. A point-treatment question might be
\[
 \theta=\mathbb E\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}.
\]
Here the expectation is over a declared eligible population, not automatically
over the customers who accepted an offer. A discovery algorithm can help
propose relationships among $X,A,Y$, but a graph proposal does not by itself
establish that $Y(a)$ is identified. Even after identification, numerical
uncertainty depends on how customers were sampled and whether branch, household
or calendar dependence matters. Software comparison must preserve each stage.

The selected source paths considered here make these differences concrete.
DirectLiNGAM exposes a functional discovery construction; y0 exposes symbolic
identification; delicatessen exposes estimating equations; and statsmodels
exposes fitted treatment-effect operations with a joint GMM representation.
These are useful components of a workflow, with different scientific contracts.
The source inspections establish the specific interfaces and branches described
below. They do not establish execution speed, numerical agreement or empirical
coverage for this bank. \citep{v13_sw_lingam,v13_sw_y0,v13_sw_delicatessen,v13_sw_statsmodels}
~~~

~~~text
\chapter{Keeping causal uncertainty through the calculation}
\label{v131:graph-uncertainty}

\section{A graph representation carries a scientific commitment}

A funding analyst may represent the offer rate, customer response and retained
balance by three columns in a matrix. The columns are measurements; the graph
and structural equations give those measurements causal meaning. An adapter
must preserve both. A correctly sized matrix can still encode every arrow in
the wrong direction, and a software object called a mixed graph can still
contain a directed cycle that is inadmissible for the requested identification
algorithm. These errors arise before questions of estimation accuracy.

The inspected LiNGAM base class stores a coefficient in
$B_{\text{target},\text{predictor}}$. It fits each variable against eligible
variables earlier in its selected causal order, optionally removing predictors
excluded by the prior-knowledge matrix. The adaptive-Lasso branch and ordinary
regression branch therefore share an orientation convention. A conversion to
an edge list must interpret a nonzero $B_{jk}$ as $k\to j$.
\cite{v131_sw_lingam}

For a stipulated banking example, let $F$ denote a funding change, $A$ an offer
adjustment and $Y$ the balance response, in declared normalized units:
\[
 A=0.4F+\varepsilon_A,\qquad
 Y=0.1F+2A+\varepsilon_Y.
\]
Holding the disturbance law fixed, a one-unit intervention on $F$ changes $A$
by $0.4$ and $Y$ by $0.1+2(0.4)=0.9$. The latter consists of the direct path
and the mediated path. Transposing this coefficient matrix produces a different
acyclic graph with no path from $F$ to $Y$; its corresponding total effect is
zero. Shape checks and acyclicity checks both pass. An asymmetric intervention
example, rather than a symmetric matrix fixture, detects the semantic error.
The arithmetic is an original project example, not a fit to a bank dataset.
\cite{v131_project_semantics}

LiNGAM's inspected \texttt{estimate\_total\_effect} method regresses the target
on the source and the source's estimated parents. If the requested target is
earlier than the source in the selected causal order, the method warns and
continues. The numerical coefficient remains an output; the warning records a
reason its causal interpretation may be inappropriate. A service that records
only the returned floating-point number would lose that distinction. It should
carry the order, adjustment variables, warning and fitted graph together, and
state whether the request falls within the admitted functional model.
\cite{v131_sw_lingam}

An alternative based on a CPDAG or PAG has different obligations. Its uncertain
arrowheads cannot be collapsed to LiNGAM's selected directed order solely to
make a common schema convenient. An output union or equivalence-class type is
necessary when the method retains several causal possibilities. The common
operation contract should make the statistical and scientific scope visible
without suggesting that one representation is intrinsically superior.

\section{Recursive identification and the reasons for refusal}

y0's inspected ID implementation first handles the absence of interventions,
restricts to outcome ancestors, adds irrelevant variables to the intervention
set and decomposes the graph remaining after treatment removal into districts.
Districts represent connected components of the graph's bidirected component.
The implementation multiplies and marginalizes expressions across the relevant
recursive subproblems. In the connected-district failure branch it raises
\texttt{Unidentifiable}; separate invariant failures raise \texttt{RuntimeError}.
These paths support different service responses. \cite{v131_sw_y0}

For a mixed graph with $A\to Y$ and a latent common cause represented by
$A\leftrightarrow Y$, observational data alone generally cannot distinguish
the intervention response from the common-cause association. The absence of an
identifying functional is a scientific conclusion within that model class.
It is distinct from a malformed graph, an unsupported query or a failed
computation. Treating all four as the same missing value would conceal whether
the analyst needs another measurement, another algorithm or a software repair.

The inspected IDC routine examines whether a conditioning variable can be
exchanged for an intervention using a rule-2 condition. Its helper removes
incoming edges to existing interventions and outgoing edges from the candidate
variable, then delegates a separation test. If the exchange is allowed, IDC
recurses; otherwise it identifies the unconditioned joint query and normalizes
the resulting expression. This establishes the inspected control flow. The
separation routine, expression normalization and all graph-mutilation behavior
require their own correctness evidence. Section~\ref{exec18:pyid-packages}
provides a separation counterexample and excludes this selected native
conditional route pending repair. The control flow described here does not
supersede that restriction. A claim about the whole algorithm cannot be based
solely on recognizing the names of its steps.
\cite{v131_sw_y0}

Graph construction deserves explicit validation at the library boundary.
y0's inspected constructor sets graph metadata, while directed-edge insertion
adds edges and corresponding nodes to both graph components. These methods do
not themselves enforce acyclicity. The identification wrapper should therefore
validate the admitted graph class, query membership, permissible overlap of
treatment/outcome/conditioning sets and any supplied ordering before invoking
the algorithm. The required checks depend on the query language; counterfactual
variables need more than the rules for ordinary intervention queries.
\cite{v131_sw_y0}

A useful qualification set should contain an adjustment example, a front-door
example, a genuinely nonidentified example and malformed inputs with known
failure types. Variable renaming should change names while preserving the
mathematical expression. Adding an irrelevant measured variable should preserve
the effect after valid marginalization. These are proposed behavioral checks;
they have not been run against the newly acquired source here. A symbolic
expression still needs a numerical estimator and a sampling argument before a
bank can use it to choose an offer.

\section{What an estimating-equation column means}

For contributions $\psi_i(\eta)\in\mathbb R^p$, an estimating-equation method
solves $\sum_i\psi_i(\widehat\eta)=0$. A usual independent-unit sandwich takes
\[
 \widehat A=-\frac1n\sum_i\frac{\partial\psi_i}{\partial\eta^\top},
 \qquad
 \widehat B=\frac1n\sum_i\psi_i\psi_i^\top,
 \qquad
 \widehat{\operatorname{Var}}(\widehat\eta)
 =\frac1n\widehat A^{-1}\widehat B\widehat A^{-\top}.
\]
This display states a construction under regular identification and an
appropriate independent-unit asymptotic law. It is not a universal uncertainty
formula for arbitrary records.

The inspected delicatessen \texttt{MEstimator} obtains the number of observations
from the supplied contribution array, solves the stacked equations, differentiates
their sum and scales both bread and meat by that number. It then divides the
assembled sandwich by the number again to obtain estimator variance.
\texttt{compute\_meat} forms the matrix product of the supplied contribution
array with its transpose. The function does not discover household clusters
or add serial lag products based on variable names. \cite{v131_sw_delicatessen}

Suppose two independent groups each contribute two perfectly aligned scores,
$(1,1)$ and $(-1,-1)$. The sum of individual squared contributions is four;
the sum of squared group totals is eight. With the same total-score derivative
of four, the uncorrected variance calculations are $4/16=0.25$ and $8/16=0.5$.
The discrepancy is structural. A finite-sample degrees-of-freedom adjustment
does not create the missing cross-products. This example illustrates covariance
bookkeeping, not consistency with only two groups. \cite{v131_project_semantics}

For $K$ independent groups of size $m$, equal marginal variance $\sigma^2$ and
within-group correlation $\rho$, direct summation of the covariance terms gives
\[
 \operatorname{Var}(\overline Y)
 =\frac{\sigma^2}{Km}\{1+(m-1)\rho\}.
\]
With $K=100$, $m=10$ and $\rho=0.3$, the multiplier is $3.7$. A thousand records
then have the same variance as about $270.27$ independent records with the same
marginal variance. A common calendar shock across the groups violates the
independence used in this calculation; merely labeling borrowers as clusters
does not resolve it. \cite{v131_project_semantics}

One possible integration lets the caller supply independent group totals as
columns. That choice needs a derivation of weighting, normalization and target
population, especially for unequal group sizes. Another constructs an explicit
long-run covariance from time-ordered scores. A third retains dependence within
independently sampled whole histories. These alternatives change the sampling
contract and must be compared on that basis.

Delicatessen permits a pseudoinverse by default when building the sandwich.
This is useful numerical behavior in some problems, but a finite matrix is not
evidence that the parameter is identified. If the derivative of the moment
condition loses rank, an ordinary inverse-based asymptotic argument may fail;
using a generalized inverse does not supply the missing information. Solver
residuals, Jacobian rank, scaling and the distinction between regular intervals
and weak-information set inversion belong in the result contract.
\cite{v131_sw_delicatessen}

\section{Final covariance settings must reach the final estimator}

The inspected statsmodels GMM path defaults to \texttt{weights\_method=cov},
using an outer-product moment covariance divided by the number of observations.
There is a separate HAC path with an explicit maximum lag and a kernel, defaulting
to Bartlett. The results object obtains the method from the GMM fit options and
uses it in the final covariance calculation. The low-level presence of HAC is
evidence of an available code path, not evidence that every treatment-effect
wrapper exposes or activates it. \cite{v131_sw_statsmodels}

The treatment-effect entry point explicitly distinguishes its preliminary
outcome-regression covariance setting from final GMM inference. An adapter must
therefore record both stages and trace the actual final call. A statement such
as ``robust errors requested'' is insufficient to distinguish independent
heteroskedastic, household-cluster and serially dependent constructions.
Calendar ordering, lag choice and the relation between estimation weights and
covariance weights also need to survive serialization. \cite{v13_sw_statsmodels,v131_sw_statsmodels}

The inspected GMM centering expression subtracts \texttt{moms.mean()}, a scalar
mean over the array, rather than an explicit vector of column means. This is
a concrete behavior to compare against an analytic moment reference, particularly
when numerical residuals or overidentification leave nonzero sample moments.
It is not sufficient by itself to conclude that the intended fitted estimator
is incorrect. The outcome depends on the full score, weighting and convergence
conditions. Source review identifies the test; executed evidence must answer it.
\cite{v131_sw_statsmodels}

\section{Orthogonality isolates a rate obligation}

Let $Z$ be an instrument, $D$ an action, $Y$ an outcome and $X$ measured
pre-action information. Write $m(X)=E(Z\mid X)$, $r(X)=E(D\mid X)$ and
$\ell(X)=E(Y\mid X)$. Suppose the declared causal model identifies $\theta_0$
through
\[
 E[(Z-m(X))\{Y-\ell(X)-\theta_0(D-r(X))\}]=0.
\]
This is an assumption about an identified model; the existence of these
conditional means does not establish a valid instrument.

Condition on nuisance functions trained on an independent pilot with the same
target law. Denote their errors by $\delta_m,\delta_r,\delta_\ell$. Expanding
the fitted score and using the zero conditional means of the centered variables
gives the exact remainder
\[
 R(\theta_0)
 =E[\delta_m(X)\{\delta_\ell(X)-\theta_0\delta_r(X)\}],
 \qquad
 |R(\theta_0)|\leq
 \|\delta_m\|_2\bigl(\|\delta_\ell\|_2+|\theta_0|\|\delta_r\|_2\bigr).
\]
The inequality follows from Cauchy--Schwarz and the triangle inequality. It
exposes exactly what must be bounded for this score. In a two-stratum arithmetic
example with equal stratum probabilities, errors $(0.1,0.2,0.05)$ and
$(-0.1,0.1,-0.05)$, ordered as $(\delta_m,\delta_\ell,\delta_r)$, and
$\theta_0=2$, the remainder is $-0.005$. \cite{v131_project_semantics}

The identity provides no convergence rate for a particular learner. Reusing
dependent observations for training and evaluation also invalidates the simple
conditioning argument unless additional separation or dependence control is
proved. Support failures and misspecification can prevent the errors from
shrinking. For weak instrument relevance, even a well-controlled score may
yield an unbounded effect set under test inversion. This is why source-level
availability of flexible learners and sandwich calculations cannot settle
the unresolved research on dependent learning and useful precision.


The same requirement runs from graph storage to the final interval: retain
the scientific meaning of each intermediate object. Directed coefficients,
identified expressions, independent score contributions and rate bounds do
not become substitutes for one another when they share a software interface.
The estimation comparisons that follow use this distinction to examine which
population is averaged, which estimating equation is solved and which
variation the reported uncertainty actually includes.

~~~
