~~~text
\chapter{Keeping causal uncertainty through the calculation}
\label{v131:graph-uncertainty}

\section{A graph representation carries a scientific commitment}

A funding analyst may represent the offer rate, customer response and retained
balance by three columns in a matrix. The columns are measurements; the graph
and structural equations give those measurements causal meaning. An adapter
must preserve both. A correctly sized matrix can still encode every arrow in
the wrong direction, and a software object called a mixed graph can still
contain a directed cycle that is inadmissible for the requested identification
algorithm. These errors arise before questions of estimation accuracy.

The inspected LiNGAM base class stores a coefficient in
$B_{\text{target},\text{predictor}}$. It fits each variable against eligible
variables earlier in its selected causal order, optionally removing predictors
excluded by the prior-knowledge matrix. The adaptive-Lasso branch and ordinary
regression branch therefore share an orientation convention. A conversion to
an edge list must interpret a nonzero $B_{jk}$ as $k\to j$.
\citep{v131_sw_lingam}

For a stipulated banking example, let $F$ denote a funding change, $A$ an offer
adjustment and $Y$ the balance response, in declared normalized units:
\[
 A=0.4F+\varepsilon_A,\qquad
 Y=0.1F+2A+\varepsilon_Y.
\]
Holding the disturbance law fixed, a one-unit intervention on $F$ changes $A$
by $0.4$ and $Y$ by $0.1+2(0.4)=0.9$. The latter consists of the direct path
and the mediated path. Transposing this coefficient matrix produces a different
acyclic graph with no path from $F$ to $Y$; its corresponding total effect is
zero. Shape checks and acyclicity checks both pass. An asymmetric intervention
example, rather than a symmetric matrix fixture, detects the semantic error.
The arithmetic is an original project example, not a fit to a bank dataset.
\citep{v131_project_semantics}

LiNGAM's inspected \texttt{estimate\_total\_effect} method regresses the target
on the source and the source's estimated parents. If the requested target is
earlier than the source in the selected causal order, the method warns and
continues. The numerical coefficient remains an output; the warning records a
reason its causal interpretation may be inappropriate. A service that records
only the returned floating-point number would lose that distinction. It should
carry the order, adjustment variables, warning and fitted graph together, and
state whether the request falls within the admitted functional model.
\citep{v131_sw_lingam}

An alternative based on a CPDAG or PAG has different obligations. Its uncertain
arrowheads cannot be collapsed to LiNGAM's selected directed order solely to
make a common schema convenient. An output union or equivalence-class type is
necessary when the method retains several causal possibilities. The common
operation contract should make the statistical and scientific scope visible
without suggesting that one representation is intrinsically superior.

\section{Recursive identification and the reasons for refusal}

y0's inspected ID implementation first handles the absence of interventions,
restricts to outcome ancestors, adds irrelevant variables to the intervention
set and decomposes the graph remaining after treatment removal into districts.
Districts represent connected components of the graph's bidirected component.
The implementation multiplies and marginalizes expressions across the relevant
recursive subproblems. In the connected-district failure branch it raises
\texttt{Unidentifiable}; separate invariant failures raise \texttt{RuntimeError}.
These paths support different service responses. \citep{v131_sw_y0}

For a mixed graph with $A\to Y$ and a latent common cause represented by
$A\leftrightarrow Y$, observational data alone generally cannot distinguish
the intervention response from the common-cause association. The absence of an
identifying functional is a scientific conclusion within that model class.
It is distinct from a malformed graph, an unsupported query or a failed
computation. Treating all four as the same missing value would conceal whether
the analyst needs another measurement, another algorithm or a software repair.

The inspected IDC routine examines whether a conditioning variable can be
exchanged for an intervention using a rule-2 condition. Its helper removes
incoming edges to existing interventions and outgoing edges from the candidate
variable, then delegates a separation test. If the exchange is allowed, IDC
recurses; otherwise it identifies the unconditioned joint query and normalizes
the resulting expression. This establishes the inspected control flow. The
separation routine, expression normalization and all graph-mutilation behavior
require their own correctness evidence. Section~\ref{exec18:pyid-packages}
provides a separation counterexample and excludes this selected native
conditional route pending repair. The control flow described here does not
supersede that restriction. A claim about the whole algorithm cannot be based
solely on recognizing the names of its steps.
\citep{v131_sw_y0}

Graph construction deserves explicit validation at the library boundary.
y0's inspected constructor sets graph metadata, while directed-edge insertion
adds edges and corresponding nodes to both graph components. These methods do
not themselves enforce acyclicity. The identification wrapper should therefore
validate the admitted graph class, query membership, permissible overlap of
treatment/outcome/conditioning sets and any supplied ordering before invoking
the algorithm. The required checks depend on the query language; counterfactual
variables need more than the rules for ordinary intervention queries.
\citep{v131_sw_y0}

A useful qualification set should contain an adjustment example, a front-door
example, a genuinely nonidentified example and malformed inputs with known
failure types. Variable renaming should change names while preserving the
mathematical expression. Adding an irrelevant measured variable should preserve
the effect after valid marginalization. These are proposed behavioral checks;
they have not been run against the newly acquired source here. A symbolic
expression still needs a numerical estimator and a sampling argument before a
bank can use it to choose an offer.

\section{What an estimating-equation column means}

For contributions $\psi_i(\eta)\in\mathbb R^p$, an estimating-equation method
solves $\sum_i\psi_i(\widehat\eta)=0$. A usual independent-unit sandwich takes
\[
 \widehat A=-\frac1n\sum_i\frac{\partial\psi_i}{\partial\eta^\top},
 \qquad
 \widehat B=\frac1n\sum_i\psi_i\psi_i^\top,
 \qquad
 \widehat{\operatorname{Var}}(\widehat\eta)
 =\frac1n\widehat A^{-1}\widehat B\widehat A^{-\top}.
\]
This display states a construction under regular identification and an
appropriate independent-unit asymptotic law. It is not a universal uncertainty
formula for arbitrary records.

The inspected delicatessen \texttt{MEstimator} obtains the number of observations
from the supplied contribution array, solves the stacked equations, differentiates
their sum and scales both bread and meat by that number. It then divides the
assembled sandwich by the number again to obtain estimator variance.
\texttt{compute\_meat} forms the matrix product of the supplied contribution
array with its transpose. The function does not discover household clusters
or add serial lag products based on variable names. \citep{v131_sw_delicatessen}

Suppose two independent groups each contribute two perfectly aligned scores,
$(1,1)$ and $(-1,-1)$. The sum of individual squared contributions is four;
the sum of squared group totals is eight. With the same total-score derivative
of four, the uncorrected variance calculations are $4/16=0.25$ and $8/16=0.5$.
The discrepancy is structural. A finite-sample degrees-of-freedom adjustment
does not create the missing cross-products. This example illustrates covariance
bookkeeping, not consistency with only two groups. \citep{v131_project_semantics}

For $K$ independent groups of size $m$, equal marginal variance $\sigma^2$ and
within-group correlation $\rho$, direct summation of the covariance terms gives
\[
 \operatorname{Var}(\overline Y)
 =\frac{\sigma^2}{Km}\{1+(m-1)\rho\}.
\]
With $K=100$, $m=10$ and $\rho=0.3$, the multiplier is $3.7$. A thousand records
then have the same variance as about $270.27$ independent records with the same
marginal variance. A common calendar shock across the groups violates the
independence used in this calculation; merely labeling borrowers as clusters
does not resolve it. \citep{v131_project_semantics}

One possible integration lets the caller supply independent group totals as
columns. That choice needs a derivation of weighting, normalization and target
population, especially for unequal group sizes. Another constructs an explicit
long-run covariance from time-ordered scores. A third retains dependence within
independently sampled whole histories. These alternatives change the sampling
contract and must be compared on that basis.

Delicatessen permits a pseudoinverse by default when building the sandwich.
This is useful numerical behavior in some problems, but a finite matrix is not
evidence that the parameter is identified. If the derivative of the moment
condition loses rank, an ordinary inverse-based asymptotic argument may fail;
using a generalized inverse does not supply the missing information. Solver
residuals, Jacobian rank, scaling and the distinction between regular intervals
and weak-information set inversion belong in the result contract.
\citep{v131_sw_delicatessen}

\section{Final covariance settings must reach the final estimator}

The inspected statsmodels GMM path defaults to \texttt{weights\_method=cov},

It is not sufficient by itself to conclude that the intended fitted estimator
~~~

~~~text
\section{Julia effect estimators and the meaning of a reusable result}
\label{exec21:effects-scope}

A servicing manager asks whether an additional contact is worth its cost for
each risk group. A numerical answer requires more than a treatment-effect
method name. The population average, the average among previously contacted
customers, a conditional contrast and a coefficient from residual regression
can differ even when every conditional model is known. Missing outcomes can
change the averaging population again. A reusable interface must carry this
meaning from the business question through the numerical calculation.

The selected Julia sources offer several distinct building blocks.
CausalELM~0.8.0 supplies g-computation and effect learners; CausalForest~0.1.5
implements tree-based contrasts; TMLE~0.20.5 supplies targeted and one-step
estimation; and CausalTables~1.3.4 represents causal data and interventions in
supplied structural models. Their selected source revisions precede the
7~October~2026 cutoff. Their MIT notices permit substantial reuse subject to
retaining the specified notices, but do not establish dependency clearance or
statistical validity. The following comparison concerns inspected source
paths, not native execution or a tested combination of these packages.
\cite{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_causalforest,exec21_effects_tmle,exec21_effects_causaltables}

\subsection{An average effect is a population-specific quantity}
\label{exec21:effects-target}

Let $W$ denote pre-contact risk information, $A\in\{0,1\}$ the contact action,
and $Y(a)$ the monetary outcome under action $a$. Under consistency,
conditional exchangeability and treatment positivity, write
$e(w)=\Pr(A=1\mid W=w)$ and
$\tau(w)=E\{Y(1)-Y(0)\mid W=w\}$. The population average treatment effect is
$E\{\tau(W)\}$; the treated-population effect is
$E\{\tau(W)\mid A=1\}$. A prediction vector containing estimated
$\tau(W_i)$ is useful, but does not by itself provide uncertainty for a
selected subgroup or a policy chosen using that vector.

In the inspected CausalELM g-formula path, ATE, ITT and CATE labels use
counterfactual predictions at all supplied covariate rows; the other branch
uses treated rows. The g-computation wrapper averages the contrast, while the
S-learner retains a contrast vector. The T-learner fits each treatment arm and
subtracts its predictions on the supplied covariates. Thus an ITT label only
has an assignment-effect interpretation when the supplied treatment actually
represents assignment; it cannot convert receipt of contact into randomized
offer assignment. Categorical coding, the action clock and the averaging
population must be explicit inputs. \cite{exec21_effects_causalelm}

The same source's double-machine-learning path forms outcome and treatment
residuals and averages foldwise least-squares coefficients. Its fold
constructor slices contiguous rows. Neither the name nor contiguous slicing
supplies a dependence argument for repeated customer records. Even with
oracle nuisance functions and independent observations, a partially linear
coefficient need not be the population average under heterogeneous effects.
To see the distinction, take
$Y=m_0(W)+A\tau(W)+\varepsilon$ with
$E(\varepsilon\mid A,W)=0$. If $m(W)=E(Y\mid W)$, then
$Y-m(W)=(A-e(W))\tau(W)+\varepsilon$. Consequently the population residual
coefficient is
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-residual}
 \theta_{\rm res}
 =\frac{E[(A-e(W))(Y-m(W))]}{E[(A-e(W))^2]}
 =\frac{E[e(W)(1-e(W))\tau(W)]}{E[e(W)(1-e(W))]}.
\end{equation}
This is an overlap-weighted average in this model. A constant effect or
additional conditions can make it agree with the ATE; cross-fitting alone
does not do so. This derivation concerns the oracle population target and is
not a claim that a fitted implementation attains it in finite samples.
\cite{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_reference}

Return to the stipulated servicing population used for the R forest in
Section~\ref{exec21:grfr-target}: the two risk groups have equal population
mass, effects $(-4,12)$ dollars and treatment propensities $(1/2,1/10)$.
Using the same law makes the target comparison across languages explicit;
it supplies no independent empirical corroboration. Exact enumeration gives
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-targetexample}
 \tau_{\rm ATE}=4,\qquad
 \tau_{\rm ATT}=-\frac43,\qquad
 \theta_{\rm res}=\frac4{17}.
\end{equation}
At an assumed cost of USD~2 per additional contact, the population margin is
USD~2. Subtracting that cost from the residual coefficient instead gives
$-30/17$ dollars. These quantities answer different questions. A proposed
product should refuse a request to relabel the coefficient as the
population-wide incremental value. A separately evaluated targeted policy
could be more useful, but selecting the profitable subgroup and estimating
its value require their own information and uncertainty contract.
\cite{exec21_effects_reference}

\subsection{Source-level uncertainty checks before adoption}
\label{exec21:effects-uncertainty}

The inspected CausalELM generic null routine samples treatment independently
and uniformly from its distinct observed values, or uniformly over its
observed range for a continuous treatment. This does not preserve the
observed treatment count. It also does not reproduce a nonuniform propensity
law or a cluster-randomized design without additional construction. For four
customers assigned exactly one contact, independent fair binary draws retain
that design with probability $4/16=1/4$ and create an empty treatment or
control arm with probability $2/16=1/8$. This exact calculation identifies an
assignment-law mismatch, rather than estimating a package error rate.
\cite{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_reference}

There is also a direct dimensional problem in the selected source. For null
statistics $z_1,\ldots,z_B$ and reported statistic $t$, its scalar
\texttt{stderr} expression is
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-scale}
 s_{\rm code}=\frac{\sum_{b=1}^{B}(t-z_b)^2}{(B-1)\sqrt B}.
\end{equation}
If $t$ and $z_b$ are monetary effects, this expression has squared monetary
units. Multiplication of all outcomes by a positive constant $c$ multiplies
it by $c^2$, whereas a standard error must scale by $c$. With
$(z_1,z_2,z_3,z_4)=(-1,-1,1,1)$ and $t=2$, the expression is $10/3$.
Changing dollars to cents multiplies it by 10,000 rather than 100. This is a
source-level defect in the expression at the selected revision. It does not
require a Julia run to establish the algebra, and it does not establish the
finite-sample behavior of every other package function.
\cite{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_reference}

Taking a square root in one location would not settle the larger inference
question. The sampled null law, the centering, the effect being tested and
the relationship between repeated randomization draws and sampling
uncertainty would still need justification. The inspected interval routine
searches a finite grid with tail comparisons against the same null draws;
that code alone is insufficient to claim a valid general confidence-interval
inversion. The proposed reuse decision is therefore to reject unqualified
reuse of this uncertainty path, while retaining the point-estimation family
as a candidate for a separately justified implementation.
\cite{exec21_effects_causalelm}

CausalForest illustrates a different boundary. Its interface exposes
bootstrap or subsampling choices and separate split and leaf samples under
the honest-tree option. However, its outcome-centering forest is fitted
before these per-tree splits. The inspected prediction paths form
within-leaf treated and control weights and return centered-outcome
contrasts. They return point predictions, rather than a calibrated covariance
object. Honesty of the tree split is consequently not sufficient evidence
that all nuisance fitting is independent of evaluation, nor evidence of
equivalence to the separately reviewed \texttt{grf} implementation. A native
comparison should examine the actual nuisance training rows, treatment
coding, arm support in leaves, random-number state and failure behavior
before considering any shared inference contract.
\cite{exec21_effects_causalforest}

\subsection{Targeted estimation, missingness and influence scales}
\label{exec21:effects-tmle}

TMLE exposes nuisance-model choices, outer resampling and propensity
truncation. The selected noncollaborative default uses no outer resampling;
a requested cross-validation design must be supplied and preserved. The
implementation's \texttt{weighted} constructor default is true although
adjacent prose says false, another reason to record actual resolved
configuration. Truncation prevents numerical division by a very small
estimated probability but cannot create absent treatment support. For an
identified ATE in an independent sampling model, the familiar influence
expression clarifies what an adapter must carry:
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-influence}
\begin{aligned}
 D_i={}&\frac{A_i}{e(W_i)}\{Y_i-m_1(W_i)\}
       -\frac{1-A_i}{1-e(W_i)}\{Y_i-m_0(W_i)\}\\
       &+m_1(W_i)-m_0(W_i)-\psi .
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The relevant source combines outcome-residual and counterfactual-mean
components. It stores the standard deviation of the estimated influence
values together with their number, and passes both to the testing
interface. This scale is not already the standard error of the effect.
Conditional asymptotic inference additionally needs suitable nuisance
remainder control, positivity, moments and a sampling-law argument; the
presence of an influence vector does not prove those conditions.
\cite{exec21_effects_tmle}

For example, influence values $(-3,-1,1,3)$ have sample variance $20/3$.
Under the independent mean calculation, the variance of the average is
$5/3$, since $n=4$. Exporting the influence standard deviation as an effect
standard error would miss the factor $\sqrt n$. Conversely, dividing a
quantity that is already a standard error by $\sqrt n$ again would understate
uncertainty. For joint outputs, the source stacks influence vectors and
computes their covariance. A bridge must preserve the identity and order
of contributing customers; equal vector length is insufficient when
different outcomes select different complete cases. Cluster or calendar
dependence requires an appropriate covariance construction, not a new field
name on the independent calculation. \cite{exec21_effects_tmle,exec21_effects_reference}

The missingness documentation is explicit: rows missing variables needed
for estimation are dropped. Without cross-validation, propensity fitting
may use additional records with observed treatment and confounders even
when outcomes are missing. The source's final fluctuation dataset nevertheless
uses complete relevant records. This is useful data reuse, but is not a
general missing-outcome correction. \cite{exec21_effects_tmle}

Let $R$ indicate observation of the outcome. Consider independent customers
with equally frequent risk groups, randomized contact probability $1/2$,
effects $(-4,12)$ and outcome-response probabilities $(9/10,1/10)$ by risk
group. Suppose response is independent of potential outcomes and contact
conditional on risk, so missing at random holds in this example and response
positivity holds. The complete cases have group probabilities $(9/10,1/10)$,
giving
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-response}
 E\{\tau(W)\}=4,\qquad
 E\{\tau(W)\mid R=1\}=-\frac{12}{5}.
\end{equation}
Correct conditional response weighting recovers 4 in the exact known law.
At cost USD~2, the population margin is USD~2 and the complete-case margin
is USD~$-22/5$. The example does not claim these are observed package
outputs. It shows why improving a treatment-propensity fit cannot, by
itself, repair a changed marginal population. A missing-data-aware
identification and estimation design is required for the original request.
\cite{exec21_effects_reference}

Case-control sampling is also a distinct design. The inspected TMLE
interface accepts a population prevalence as a scalar and constructs
prevalence weights. Uncertainty in an externally estimated prevalence is
not an input to those inspected interfaces. A banking adapter must therefore
state whether prevalence is assumed fixed or estimated and how its
uncertainty enters a proposed joint analysis. It should not silently reuse
case-control weights for nonresponse, population transport or clustered
sampling, whose information and identifying assumptions differ.
\cite{exec21_effects_tmle}

\subsection{Supplied causal models are valuable public references}
\label{exec21:effects-scm}

CausalTables supplies a useful route to inexpensive comparisons before bank
experiments. Its intervention functions modify treatment, recompute
summaries and draw counterfactual responses from a supplied structural
causal model. Its \texttt{cfmean} and \texttt{cfdiff} functions approximate
intervention means by Monte Carlo. Despite efficiency-bound wording in
their documentation, the inspected return tuples contain only the mean.
A reference-generation service must separately retain the generating
equations, intervention, random seed, Monte Carlo error and dependence
structure. A simulated network summary does not establish a variance formula
for observations from a bank network. \cite{exec21_effects_causaltables}

The distinction between supplied truth and observational identification is
not semantic. Let $U$ be a fair binary variable and consider two structural
models. In both, $A=U$. In the first, $Y=A$; in the second, $Y=U$. Both
produce the same observed law, with probability $1/2$ on each of
$(A,Y)=(0,0)$ and $(1,1)$. Yet the intervention contrast is 1 in the first
model and 0 in the second. A simulator can reproduce either contrast once
its model is supplied. The observed distribution alone does not choose
between them. At an illustrative cost of $1/2$, the corresponding margins
are $1/2$ and $-1/2$. \cite{exec21_effects_reference}

TMLE's CausalTables extension converts treatment columns to categorical
arrays and identifies an estimand using the causes recorded in the supplied
table. This is an explicit and promising connection, provided that level
order, graph semantics and the provenance of the causal declarations survive
conversion. It does not verify that the declared graph is true. The
matching CausalInference revision supplies separately reviewed graphical
interfaces; that evidence should be reused for its established scope rather
than treated as qualification of heterogeneous-effect estimation.
\cite{exec21_effects_tmle,exec19_otherid_julia}

\subsection{A bounded adoption path and the wider language search}
\label{exec21:effects-integration}

The proposed architecture is an isolated Julia worker with a pinned
environment and an explicit estimand/result schema. TMLE is a candidate for
targeted mean estimation and CausalTables for supplied-model reference
generation. A first public comparison should use exact finite distributions
to verify scalar targets, treatment-level order, outcome-unit conversion,
complete-case selection and graph-to-estimand conversion. Subsequent native
trials should compare influence-vector scaling, fold membership, fitted
nuisance behavior and refusal on absent support. CausalELM's selected
uncertainty path needs a justified repair before inclusion; CausalForest's
point predictions need their own inference qualification. Porting either
path before understanding these contracts would copy its unresolved
scientific meaning into a new language.

The library, CLI and MCP representations should carry the action/assignment
distinction, target population, contributing unit IDs, missingness rule,
fitted-model configuration, uncertainty kind and all unavailable results.
An agent can propose mappings and inspect contradictory declarations, but
must leave unsupported identification or uncertainty requests unresolved.
This gives the business user a meaningful answer when a requested population
effect cannot be supplied, and permits genuine comparison of methods that
do answer the same question.

Rust coverage remains incomplete. Separate official repository and crate
queries identify leads including \texttt{perpetual}, \texttt{causalkit},
\texttt{oxicuda-causal} and \texttt{propensity-score}, alongside graph and
event-ordering projects. These are metadata leads; current descriptions are
not proof of functionality at the cutoff. The queries inspect only their
first pages, and an exact treatment-effect phrase query returning zero does
not establish absence. Selected pre-cutoff source revisions, licenses,
estimators and inference paths must be inspected before any capability or
reuse conclusion. Wider language coverage and package-paper originals also
remain to be established. \cite{exec21_effects_discovery}

These public comparisons can determine which Julia integrations merit a
native prototype and which assumptions a later banking experiment must test.
~~~

~~~text
\section{Julia effect estimators and the meaning of a reusable result}
\label{exec21:effects-scope}

A servicing manager asks whether an additional contact is worth its cost for
each risk group. A numerical answer requires more than a treatment-effect
method name. The population average, the average among previously contacted
customers, a conditional contrast and a coefficient from residual regression
can differ even when every conditional model is known. Missing outcomes can
change the averaging population again. A reusable interface must carry this
meaning from the business question through the numerical calculation.

The selected Julia sources offer several distinct building blocks.
CausalELM~0.8.0 supplies g-computation and effect learners; CausalForest~0.1.5
implements tree-based contrasts; TMLE~0.20.5 supplies targeted and one-step
estimation; and CausalTables~1.3.4 represents causal data and interventions in
supplied structural models. Their selected source revisions precede the
7~October~2026 cutoff. Their MIT notices permit substantial reuse subject to
retaining the specified notices, but do not establish dependency clearance or
statistical validity. The following comparison concerns inspected source
paths, not native execution or a tested combination of these packages.
\citep{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_causalforest,exec21_effects_tmle,exec21_effects_causaltables}

\subsection{An average effect is a population-specific quantity}
\label{exec21:effects-target}

Let $W$ denote pre-contact risk information, $A\in\{0,1\}$ the contact action,
and $Y(a)$ the monetary outcome under action $a$. Under consistency,
conditional exchangeability and treatment positivity, write
$e(w)=\Pr(A=1\mid W=w)$ and
$\tau(w)=E\{Y(1)-Y(0)\mid W=w\}$. The population average treatment effect is
$E\{\tau(W)\}$; the treated-population effect is
$E\{\tau(W)\mid A=1\}$. A prediction vector containing estimated
$\tau(W_i)$ is useful, but does not by itself provide uncertainty for a
selected subgroup or a policy chosen using that vector.

In the inspected CausalELM g-formula path, ATE, ITT and CATE labels use
counterfactual predictions at all supplied covariate rows; the other branch
uses treated rows. The g-computation wrapper averages the contrast, while the
S-learner retains a contrast vector. The T-learner fits each treatment arm and
subtracts its predictions on the supplied covariates. Thus an ITT label only
has an assignment-effect interpretation when the supplied treatment actually
represents assignment; it cannot convert receipt of contact into randomized
offer assignment. Categorical coding, the action clock and the averaging
population must be explicit inputs. \citep{exec21_effects_causalelm}

The same source's double-machine-learning path forms outcome and treatment
residuals and averages foldwise least-squares coefficients. Its fold
constructor slices contiguous rows. Neither the name nor contiguous slicing
supplies a dependence argument for repeated customer records. Even with
oracle nuisance functions and independent observations, a partially linear
coefficient need not be the population average under heterogeneous effects.
To see the distinction, take
$Y=m_0(W)+A\tau(W)+\varepsilon$ with
$E(\varepsilon\mid A,W)=0$. If $m(W)=E(Y\mid W)$, then
$Y-m(W)=(A-e(W))\tau(W)+\varepsilon$. Consequently the population residual
coefficient is
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-residual}
 \theta_{\rm res}
 =\frac{E[(A-e(W))(Y-m(W))]}{E[(A-e(W))^2]}
 =\frac{E[e(W)(1-e(W))\tau(W)]}{E[e(W)(1-e(W))]}.
\end{equation}
This is an overlap-weighted average in this model. A constant effect or
additional conditions can make it agree with the ATE; cross-fitting alone
does not do so. This derivation concerns the oracle population target and is
not a claim that a fitted implementation attains it in finite samples.
\citep{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_reference}

Return to the stipulated servicing population used for the R forest in
Section~\ref{exec21:grfr-target}: the two risk groups have equal population
mass, effects $(-4,12)$ dollars and treatment propensities $(1/2,1/10)$.
Using the same law makes the target comparison across languages explicit;
it supplies no independent empirical corroboration. Exact enumeration gives
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-targetexample}
 \tau_{\rm ATE}=4,\qquad
 \tau_{\rm ATT}=-\frac43,\qquad
 \theta_{\rm res}=\frac4{17}.
\end{equation}
At an assumed cost of USD~2 per additional contact, the population margin is
USD~2. Subtracting that cost from the residual coefficient instead gives
$-30/17$ dollars. These quantities answer different questions. A proposed
product should refuse a request to relabel the coefficient as the
population-wide incremental value. A separately evaluated targeted policy
could be more useful, but selecting the profitable subgroup and estimating
its value require their own information and uncertainty contract.
\citep{exec21_effects_reference}

\subsection{Source-level uncertainty checks before adoption}
\label{exec21:effects-uncertainty}

The inspected CausalELM generic null routine samples treatment independently
and uniformly from its distinct observed values, or uniformly over its
observed range for a continuous treatment. This does not preserve the
observed treatment count. It also does not reproduce a nonuniform propensity
law or a cluster-randomized design without additional construction. For four
customers assigned exactly one contact, independent fair binary draws retain
that design with probability $4/16=1/4$ and create an empty treatment or
control arm with probability $2/16=1/8$. This exact calculation identifies an
assignment-law mismatch, rather than estimating a package error rate.
\citep{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_reference}

There is also a direct dimensional problem in the selected source. For null
statistics $z_1,\ldots,z_B$ and reported statistic $t$, its scalar
\texttt{stderr} expression is
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-scale}
 s_{\rm code}=\frac{\sum_{b=1}^{B}(t-z_b)^2}{(B-1)\sqrt B}.
\end{equation}
If $t$ and $z_b$ are monetary effects, this expression has squared monetary
units. Multiplication of all outcomes by a positive constant $c$ multiplies
it by $c^2$, whereas a standard error must scale by $c$. With
$(z_1,z_2,z_3,z_4)=(-1,-1,1,1)$ and $t=2$, the expression is $10/3$.
Changing dollars to cents multiplies it by 10,000 rather than 100. This is a
source-level defect in the expression at the selected revision. It does not
require a Julia run to establish the algebra, and it does not establish the
finite-sample behavior of every other package function.
\citep{exec21_effects_causalelm,exec21_effects_reference}

Taking a square root in one location would not settle the larger inference
question. The sampled null law, the centering, the effect being tested and
the relationship between repeated randomization draws and sampling
uncertainty would still need justification. The inspected interval routine
searches a finite grid with tail comparisons against the same null draws;
that code alone is insufficient to claim a valid general confidence-interval
inversion. The proposed reuse decision is therefore to reject unqualified
reuse of this uncertainty path, while retaining the point-estimation family
as a candidate for a separately justified implementation.
\citep{exec21_effects_causalelm}

CausalForest illustrates a different boundary. Its interface exposes
bootstrap or subsampling choices and separate split and leaf samples under
the honest-tree option. However, its outcome-centering forest is fitted
before these per-tree splits. The inspected prediction paths form
within-leaf treated and control weights and return centered-outcome
contrasts. They return point predictions, rather than a calibrated covariance
object. Honesty of the tree split is consequently not sufficient evidence
that all nuisance fitting is independent of evaluation, nor evidence of
equivalence to the separately reviewed \texttt{grf} implementation. A native
comparison should examine the actual nuisance training rows, treatment
coding, arm support in leaves, random-number state and failure behavior
before considering any shared inference contract.
\citep{exec21_effects_causalforest}

\subsection{Targeted estimation, missingness and influence scales}
\label{exec21:effects-tmle}

TMLE exposes nuisance-model choices, outer resampling and propensity
truncation. The selected noncollaborative default uses no outer resampling;
a requested cross-validation design must be supplied and preserved. The
implementation's \texttt{weighted} constructor default is true although
adjacent prose says false, another reason to record actual resolved
configuration. Truncation prevents numerical division by a very small
estimated probability but cannot create absent treatment support. For an
identified ATE in an independent sampling model, the familiar influence
expression clarifies what an adapter must carry:
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-influence}
\begin{aligned}
 D_i={}&\frac{A_i}{e(W_i)}\{Y_i-m_1(W_i)\}
       -\frac{1-A_i}{1-e(W_i)}\{Y_i-m_0(W_i)\}\\
       &+m_1(W_i)-m_0(W_i)-\psi .
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The relevant source combines outcome-residual and counterfactual-mean
components. It stores the standard deviation of the estimated influence
values together with their number, and passes both to the testing
interface. This scale is not already the standard error of the effect.
Conditional asymptotic inference additionally needs suitable nuisance
remainder control, positivity, moments and a sampling-law argument; the
presence of an influence vector does not prove those conditions.
\citep{exec21_effects_tmle}

For example, influence values $(-3,-1,1,3)$ have sample variance $20/3$.
Under the independent mean calculation, the variance of the average is
$5/3$, since $n=4$. Exporting the influence standard deviation as an effect
standard error would miss the factor $\sqrt n$. Conversely, dividing a
quantity that is already a standard error by $\sqrt n$ again would understate
uncertainty. For joint outputs, the source stacks influence vectors and
computes their covariance. A bridge must preserve the identity and order
of contributing customers; equal vector length is insufficient when
different outcomes select different complete cases. Cluster or calendar
dependence requires an appropriate covariance construction, not a new field
name on the independent calculation. \citep{exec21_effects_tmle,exec21_effects_reference}

The missingness documentation is explicit: rows missing variables needed
for estimation are dropped. Without cross-validation, propensity fitting
may use additional records with observed treatment and confounders even
when outcomes are missing. The source's final fluctuation dataset nevertheless
uses complete relevant records. This is useful data reuse, but is not a
general missing-outcome correction. \citep{exec21_effects_tmle}

Let $R$ indicate observation of the outcome. Consider independent customers
with equally frequent risk groups, randomized contact probability $1/2$,
effects $(-4,12)$ and outcome-response probabilities $(9/10,1/10)$ by risk
group. Suppose response is independent of potential outcomes and contact
conditional on risk, so missing at random holds in this example and response
positivity holds. The complete cases have group probabilities $(9/10,1/10)$,
giving
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:effects-response}
 E\{\tau(W)\}=4,\qquad
 E\{\tau(W)\mid R=1\}=-\frac{12}{5}.
\end{equation}
Correct conditional response weighting recovers 4 in the exact known law.
At cost USD~2, the population margin is USD~2 and the complete-case margin
is USD~$-22/5$. The example does not claim these are observed package
outputs. It shows why improving a treatment-propensity fit cannot, by
itself, repair a changed marginal population. A missing-data-aware
identification and estimation design is required for the original request.
\citep{exec21_effects_reference}

Case-control sampling is also a distinct design. The inspected TMLE
interface accepts a population prevalence as a scalar and constructs
prevalence weights. Uncertainty in an externally estimated prevalence is
not an input to those inspected interfaces. A banking adapter must therefore
state whether prevalence is assumed fixed or estimated and how its
uncertainty enters a proposed joint analysis. It should not silently reuse
case-control weights for nonresponse, population transport or clustered
sampling, whose information and identifying assumptions differ.
\citep{exec21_effects_tmle}

\subsection{Supplied causal models are valuable public references}
\label{exec21:effects-scm}
~~~

~~~text
\chapter{Design-based estimation, weak information and diagnostics}
\label{sw:design}

The preceding comparisons establish why a common interface must preserve
design-specific meanings. This chapter gathers their consequences for the
analyst's report: state the instrument claim, retain the complete uncertainty
set, distinguish threshold designs and show what a sensitivity parameter does
and does not reveal. The detailed implementation findings remain in the
preceding chapter; the purpose here is to connect them to the bank's decision.

\section{An instrument is a scientific claim}

Suppose a bank uses a rule-based funding incentive \(Z\) as an instrument for
an offered rate \(A\), with retained balances \(Y\) as the outcome and baseline
controls \(W\). Software can encode a regression system, but it cannot establish
that the incentive has no direct effect on balances or that its assignment is
independent of omitted balance determinants. Those are properties of the
economic setting and information process. The package comparison begins after
the instrument, target population, timing and exclusion claim have been stated.

The selected \texttt{ivreg} documentation describes ordinary two-stage least
squares and robust M/MM regression choices. Exogenous regressors in the outcome
equation must also appear among the instruments. An adapter should therefore
retain explicit endogenous, exogenous and instrument column roles rather than
constructing the formula from a single list of predictors. The robust regression
option is an estimator choice; it does not supply a weak-instrument confidence
guarantee. The inspected package is ivreg 0.6-8. The separate AER implementation
is compared in Section~\ref{exec22:riv-inference}; shared function names do not
make the engines interchangeable. That bounded review does not qualify the
whole AER family. \cite{v12_sw_ivreg}

Weak information changes the result contract. The inspected \texttt{ivmodel}
documentation exposes Anderson--Rubin and conditional-likelihood-ratio routines
whose confidence-set outputs are matrices of interval components. A client
that extracts one lower and one upper endpoint can lose disconnectedness or
unboundedness. The appropriate object is
\[
 C=\bigcup_{j=1}^{J} I_j,
\]
where each component \(I_j\) retains its endpoints, possible infinite bounds and
method-specific interpretation. The number of components is part of the result,
not formatting metadata. \cite{v12_sw_ivmodel}

For illustration, a hypothetical reported set
\(C=(-\infty,-2]\cup[3,\infty)\) excludes zero while remaining unbounded in both
directions. Its interval hull is the whole real line. Replacing the set with
that hull changes the question ``is zero in the set?'' and replacing it with a
midpoint invents a finite estimate. Conversely, an empty inversion result is
not equivalent to a missing calculation. The proposed result schema must
preserve these distinctions before any banking decision rule uses the output.
This hypothetical set is not a computed result from ivmodel.

The two R packages are therefore complementary candidates. ivreg supplies a
regression interface with specific estimation and diagnostic choices; ivmodel
supplies selected weak-information test/inversion interfaces. A matched trial
must align instruments, controls, scaling, sampling assumptions and covariance
choices. It must retain weak and zero first stages, singular designs and
unbounded sets. A regression returning quickly on a well-conditioned example
would not qualify the weak-information case, and the presence of an inversion
API would not establish coverage for dependent bank panels.
The source-level CLR failure substitution described in
Section~\ref{exec22:riv-inference} also remains a condition on reuse:
a numerical failure must not be reported as scientific rejection merely
because the returned p-value is zero.

\section{Regression discontinuity is a family of designs}

A credit policy can change at a score cutoff, but several causal questions can
be attached to that threshold. A sharp design studies a deterministic assignment
change. A fuzzy design uses a discontinuity in treatment probability. A kink
design studies a change in slope. These require different estimands and
denominators. Treating them as one generic local regression would make an
apparently comparable benchmark compare different scientific problems.

The inspected rdrobust 4.0.0 documentation exposes sharp, fuzzy and kink
settings, local-polynomial choices, bias correction, bandwidth choices and
variance options. The adapter should bind the cutoff and running-variable
units to the result and export the actual bandwidth and inference settings.
The bandwidth for a point-estimation objective and the choices underlying a
bias-corrected interval cannot be silently conflated. The saved 4.0.0 snapshot
predates the literature cutoff. Section~\ref{exec22:riv-scope} explains why
the subsequently authenticated CRAN publication time retains that distribution
for the client cutoff; it does not settle every upstream channel's history.
\cite{v12_sw_rdrobust}

For a fuzzy design, the treatment jump matters as much as the outcome jump.
If a local effect is expressed schematically as \(\tau_Y/\tau_A\), a small
\(\tau_A\) produces an unstable ratio even when each fitted regression has a
clean numerical convergence report. The bank's first question should be
whether the score rule changes the specified treatment in the relevant
population. The software trial should vary that first-stage jump and preserve
weak cases rather than selecting only a strong demonstration.

rddensity supplies a different operation: local-polynomial density or
manipulation diagnostics near the cutoff. Such diagnostics can challenge a
design, but failure to reject a discontinuity does not verify the assignment
mechanism or continuity of potential outcomes. An interface that displays
``RD valid'' after a density test would convert one diagnostic into a much
stronger scientific statement. A useful report instead gives the test,
bandwidth, support and interpretation limit alongside other design evidence.
Discrete scores and mass points require explicit attention. \cite{v12_sw_rddensity}

rdlocrand represents another approach, based on a declared local-randomization
window. Its selected documentation states that a positive polynomial order
uses asymptotic inference and that confidence-set inversion is performed over
a supplied grid. Thus a finite grid endpoint is not automatically a finite
confidence bound. If all evaluated values near the boundary remain accepted,
the result should say that the search is truncated. Extending the grid is a
computational remedy; it is not evidence for the local-randomization assumption.
Window selection, assignment probabilities and the intervention must still be
recorded. \cite{v12_sw_rdlocrand}

These packages should be evaluated as a collection of design-specific
operations. The proposed integration uses a common report for the score,
cutoff, assignment rule and target, then separate method records. The product
should not expose a single switch called ``robust RD'' that hides whether the
calculation is continuity-based local-polynomial inference or local-randomization
inference. The alternatives may both be useful, but they answer their questions
under different assumptions.

\section{Sensitivity parameters are not missing truth}

Two other R interfaces provide useful sensitivity summaries. sensemakr
parameterizes omitted-variable sensitivity through partial \(R^2\) quantities
and observed-covariate benchmarks. A large benchmark asks how conclusions would
change if an omitted variable had specified residual associations; it does not
measure the actual association of an unobserved variable. The bank must identify
what makes a proposed benchmark plausible, and the report should retain both
the chosen benchmark and the effect scale. \cite{v12_sw_sensemakr}

The selected EValue \texttt{evalues.RR} interface takes a risk ratio and optional
confidence limits and reports E-values for the point and the near-null confidence
limit. This is a particular scale-specific sensitivity operation. A default
probability difference, a standardized regression coefficient and a net present
value should not be passed to it as if they were risk ratios. Any conversion
needs an explicit target and assumptions. An E-value does not estimate a
confounding correction or certify an absence of confounding. \cite{v12_sw_evalue}

These tools can make a funding or credit recommendation more intelligible:
they expose how much an assumption would need to change before a conclusion
changes. They cannot supply verified instrument exclusion, known ownership,
correct record inclusion or a gold audit label. Their adoption is therefore
conditional on a result contract that separates observed data summaries,
user-supplied sensitivity assumptions and causal conclusions.
~~~

~~~text
regression interface with specific estimation and diagnostic choices; ivmodel
supplies selected weak-information test/inversion interfaces. A matched trial
must align instruments, controls, scaling, sampling assumptions and covariance
choices. It must retain weak and zero first stages, singular designs and
unbounded sets. A regression returning quickly on a well-conditioned example
would not qualify the weak-information case, and the presence of an inversion
API would not establish coverage for dependent bank panels.
The source-level CLR failure substitution described in
Section~\ref{exec22:riv-inference} also remains a condition on reuse:
a numerical failure must not be reported as scientific rejection merely
because the returned p-value is zero.

\section{Regression discontinuity is a family of designs}

A credit policy can change at a score cutoff, but several causal questions can
be attached to that threshold. A sharp design studies a deterministic assignment
change. A fuzzy design uses a discontinuity in treatment probability. A kink
design studies a change in slope. These require different estimands and
denominators. Treating them as one generic local regression would make an
apparently comparable benchmark compare different scientific problems.

The inspected rdrobust 4.0.0 documentation exposes sharp, fuzzy and kink
settings, local-polynomial choices, bias correction, bandwidth choices and
variance options. The adapter should bind the cutoff and running-variable
units to the result and export the actual bandwidth and inference settings.
The bandwidth for a point-estimation objective and the choices underlying a
bias-corrected interval cannot be silently conflated. The saved 4.0.0 snapshot
predates the literature cutoff. Section~\ref{exec22:riv-scope} explains why
the subsequently authenticated CRAN publication time retains that distribution
for the client cutoff; it does not settle every upstream channel's history.
\citep{v12_sw_rdrobust}

For a fuzzy design, the treatment jump matters as much as the outcome jump.
If a local effect is expressed schematically as \(\tau_Y/\tau_A\), a small
\(\tau_A\) produces an unstable ratio even when each fitted regression has a
clean numerical convergence report. The bank's first question should be
whether the score rule changes the specified treatment in the relevant
population. The software trial should vary that first-stage jump and preserve
weak cases rather than selecting only a strong demonstration.

rddensity supplies a different operation: local-polynomial density or
manipulation diagnostics near the cutoff. Such diagnostics can challenge a
design, but failure to reject a discontinuity does not verify the assignment
mechanism or continuity of potential outcomes. An interface that displays
``RD valid'' after a density test would convert one diagnostic into a much
stronger scientific statement. A useful report instead gives the test,
bandwidth, support and interpretation limit alongside other design evidence.
Discrete scores and mass points require explicit attention. \citep{v12_sw_rddensity}

rdlocrand represents another approach, based on a declared local-randomization
window. Its selected documentation states that a positive polynomial order
uses asymptotic inference and that confidence-set inversion is performed over
a supplied grid. Thus a finite grid endpoint is not automatically a finite
confidence bound. If all evaluated values near the boundary remain accepted,
the result should say that the search is truncated. Extending the grid is a
computational remedy; it is not evidence for the local-randomization assumption.
Window selection, assignment probabilities and the intervention must still be
recorded. \citep{v12_sw_rdlocrand}

These packages should be evaluated as a collection of design-specific
operations. The proposed integration uses a common report for the score,
cutoff, assignment rule and target, then separate method records. The product
should not expose a single switch called ``robust RD'' that hides whether the
calculation is continuity-based local-polynomial inference or local-randomization
inference. The alternatives may both be useful, but they answer their questions
under different assumptions.

\section{Sensitivity parameters are not missing truth}

Two other R interfaces provide useful sensitivity summaries. sensemakr
parameterizes omitted-variable sensitivity through partial \(R^2\) quantities
and observed-covariate benchmarks. A large benchmark asks how conclusions would
change if an omitted variable had specified residual associations; it does not
measure the actual association of an unobserved variable. The bank must identify
what makes a proposed benchmark plausible, and the report should retain both
the chosen benchmark and the effect scale. \citep{v12_sw_sensemakr}

The selected EValue \texttt{evalues.RR} interface takes a risk ratio and optional
confidence limits and reports E-values for the point and the near-null confidence
limit. This is a particular scale-specific sensitivity operation. A default
probability difference, a standardized regression coefficient and a net present
value should not be passed to it as if they were risk ratios. Any conversion
needs an explicit target and assumptions. An E-value does not estimate a
confounding correction or certify an absence of confounding. \citep{v12_sw_evalue}

These tools can make a funding or credit recommendation more intelligible:
they expose how much an assumption would need to change before a conclusion
changes. They cannot supply verified instrument exclusion, known ownership,
correct record inclusion or a gold audit label. Their adoption is therefore
conditional on a result contract that separates observed data summaries,
user-supplied sensitivity assumptions and causal conclusions.
~~~

~~~text
\section[Python mediation and sensitivity contracts]{Python mediation and sensitivity: effects, resampling, and the limits of computed bounds}
\label{sec:exec25-pymed}

A bank evaluating a digital servicing channel needs more than a coefficient labelled indirect effect. Let $A$ denote the assigned channel, $M$ a subsequent customer response, $X$ information fixed before assignment, and $Y$ the net outcome over a specified horizon. A response measured after a collection decision cannot silently become a baseline adjustment variable. The proposal therefore treats the observation clock, intervention, population and outcome scale as part of the request. A package comparison is useful only after these quantities have been fixed. The Python ecosystem supplies several different services: regression path summaries, model-based potential-outcome integration, graph-guided orchestration, and partial-identification calculations. They should be compared at the level of their estimands and failure conditions rather than placed behind one interchangeable mediation command. \cite{exec25_pymed_statsmodels,exec25_pymed_pingouin,exec25_pymed_dowhy,exec25_pymed_process,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{An estimand contract before a package choice}
Write $M(a)$ for the mediator under assignment $a$, and $Y(a,m)$ for the outcome under joint interventions. A useful nested mean is
\[
 \theta(a,a')=E\{Y(a,M(a'))\}.
\]
For a binary contrast, $\theta(1,1)-\theta(1,0)$ is one natural indirect effect and $\theta(1,0)-\theta(0,0)$ its complementary direct effect. Their sum is the total effect, provided these nested quantities are justified. The corresponding definitions with the outcome exposure fixed at zero need not give the same indirect effect when there is interaction. Identification additionally requires a defensible causal model, consistency, overlap, and the relevant treatment--mediator and mediator--outcome exchangeability or stronger cross-world restrictions. Neither a fitted path nor a sensitivity plot certifies those assumptions. The preceding R comparison develops alternative interventional targets; the Python interface must retain that distinction. \cite{exec25_pymed_choice,exec25_pymed_sharp_paper}

For a concrete scale check, suppose $P\{M(0)=1\}=1/4$, $P\{M(1)=1\}=3/4$, and $E\{Y(a,m)\}=3a+4m$. Under an otherwise identified law, either natural indirect effect is $4(3/4-1/4)=2$. A logistic regression of the binary mediator on $A$ instead has slope $\log 9$; multiplying this slope by the outcome coefficient gives $4\log9\simeq8.789$. This is a coefficient product on mixed scales, not the same outcome contrast. Reporting the latter number as the former would change the business quantity before any uncertainty calculation begins. This is an authored population example, not an observed error rate from a fitted package. \cite{exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{Potential-outcome simulation versus regression paths}
The inspected statsmodels mediation implementation is a useful reference for explicit counterfactual construction. Its formula route rebuilds the design matrix after exposure and mediator interventions; this matters for interaction terms. Its matrix route directly replaces columns and cannot be assumed to rebuild derived interaction columns. It simulates mediator draws, uses the same mediator realization for both outcome-exposure levels within a given mediator assignment, and computes direct and indirect differences. The selected loops fix assignments to zero and one, so another treatment contrast requires an explicit transformation or an extended implementation. \cite{exec25_pymed_statsmodels}

Uncertainty requires a separate review. The parametric route draws mediator and outcome parameters in separate calls. The bootstrap route calls a fitting helper twice; each call generates its own row indices. These operations do not preserve arbitrary cross-equation dependence merely because both equations originated in one customer table. An exact toy calculation makes the distinction visible. Draw two values with replacement from $\{-1,1\}$, and let $Z^*$ be their mean. Its distribution is $(-1,0,1)$ with probabilities $(1/4,1/2,1/4)$. If two fitted components share this perturbation, $E[(Z^*)^2]=1/2$ and $\operatorname{Var}(2Z^*)=2$. Independently drawing the components gives product expectation zero and sum variance one. This does not prove that every statsmodels mediation interval is invalid: joint covariance can vanish under additional model conditions. It proves that a shared-resample contract and a separate-resample contract need a stated justification. Customer, household and calendar dependence introduce further choices that an ordinary row bootstrap does not settle. \cite{exec25_pymed_statsmodels,exec25_pymed_choice}

Two smaller boundaries also deserve tests before exposure through a product interface. Initial method validation accepts a prefix beginning with \texttt{para}, whereas the iteration dispatch selects parameter simulation only for the exact string \texttt{parametric}; accepting a prefix can consequently change the computational route. The sign-tail expression for reported significance counts strict positive and strict negative draws. With four exact zeros its arithmetic returns zero, which is not evidence against the null. The proportion-mediated calculation also divides by the total effect without defining an economic interpretation when that total is zero or changes sign. We would wrap a narrowly qualified version with exact enumerations, typed zero-total results and an explicit sampling contract, rather than infer qualification from the API name. \cite{exec25_pymed_statsmodels,exec25_pymed_choice}

Pingouin exposes a compact parallel-mediation workflow. The inspected implementation drops incomplete rows, detects binary mediators and uses logistic mediator regressions for them, fits a linear outcome model, and returns products of the mediator and outcome coefficients. The source therefore directly motivates the mixed-scale example above. Its bootstrap applies one row-index array to the mediator and outcome inputs, preserving the sampled row pairing. Its bias-corrected interval is explicitly not an accelerated BCa interval, and its documentation warns that bootstrap sign tails are not a null-conditioned permutation test. The zero-point-estimate branch returns a significance value of one, unlike the strict-sign expression just discussed. Moreover, the displayed $Y\sim M+X$ path omits treatment while the coefficient used for the indirect product comes from the full treatment-and-mediator outcome model; the product should not be reconstructed by multiplying arbitrary displayed rows. \cite{exec25_pymed_pingouin}

For continuous linear models with appropriately justified assumptions, Pingouin can be a conditional comparator. For binary mediators it should be presented as a regression-path summary unless a separate target derivation is supplied. Complete-case selection changes the analyzed population and requires its own missingness argument. Multiple mediator coefficients do not establish a causal ordering, joint intervention or absence of mediator--outcome confounding. These distinctions justify retaining the package as a candidate while rejecting a universal causal-effect interpretation. \cite{exec25_pymed_pingouin,exec25_pymed_choice}

The selected PyProcessMacro revision extends this regression-path family with moderated and serial constructions, categorical coding and outcome-family choices. Its serial path calculation multiplies coefficients along each ordered path. The bootstrap code fits the equations on the same resampled rows and retains a count of failed fits. It continues until the requested number of successful resamples is obtained, with an explicit failure cap. This is useful instrumentation, but the retained draws are conditional on successful fitting. A later public evaluation must report attempted, failed and accepted draws, separation and rank failures, and the effect of those failures on the reported interval. A request that produces enough successful draws has not thereby shown that discarded draws are ignorable. We would adopt the failure-accounting and shared-resample interface ideas, compare serial linear paths under an exact graph, and require a separate causal derivation for nonlinear coefficient products. \cite{exec25_pymed_process,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{Graph orchestration does not make all two-stage estimates equivalent}
DoWhy supplies graph-oriented identification and estimator orchestration. In the inspected mediation branch, variables on directed paths are collected and separate adjustment searches are performed for the treatment--mediator and mediator--outcome stages. These are valuable, inspectable steps. The selected two-stage estimator nevertheless restricts treatment and mediator dimensions, defaults to linear regression stages, multiplies the two estimated effects, and obtains a direct effect by subtracting that product from an estimated total. The request must therefore bind the graph assumptions to this estimator's actual algebra. An identifier returning a mediation expression is not certification of arbitrary nonlinear mediation. \cite{exec25_pymed_dowhy}

A nonunit contrast exposes an especially concrete interface issue. Suppose $M=2A+\varepsilon_M$ and $Y=3A+4M+\varepsilon_Y$, with suitable independent, mean-zero errors and an identified continuous treatment. Changing $A$ from zero to two gives an indirect effect $2\cdot4\cdot2=16$ and direct effect six. The selected source forwards the same control and treatment values to both stages. Their regression intervention contrasts are therefore four and eight; multiplying gives 32. Subtracting that from the total contrast 22 gives $-10$. The intended unit mediator slope has been replaced by a second nonunit contrast. The source route agrees with the product formula for a unit contrast under its linear restrictions; other contrasts need correction or explicit refusal. This is exact algebra for the inspected calls, not a native execution result. \cite{exec25_pymed_dowhy,exec25_pymed_choice}

DoWhy also offers distinct sensitivity analyzers rather than one universal sensitivity parameter. The inspected linear analyzer uses partial $R^2$ values and a bias factor proportional to
\[
 \sqrt{\frac{R^2_{Y\sim U\mid A,X}R^2_{A\sim U\mid X}}
 {1-R^2_{A\sim U\mid X}}}.
\]
The partially linear and nonparametric analyzers involve different scores, variance quantities and learner conditions. Its E-value path dispatches by outcome family and link; the logistic route chooses a rare-outcome approximation using an observed outcome-frequency threshold of 0.15. These contracts must remain separately named, with domains, scales, estimands and nuisance assumptions recorded. A generic refutation score should not be translated into an identified mediation effect or probability that the graph is true. Remaining learner delegates and validity arguments require their own inspection and public qualification. \cite{exec25_pymed_dowhy,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{Sensitivity bounds with explicit mathematical scope}
The generalized marginal sensitivity model of Frauen, Melnychuk and Feuerriegel is particularly relevant to channel pathways. It bounds the change in the latent-confounder distribution between conditioning on treatment and intervening. For each relevant mediator or outcome node $W$, the selected paper uses bounds $s_W^-\leq1\leq s_W^+$ on a density ratio. Its framework allows a sequence of discrete mediators, but excludes additional unobserved confounding between mediators and the outcome for the stated path-effect interpretation. For a weighted construction, a sensitivity parameter $\Gamma_W\geq1$ and weight $q_W(a,x)\in[0,1]$ give
\[
 s_W^-=\frac{1}{(1-\Gamma_W)q_W+\Gamma_W},\qquad
 s_W^+=\frac{1}{(1-\Gamma_W^{-1})q_W+\Gamma_W^{-1}}.
\]
The binary-treatment specialization uses the observed treatment probability; the continuous specialization has $q_W=0$. These are restrictions on a possible data-generating law, not fitted probabilities that confounding is small. \cite{exec25_pymed_sharp_paper}

The paper derives extremal distribution shifts and a backward recursion over ordered mediator states. Sharpness has additional conditions: the intervention must leave the relevant latent distribution equal to its baseline conditional distribution, with the specified discrete-treatment feasibility condition or continuous treatment. The paper explicitly excludes its time-varying extension from that sharpness conclusion. Its inclusion of a longitudinal sensitivity model must therefore not become a claim of generally sharp longitudinal bounds. Nor does a weighted average of stratum-specific quantiles automatically equal the quantile of the mixed outcome distribution; the functional in the request must match the paper's definition. \cite{exec25_pymed_sharp_paper}

The selected SharpCausalSensitivity source implements binary, continuous and weighted variants, mediator-state enumeration, Monte Carlo outcome calculations and bootstrap ensembles. It also contains important boundaries for any port. The discrete mass-shift branch uses strict comparisons around a cumulative-probability threshold, with separate handling of the lagged cumulative probability. Take $\Gamma=3$ in the continuous-treatment sensitivity specialization, so $s^-=1/3$, $s^+=3$, and $c^+=3/4$. For an ordered binary mediator with probabilities $(3/4,1/4)$, the first category lands exactly on the threshold and satisfies none of the selected positive-mass conditions. The expressions then produce masses $(0,3/4)$, and the source's normalization assertion should reject them. The mathematical split at this boundary gives $(1/4,3/4)$. The assertion is protective; the missing equality case still needs reconciliation. \cite{exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_choice}

The finite-sample continuous-mean routine also merits an explicit quadrature check. With four sample values, $\Gamma=2$, and the branch without a fitted outcome-mean regression, the cumulative sums include the index obtained by flooring the quantile position. For four unit values, the selected upper expression gives $7/8$ and the lower expression $5/4$, although a normalized weighted constant must remain one. This fixed-array diagnostic identifies a normalization and fractional-boundary requirement; it does not estimate how often trained neural distributions encounter such a configuration. Continuous integration should be checked against exact finite-support or analytic oracles, including translation of the outcome, small sample counts, ties and $\Gamma=1$. Selected binary-outcome paths explicitly raise \texttt{NotImplementedError}. We would retain the mathematical recursion as a design reference and defer this implementation's automatic admission pending these repairs and version-specific tests. \cite{exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{Neural bounds need both a theorem and numerical certificates}
NeuralCSA generalizes the sensitivity family using two conditional normalizing flows. The retained primary paper assumes independent observations, consistency, positivity and latent unconfoundedness. Its key sufficiency theorem concerns a transformation-invariant generalized treatment sensitivity model, an invertible map that exactly reproduces the observational outcome distribution, and optimization over the full admissible distribution class. That result motivates the two-stage construction. It does not say that any fitted flow, finite Monte Carlo sample and stopped optimizer produces certified extrema. \cite{exec25_pymed_neural_paper}

The selected implementation samples latent variables, evaluates sensitivity constraints and updates a constrained objective using multipliers and a penalty. For supremum-type restrictions, sampled extrema can miss a small violating set. If a violation occupies probability 0.001 and 100 independent probes are drawn, the probability of seeing none is $0.999^{100}\simeq0.905$. This is an authored diagnostic, not an assessment of the paper's experiments. A feasible maximization iterate is generally a lower bound on the optimal maximum, not by itself a valid upper bound on the true causal query. A proposal for certified output therefore needs a feasibility assessment, a justified optimization-gap bound or analytic outer envelope, and a separate account of density-estimation and sampling uncertainty. \cite{exec25_pymed_neuralcsa,exec25_pymed_neural_paper,exec25_pymed_choice}

The source inspection also finds that a public sensitivity-check helper calls a three-argument sensitivity method with only the training batch and latent sample; both selected subclass definitions require the additional mixture sample. That static signature mismatch is a focused candidate for a native regression test, not evidence about every training route. Continuous Rosenbaum sensitivity is explicitly unimplemented. No license-named file was found in the complete selected NeuralCSA and SharpCausalSensitivity trees; publication of source does not settle permission to copy or redistribute it. We would compare their methods and seek artifact-specific rights and delegate completion before code adoption or a port. \cite{exec25_pymed_neuralcsa,exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_choice}

Overcast supplies a contrasting continuous-treatment workflow. The retained Jesson and colleagues preprint bounds a treatment density ratio rather than inserting a density into the binary-treatment odds-ratio formula. Its consistency theorem requires convergence of the fitted outcome density and conditional mean, increasing Monte Carlo effort and a positivity condition; its uncertainty section proposes bootstrap intervals for the bound endpoints. The retained version is the April 2022 author original. A later title and author list in the repository README are not substituted for that original. \cite{exec25_pymed_overcast_paper,exec25_pymed_overcast}

In the selected continuous-treatment source, the sensitivity input is logarithmic, the code exponentiates it, and an epsilon regularizes the expression near the no-confounding boundary. It forms pairwise differences between outcome draws to evaluate threshold candidates. The intermediate array has order $m^2bd$ values for $m$ draws, batch size $b$ and $d$ outcomes. For $m=1000$, $b=256$, $d=1$ and four-byte values, one such array alone is 1,024,000,000 bytes, before masks and other working arrays. This is a storage calculation, not a measured platform limit. A sorted cumulative-sum alternative is a possible port only after tie handling, centering, endpoint semantics and numerical parity have been demonstrated. The method is a conditional dose-response candidate, not a substitute for a longitudinal policy or mediation model. \cite{exec25_pymed_overcast,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{A public qualification sequence with economic consequences}
The package decisions imply independent, executable prototype questions. First, freeze a graph, target, contrast, units, missingness policy and observation clock. Use public synthetic laws with known nested means, including binary mediators, interaction, nonunit treatments and exposure-induced confounding. Compare a regression-path service and a potential-outcome service only where their assumptions imply the same target. Next, enumerate small resampling distributions and finite-support sensitivity sets to check shared-row behavior, failed-fit denominators, probability normalization, ties, contrast scaling and explicit refusal. Neural candidates additionally require held-out density diagnostics, sensitivity feasibility, increasing integration effort, repeated optimization and an external bound oracle. Dependent customer and calendar experiments form a distinct qualification branch. \cite{exec25_pymed_choice}

The same interface must preserve the economics of uncertainty. Suppose, purely for illustration, that a proposed intervention serves 10,000 customers, its benefit lies between USD 2 and USD 8 per customer under the declared sensitivity model, and delivery costs USD 5 per customer. The resulting net interval is USD $[-30{,}000,30{,}000]$. Neither the positive endpoint nor a narrower optimizer output establishes a profitable decision. Further information is valuable only if its expected decision benefit justifies acquisition, computation and review costs. Public known-law fixtures can establish whether the proposed interface preserves this reasoning before expensive bank data work is commissioned. \cite{exec25_pymed_choice}

Accordingly, statsmodels and DoWhy are conditional integration candidates with explicit counterfactual and contrast wrappers; Pingouin and PyProcessMacro are useful regression-path comparators with restricted causal interpretations; SharpCausalSensitivity and NeuralCSA require the stated mathematical, numerical and rights conditions before implementation admission; and Overcast is a continuous-dose sensitivity candidate with a measurable integration-cost question. Selected BSD-style, MIT, GPL and Apache declarations must be assessed together with the actual artifacts and dependency chain; no umbrella rights clearance is inferred. Search tails, negative-control and path-specific alternatives, installed-artifact validation and public native trials remain open. The proposed product should expose an informative refusal whenever a request exceeds the admitted target and sensitivity qualification. \cite{exec25_pymed_statsmodels,exec25_pymed_pingouin,exec25_pymed_dowhy,exec25_pymed_process,exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_neuralcsa,exec25_pymed_overcast,exec25_pymed_choice}
~~~
