The request must therefore bind the graph assumptions to this estimator's actual algebra. An identifier returning a mediation expression is not certification of arbitrary nonlinear mediation. \citep{exec25_pymed_dowhy}

A nonunit contrast exposes an especially concrete interface issue. Suppose $M=2A+\varepsilon_M$ and $Y=3A+4M+\varepsilon_Y$, with suitable independent, mean-zero errors and an identified continuous treatment. Changing $A$ from zero to two gives an indirect effect $2\cdot4\cdot2=16$ and direct effect six. The selected source forwards the same control and treatment values to both stages. Their regression intervention contrasts are therefore four and eight; multiplying gives 32. Subtracting that from the total contrast 22 gives $-10$. The intended unit mediator slope has been replaced by a second nonunit contrast. The source route agrees with the product formula for a unit contrast under its linear restrictions; other contrasts need correction or explicit refusal. This is exact algebra for the inspected calls, not a native execution result. \citep{exec25_pymed_dowhy,exec25_pymed_choice}

DoWhy also offers distinct sensitivity analyzers rather than one universal sensitivity parameter. The inspected linear analyzer uses partial $R^2$ values and a bias factor proportional to
\[
 \sqrt{\frac{R^2_{Y\sim U\mid A,X}R^2_{A\sim U\mid X}}
 {1-R^2_{A\sim U\mid X}}}.
\]
The partially linear and nonparametric analyzers involve different scores, variance quantities and learner conditions. Its E-value path dispatches by outcome family and link; the logistic route chooses a rare-outcome approximation using an observed outcome-frequency threshold of 0.15. These contracts must remain separately named, with domains, scales, estimands and nuisance assumptions recorded. A generic refutation score should not be translated into an identified mediation effect or probability that the graph is true. Remaining learner delegates and validity arguments require their own inspection and public qualification. \citep{exec25_pymed_dowhy,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{Sensitivity bounds with explicit mathematical scope}
The generalized marginal sensitivity model of Frauen, Melnychuk and Feuerriegel is particularly relevant to channel pathways. It bounds the change in the latent-confounder distribution between conditioning on treatment and intervening. For each relevant mediator or outcome node $W$, the selected paper uses bounds $s_W^-\leq1\leq s_W^+$ on a density ratio. Its framework allows a sequence of discrete mediators, but excludes additional unobserved confounding between mediators and the outcome for the stated path-effect interpretation. For a weighted construction, a sensitivity parameter $\Gamma_W\geq1$ and weight $q_W(a,x)\in[0,1]$ give
\[
 s_W^-=\frac{1}{(1-\Gamma_W)q_W+\Gamma_W},\qquad
 s_W^+=\frac{1}{(1-\Gamma_W^{-1})q_W+\Gamma_W^{-1}}.
\]
The binary-treatment specialization uses the observed treatment probability; the continuous specialization has $q_W=0$. These are restrictions on a possible data-generating law, not fitted probabilities that confounding is small. \citep{exec25_pymed_sharp_paper}

The paper derives extremal distribution shifts and a backward recursion over ordered mediator states. Sharpness has additional conditions: the intervention must leave the relevant latent distribution equal to its baseline conditional distribution, with the specified discrete-treatment feasibility condition or continuous treatment. The paper explicitly excludes its time-varying extension from that sharpness conclusion. Its inclusion of a longitudinal sensitivity model must therefore not become a claim of generally sharp longitudinal bounds. Nor does a weighted average of stratum-specific quantiles automatically equal the quantile of the mixed outcome distribution; the functional in the request must match the paper's definition. \citep{exec25_pymed_sharp_paper}

The selected SharpCausalSensitivity source implements binary, continuous and weighted variants, mediator-state enumeration, Monte Carlo outcome calculations and bootstrap ensembles. It also contains important boundaries for any port. The discrete mass-shift branch uses strict comparisons around a cumulative-probability threshold, with separate handling of the lagged cumulative probability. Take $\Gamma=3$ in the continuous-treatment sensitivity specialization, so $s^-=1/3$, $s^+=3$, and $c^+=3/4$. For an ordered binary mediator with probabilities $(3/4,1/4)$, the first category lands exactly on the threshold and satisfies none of the selected positive-mass conditions. The expressions then produce masses $(0,3/4)$, and the source's normalization assertion should reject them. The mathematical split at this boundary gives $(1/4,3/4)$. The assertion is protective; the missing equality case still needs reconciliation. \citep{exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_choice}

The finite-sample continuous-mean routine also merits an explicit quadrature check. With four sample values, $\Gamma=2$, and the branch without a fitted outcome-mean regression, the cumulative sums include the index obtained by flooring the quantile position. For four unit values, the selected upper expression gives $7/8$ and the lower expression $5/4$, although a normalized weighted constant must remain one. This fixed-array diagnostic identifies a normalization and fractional-boundary requirement; it does not estimate how often trained neural distributions encounter such a configuration. Continuous integration should be checked against exact finite-support or analytic oracles, including translation of the outcome, small sample counts, ties and $\Gamma=1$. Selected binary-outcome paths explicitly raise \texttt{NotImplementedError}. We would retain the mathematical recursion as a design reference and defer this implementation's automatic admission pending these repairs and version-specific tests. \citep{exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{Neural bounds need both a theorem and numerical certificates}
NeuralCSA generalizes the sensitivity family using two conditional normalizing flows. The retained primary paper assumes independent observations, consistency, positivity and latent unconfoundedness. Its key sufficiency theorem concerns a transformation-invariant generalized treatment sensitivity model, an invertible map that exactly reproduces the observational outcome distribution, and optimization over the full admissible distribution class. That result motivates the two-stage construction. It does not say that any fitted flow, finite Monte Carlo sample and stopped optimizer produces certified extrema. \citep{exec25_pymed_neural_paper}

The selected implementation samples latent variables, evaluates sensitivity constraints and updates a constrained objective using multipliers and a penalty. For supremum-type restrictions, sampled extrema can miss a small violating set. If a violation occupies probability 0.001 and 100 independent probes are drawn, the probability of seeing none is $0.999^{100}\simeq0.905$. This is an authored diagnostic, not an assessment of the paper's experiments. A feasible maximization iterate is generally a lower bound on the optimal maximum, not by itself a valid upper bound on the true causal query. A proposal for certified output therefore needs a feasibility assessment, a justified optimization-gap bound or analytic outer envelope, and a separate account of density-estimation and sampling uncertainty. \citep{exec25_pymed_neuralcsa,exec25_pymed_neural_paper,exec25_pymed_choice}

The source inspection also finds that a public sensitivity-check helper calls a three-argument sensitivity method with only the training batch and latent sample; both selected subclass definitions require the additional mixture sample. That static signature mismatch is a focused candidate for a native regression test, not evidence about every training route. Continuous Rosenbaum sensitivity is explicitly unimplemented. No license-named file was found in the complete selected NeuralCSA and SharpCausalSensitivity trees; publication of source does not settle permission to copy or redistribute it. We would compare their methods and seek artifact-specific rights and delegate completion before code adoption or a port. \citep{exec25_pymed_neuralcsa,exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_choice}

Overcast supplies a contrasting continuous-treatment workflow. The retained Jesson and colleagues preprint bounds a treatment density ratio rather than inserting a density into the binary-treatment odds-ratio formula. Its consistency theorem requires convergence of the fitted outcome density and conditional mean, increasing Monte Carlo effort and a positivity condition; its uncertainty section proposes bootstrap intervals for the bound endpoints. The retained version is the April 2022 author original. A later title and author list in the repository README are not substituted for that original. \citep{exec25_pymed_overcast_paper,exec25_pymed_overcast}

In the selected continuous-treatment source, the sensitivity input is logarithmic, the code exponentiates it, and an epsilon regularizes the expression near the no-confounding boundary. It forms pairwise differences between outcome draws to evaluate threshold candidates. The intermediate array has order $m^2bd$ values for $m$ draws, batch size $b$ and $d$ outcomes. For $m=1000$, $b=256$, $d=1$ and four-byte values, one such array alone is 1,024,000,000 bytes, before masks and other working arrays. This is a storage calculation, not a measured platform limit. A sorted cumulative-sum alternative is a possible port only after tie handling, centering, endpoint semantics and numerical parity have been demonstrated. The method is a conditional dose-response candidate, not a substitute for a longitudinal policy or mediation model. \citep{exec25_pymed_overcast,exec25_pymed_choice}

\subsection{A public qualification sequence with economic consequences}
The package decisions imply independent, executable prototype questions. First, freeze a graph, target, contrast, units, missingness policy and observation clock. Use public synthetic laws with known nested means, including binary mediators, interaction, nonunit treatments and exposure-induced confounding. Compare a regression-path service and a potential-outcome service only where their assumptions imply the same target. Next, enumerate small resampling distributions and finite-support sensitivity sets to check shared-row behavior, failed-fit denominators, probability normalization, ties, contrast scaling and explicit refusal. Neural candidates additionally require held-out density diagnostics, sensitivity feasibility, increasing integration effort, repeated optimization and an external bound oracle. Dependent customer and calendar experiments form a distinct qualification branch. \citep{exec25_pymed_choice}

The same interface must preserve the economics of uncertainty. Suppose, purely for illustration, that a proposed intervention serves 10,000 customers, its benefit lies between USD 2 and USD 8 per customer under the declared sensitivity model, and delivery costs USD 5 per customer. The resulting net interval is USD $[-30{,}000,30{,}000]$. Neither the positive endpoint nor a narrower optimizer output establishes a profitable decision. Further information is valuable only if its expected decision benefit justifies acquisition, computation and review costs. Public known-law fixtures can establish whether the proposed interface preserves this reasoning before expensive bank data work is commissioned. \citep{exec25_pymed_choice}

Accordingly, statsmodels and DoWhy are conditional integration candidates with explicit counterfactual and contrast wrappers; Pingouin and PyProcessMacro are useful regression-path comparators with restricted causal interpretations; SharpCausalSensitivity and NeuralCSA require the stated mathematical, numerical and rights conditions before implementation admission; and Overcast is a continuous-dose sensitivity candidate with a measurable integration-cost question. Selected BSD-style, MIT, GPL and Apache declarations must be assessed together with the actual artifacts and dependency chain; no umbrella rights clearance is inferred. Search tails, negative-control and path-specific alternatives, installed-artifact validation and public native trials remain open. The proposed product should expose an informative refusal whenever a request exceeds the admitted target and sensitivity qualification. \citep{exec25_pymed_statsmodels,exec25_pymed_pingouin,exec25_pymed_dowhy,exec25_pymed_process,exec25_pymed_sharp,exec25_pymed_neuralcsa,exec25_pymed_overcast,exec25_pymed_choice}

\section[Java mediation and structural contracts]{Java mediation: structural equations, intervention semantics, and portable diagrams}
\label{sec:exec25-jmed}

A bank drawing a channel-to-response-to-repayment diagram has two distinct requirements: estimating a statistical structural equation model and evaluating an intervention justified by that model. Java software offers substantial graph and structural-equation components, but the existence of an arrow in a graphical editor does not establish the assignment mechanism, mediator timing or absence of hidden common causes. The selected Tetrad and Onyx sources support a concrete comparison of these components. The resulting proposal is to reuse narrowly specified graph, estimation and export services, with separate effect and uncertainty contracts. A bounded repository search that finds few dedicated mediation projects is not evidence that the language lacks the capability. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad,exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

\subsection{What a linear path product means}
Let $A$ be a servicing assignment, $M$ an intermediate response, and $Y$ the outcome over a fixed horizon. A recursive linear structural model can be written as
\[
 A=\varepsilon_A,\qquad M=aA+\varepsilon_M,\qquad
 Y=cA+bM+\varepsilon_Y.
\]
If these equations are valid structural assignments, intervening on $A$ changes the conditional mean of $Y$ with slope $c+ab$. The direct path has coefficient $c$, while the path through $M$ has product $ab$. This algebra does not itself identify the coefficients from observed data. Interpreting regression estimates as structural coefficients needs restrictions on disturbances, measured covariates, timing, selection and measurement. In particular, a model that fits an observed covariance matrix is not automatically an identified intervention model. \cite{exec25_jmed_choice}

For an authored example, set $(a,b,c)=(2,4,3)$ and disturbance variances to one. With independent disturbances the indirect effect per unit of assignment is eight and the total is eleven. If instead $\operatorname{Cov}(\varepsilon_M,\varepsilon_Y)=1/2$, while assignment remains independent of these disturbances, regression of $Y$ on $A$ and $M$ yields mediator slope $4.5$ and direct coefficient two. The regression path product is then nine, although the structural indirect effect remains eight. The total remains eleven: a correct total can conceal an incorrect decomposition. A proposed Java adapter must distinguish a supplied structural parameter from a regression estimate and retain any correlated-error assumptions. \cite{exec25_jmed_choice}

\subsection{Tetrad total effects and the covariance carried through an intervention}
The selected Tetrad \texttt{SemIm.getTotalEffect} method temporarily zeros coefficient parameters for directed edges entering the treatment node, recomputes an implied covariance matrix, and returns the treatment--outcome covariance divided by treatment variance. Its helper computes the usual linear-SEM expression
\[
 \Sigma=(I-B)^{-1}\Omega(I-B)^{-T},
\]
where $B$ contains structural coefficients and $\Omega$ is the disturbance covariance. The inspected method changes entering directed coefficients, but retains $\Omega$. That distinction matters when treatment shares a disturbance with another variable. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad}

Keep the same coefficients $(2,4,3)$ and set $\operatorname{Cov}(\varepsilon_A,\varepsilon_M)=1/4$, $\operatorname{Cov}(\varepsilon_A,\varepsilon_Y)=1/2$, and $\operatorname{Cov}(\varepsilon_M,\varepsilon_Y)=0$. The unit-diagonal disturbance covariance is positive definite: its determinant is $11/16$. The structural intervention derivative is still eleven, but the selected covariance-ratio expression is
\[
 \frac{\operatorname{Cov}(A,Y)}{\operatorname{Var}(A)}
 = 11+4(1/4)+1/2=12.5.
\]
The treatment has no entering directed edge to remove in this example, so retaining its error correlations retains the observational dependence that an exogenous intervention should break. This is a precise condition on this source route, not a claim that all Tetrad effect methods are invalid. A conditional integration could restrict this route to justified independent treatment disturbances or derive the appropriate graph surgery and propagate its consequences. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad,exec25_jmed_choice}

State restoration is also part of the scientific contract. The selected method restores changed parameters in a \texttt{finally} block around covariance evaluation. However, the initial parameter-zeroing loop precedes that protected block. Its setter accepts free parameters and means, while rejecting other parameters. A fixed entering coefficient can therefore cause a refusal before the protected evaluation begins; if other coefficients were already changed, the wrapper must establish that state is restored. The method additionally returns zero when treatment variance is exactly zero, although a structural intervention derivative can remain nonzero in a supplied model with degenerate observational assignment. That boundary should be a typed qualification condition, not an automatic finding of no effect. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad}

Estimation paths must be kept separate. The inspected default estimator selects different optimizers for models with fixed parameters, cycles, correlated errors or latent nodes; its local regression optimizer is used for a more restricted case. Parameter standard errors may use parent regression or a different covariance calculation, and missing or unsupported cases can return undefined values. Neither a parameter standard error nor two marginal standard errors automatically supply uncertainty for the indirect product. For estimated coefficients $\hat a,\hat b$, the first-order variance is
\[
 b^2\operatorname{Var}(\hat a)+a^2\operatorname{Var}(\hat b)
 +2ab\operatorname{Cov}(\hat a,\hat b).
\]
At $a=2$, $b=4$, variances $0.04$ and $0.09$, and covariance $0.02$, the expression is $1.32$ rather than one when the covariance term is omitted. This is an authored delta-method illustration; nonregularity near zero products, selection of a graph and dependent observations need separate uncertainty analysis. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad,exec25_jmed_choice}

The earlier Tetrad comparison considered effect sets across alternative parent configurations. The inspected IDA routine enumerates candidate sets and catches exceptions while computing effects. Consequently, a returned effect list should be accompanied by attempted and failed configurations; a shortened list is not automatically the full set implied by the graph class. This is graph uncertainty, distinct from a probability distribution over mediation effects. The proposal would preserve both the graph-class semantics and the computation denominator. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad,exec25_jmed_choice}

A related source helper demonstrates why computational limits must not become causal zeros. The total-effect eligibility helper searches potentially directed paths up to a supplied maximum length and labels an empty bounded search as no potentially directed paths. Its documentation instructs callers to force a zero effect in that case. The path delegate explicitly stops when the edge count exceeds the cutoff. In the chain $A\to M_1\to M_2\to Y$ with coefficients two, three and four, a two-edge search returns no complete path even though the structural effect is 24. An unbounded search or a separately proven reachability result is needed before turning absence into zero. Otherwise the request should return search-incomplete. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad,exec25_jmed_choice}

\subsection{Onyx covariance fitting and failure propagation}
Onyx offers a graphical structural-equation environment, a Java estimation backend and export paths to several other systems. Its selected RAM representation uses an asymmetric path matrix $B$, symmetric disturbance covariance $\Omega$, mean vector $m$ and observed-variable selection matrix $F$. The ordinary branch evaluates
\[
 \mu=F(I-B)^{-1}m,\qquad
 \Sigma=F(I-B)^{-1}\Omega(I-B)^{-T}F^T.
\]
This is an appropriate statistical representation for a declared model. Its inversion and covariance calculations do not establish whether an arrow is manipulable or whether correlated disturbances permit a particular causal decomposition. The name \texttt{isIndirectData} in the backend concerns covariance/summary-data input and must not be advertised as an indirect causal-effect service. \cite{exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

The selected covariance-evaluation branch catches an inversion exception, prints an accelerating-cycle warning, and then continues matrix multiplication using the inverse buffer. The inspected inversion delegate initializes that buffer to the identity before pivoting and throws on an exactly singular pivot. Consider the scalar case $B=1$, $\Omega=1$, $m=2$: $I-B=0$ has no inverse and the structural equilibrium is not defined. Following the selected expressions leaves a unit inverse buffer, after which the continued covariance and mean expressions are one and two. These are not valid solutions to the singular model. This is a static control-flow witness rather than an observed GUI output. A reusable backend must propagate the invalid state, invalidate prior results and prevent a warning-only path from supplying accepted estimates. Near-singular cases additionally need conditioning and residual checks. \cite{exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

The presence of a cycle must be distinguished from singularity and from instability. Invertibility of $I-B$ supplies an algebraic equilibrium under the stated linear model; convergence of an iterative dynamic interpretation requires further conditions. A warning that names an accelerating cycle does not determine which condition failed. Conversely, a stable-looking diagram is not evidence of causal identification. This distinction supports a proposed result schema containing model validity, numerical status, structural assumptions and target identity as separate fields. \cite{exec25_jmed_choice}

\subsection{A diagram export is a scientific transformation}
Cross-language reuse is valuable if it preserves variables, direction, parameters, constraints and observation semantics. The inspected generic Onyx exporter dispatches an observed-to-observed directed edge through an \texttt{ON} branch. For an internal edge $A\to M$, its lavaan formatter emits the regression orientation $M\sim A$. The selected Julia formatter emits a right arrow from target to source for the same branch, while its latent-to-observed branch emits the opposite source-to-target orientation. The literal output for the first case is therefore $M\to A$. This source inconsistency requires a target-parser and semantic round-trip test before claiming equivalence to a Julia model. No Julia runtime test is claimed here. \cite{exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

The lavaan exporter also chooses concrete defaults, including estimation with \texttt{fixed.x=FALSE} and full-information handling of missing data, and rejects definition-edge models through its validity predicate. These choices need to be visible when moving a bank analyst's diagram to another runtime. The shared name mapper does attempt collision handling after sanitization, so a superficial inspection of character replacement alone would be insufficient to conclude that labels necessarily collide. An adequate round-trip fixture should include non-ASCII labels, repeated parameter names, fixed edges, correlated errors, means, unsupported definitions and missingness. It should compare the reconstructed graph and equations before comparing fitted values. \cite{exec25_jmed_onyx}

The same principle governs CLI or MCP integration. A request should carry the causal query, versioned diagram, node identities, coefficient status, treatment contrast, graph-search completeness and numerical status. A response should preserve failure denominators and provide a refusal when an exported model changes direction or assumptions. Graph editing, statistical fitting and intervention evaluation are separate services that can be combined only under a tested contract. No sibling-repository integration or operational service is inferred from these proposed interfaces. \cite{exec25_jmed_choice}

\subsection{Reuse decisions and public qualification}
For Tetrad, conditional reuse of graph and SEM components is plausible, but the selected total-effect helper needs the disturbance, fixed-parameter and zero-variance conditions just described. The graph-search cutoff must remain an explicit computation limit. For Onyx, the graphical editor and RAM representation are useful candidates, with backend failure propagation and target-specific export parity as separate admission requirements. Equation-level ports should be considered only when a measured workflow benefit justifies maintaining the same model and uncertainty semantics. Selected GPL and Apache declarations and Onyx's separately listed dependencies require artifact-specific rights review; a top-level license does not clear every bundled component. \cite{exec25_jmed_tetrad,exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

Public qualification can proceed with generated linear systems before costly bank experiments. Enumerate acyclic path products and correlated-disturbance laws; compare graph surgery with covariance ratios; inject fixed-parameter failures and verify restoration; exercise zero treatment variance and bounded path searches; and test singular and near-singular RAM inputs. Round-trip each supported export and retain every rejected case. Then evaluate estimation and uncertainty under independent customers, clusters and shared calendars as distinct designs. A structural total of eleven mistaken for 12.5 across 10,000 customers at a hypothetical USD10 per outcome unit would overstate value by USD150,000. This calculation motivates semantic tests; it is not a bank ROI estimate. \cite{exec25_jmed_choice}

The reviewed sources support these bounded engineering and mathematical routes. They do not resolve arbitrary hidden confounding, causal direction from covariance fit, nonlinear natural effects, general longitudinal sensitivity or independent acceptance. A named Onyx scholarly candidate remains an omission until its genuine original is retained; source-backed technical statements do not substitute metadata for that paper. Complete search tails, additional JVM implementations, installed-artifact trials and rights remain open. These local limits should guide the next public tests while independent proposal work continues, preserving the intervention and export qualification. \cite{exec25_jmed_choice}

Consider the scalar case $B=1$, $\Omega=1$, $m=2$: $I-B=0$ has no inverse and the structural equilibrium is not defined. Following the selected expressions leaves a unit inverse buffer, after which the continued covariance and mean expressions are one and two. These are not valid solutions to the singular model. This is a static control-flow witness rather than an observed GUI output. A reusable backend must propagate the invalid state, invalidate prior results and prevent a warning-only path from supplying accepted estimates. Near-singular cases additionally need conditioning and residual checks. \citep{exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

The presence of a cycle must be distinguished from singularity and from instability. Invertibility of $I-B$ supplies an algebraic equilibrium under the stated linear model; convergence of an iterative dynamic interpretation requires further conditions. A warning that names an accelerating cycle does not determine which condition failed. Conversely, a stable-looking diagram is not evidence of causal identification. This distinction supports a proposed result schema containing model validity, numerical status, structural assumptions and target identity as separate fields. \citep{exec25_jmed_choice}

\subsection{A diagram export is a scientific transformation}
Cross-language reuse is valuable if it preserves variables, direction, parameters, constraints and observation semantics. The inspected generic Onyx exporter dispatches an observed-to-observed directed edge through an \texttt{ON} branch. For an internal edge $A\to M$, its lavaan formatter emits the regression orientation $M\sim A$. The selected Julia formatter emits a right arrow from target to source for the same branch, while its latent-to-observed branch emits the opposite source-to-target orientation. The literal output for the first case is therefore $M\to A$. This source inconsistency requires a target-parser and semantic round-trip test before claiming equivalence to a Julia model. No Julia runtime test is claimed here. \citep{exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

The lavaan exporter also chooses concrete defaults, including estimation with \texttt{fixed.x=FALSE} and full-information handling of missing data, and rejects definition-edge models through its validity predicate. These choices need to be visible when moving a bank analyst's diagram to another runtime. The shared name mapper does attempt collision handling after sanitization, so a superficial inspection of character replacement alone would be insufficient to conclude that labels necessarily collide. An adequate round-trip fixture should include non-ASCII labels, repeated parameter names, fixed edges, correlated errors, means, unsupported definitions and missingness. It should compare the reconstructed graph and equations before comparing fitted values. \citep{exec25_jmed_onyx}

The same principle governs CLI or MCP integration. A request should carry the causal query, versioned diagram, node identities, coefficient status, treatment contrast, graph-search completeness and numerical status. A response should preserve failure denominators and provide a refusal when an exported model changes direction or assumptions. Graph editing, statistical fitting and intervention evaluation are separate services that can be combined only under a tested contract. No sibling-repository integration or operational service is inferred from these proposed interfaces. \citep{exec25_jmed_choice}

\subsection{Reuse decisions and public qualification}
For Tetrad, conditional reuse of graph and SEM components is plausible, but the selected total-effect helper needs the disturbance, fixed-parameter and zero-variance conditions just described. The graph-search cutoff must remain an explicit computation limit. For Onyx, the graphical editor and RAM representation are useful candidates, with backend failure propagation and target-specific export parity as separate admission requirements. Equation-level ports should be considered only when a measured workflow benefit justifies maintaining the same model and uncertainty semantics. Selected GPL and Apache declarations and Onyx's separately listed dependencies require artifact-specific rights review; a top-level license does not clear every bundled component. \citep{exec25_jmed_tetrad,exec25_jmed_onyx,exec25_jmed_choice}

Public qualification can proceed with generated linear systems before costly bank experiments. Enumerate acyclic path products and correlated-disturbance laws; compare graph surgery with covariance ratios; inject fixed-parameter failures and verify restoration; exercise zero treatment variance and bounded path searches; and test singular and near-singular RAM inputs. Round-trip each supported export and retain every rejected case. Then evaluate estimation and uncertainty under independent customers, clusters and shared calendars as distinct designs. A structural total of eleven mistaken for 12.5 across 10,000 customers at a hypothetical USD10 per outcome unit would overstate value by USD150,000. This calculation motivates semantic tests; it is not a bank ROI estimate. \citep{exec25_jmed_choice}

The reviewed sources support these bounded engineering and mathematical routes. They do not resolve arbitrary hidden confounding, causal direction from covariance fit, nonlinear natural effects, general longitudinal sensitivity or independent acceptance. A named Onyx scholarly candidate remains an omission until its genuine original is retained; source-backed technical statements do not substitute metadata for that paper. Complete search tails, additional JVM implementations, installed-artifact trials and rights remain open. These local limits should guide the next public tests while independent proposal work continues, preserving the intervention and export qualification. \citep{exec25_jmed_choice}

Use parenthetical syntax for 6 sentence-final citations after complete longitudinal-synthesis review.

Substantive destination: All longitudinal, monitoring, mediation, dose and uncertainty material remains in the same chapter; citation identities unchanged.

~~~text
\chapter{Longitudinal regimes, heterogeneous effects and continuous doses}
\label{v13:sw-regimes}

The detailed comparisons distinguish complete policy evaluation from components
that estimate heterogeneity or a dose curve. This chapter brings those choices
back to a small set of servicing and incentive requests. Its finite examples
show why the same data field or output name cannot determine which component
the bank needs. The preceding source studies supply the implementation limits;
the task here is to preserve them when composing the workflow.

\section{A sequence of decisions changes the software contract}

Servicing involves repeated decisions. A customer may receive a reminder, a
repayment plan and later a restructuring offer, with delinquency and contact
history changing between actions. An operation for a single binary treatment
does not become a longitudinal estimator by adding a time column. The target
must state which histories the policy observes, when actions occur, what ends
follow-up and how default or account closure changes the outcome.

Let $L_t$ be information available before action $A_t$, and let
$H_t=(\bar L_t,\bar A_{t-1})$ denote the observed history. A deterministic regime
$d$ supplies $A_t=d_t(H_t)$. For a terminal outcome $Y$, its value is
$V(d)=\mathbb E(Y^d)$. Under the required consistency, sequential
exchangeability and positivity conditions, a g-computation construction can
integrate an outcome regression against the covariate transition laws induced
by the intervention. Schematically,
\[
 V(d)=\int m(\bar l_T,\bar d_T)
             \prod_{t=0}^{T}p(l_t\mid\bar l_{t-1},\bar d_{t-1})
             \,d\bar l_T.
\]
The notation suppresses censoring and competing events, which must be specified
in an actual application. The product follows the intervention history; it is
not the factual joint distribution with treatment columns overwritten afterward.

The software comparison should therefore examine covariate transitions,
intervention callbacks, history updates, horizon conventions, simulation error
and sampling uncertainty. It should also compare parametric g-computation with
weighting and longitudinal targeted estimation on the same policy and population.
Disagreement over a default horizon or competing event can dominate any
difference in optimization speed.

\section{pygformula exposes the history and event choices}

The inspected \texttt{ParametricGformula} class accepts individual and time
identifiers, time-varying covariate names/types/models, custom histories, custom
fit/prediction functions and intervention descriptions. Its outcome options
include survival, continuous end-of-follow-up and binary end-of-follow-up.
Separate arguments address competing events, whether to treat them as censoring,
and a censoring model used for natural-course comparisons. \cite{v13_sw_pygformula}

For servicing, default and account closure should not be casually interchanged.
If default prevents later repayment, a default-free survival probability and a
cumulative incidence of repayment are different targets. If closing an account
merely hides future payment information, a censoring argument is needed. Asking
software to treat a competing event as censoring changes the scientific
experiment; the decision requires substantive justification and must survive
the library, CLI and agent interfaces.

The class documentation sets the number of bootstrap samples to zero by
default. The selected fit path invokes separate bootstrap work when samples
are requested. Thus the presence of bootstrap support does not imply that a
particular invocation produced sampling intervals. The helper review in
Section~\ref{sec:exec24-longitudinal-python} establishes whole-subject history
resampling and documents the failed-replicate handling discrepancy. That source
finding does not qualify arbitrary calendar dependence or the fitted intervals.
The adapter must retain the actual sampling unit and complete failure counts.
\cite{v13_sw_pygformula}

Simulation size and bootstrap size address different uncertainties. Conditional
on fitted transition laws, additional simulated trajectories reduce numerical
integration error. They do not provide new bank observations or reduce the
uncertainty in estimated transition parameters. A report should distinguish
the number of observed independent sampling units, simulated histories and
bootstrap replications. Treating simulated histories as additional observed
customers would greatly overstate precision.

The first useful adapter contract therefore preserves customer identity, event
and availability clocks, action timing, history definitions, horizon, competing
events and intervention semantics. It records whether uncertainty was requested
and actually obtained. Natural-course reproduction is a diagnostic: matching
factual outcomes does not prove that intervention predictions are valid.
Unsupported actions, missing positivity and impossible history combinations
should remain explicit, even if simulation can generate a numeric outcome.

The inspected MIT license supports considering reuse. Adoption still needs the
complete simulation and bootstrap paths, environment and dependency terms, and
matched comparisons with R implementations and other longitudinal estimators.
The retained source studies resolve selected simulation and bootstrap questions;
they do not replace those native qualification requirements.
Evaluating supplied regimes also does not establish that an optimal regime has
been learned. Policy selection and its evaluation are additional operations.

\section{grf: binary heterogeneity and continuous slopes are different targets}

The inspected \texttt{causal\_forest} documentation defines a binary-treatment
conditional effect and, for continuous treatment, describes the conditional
covariance ratio
\[
 \tau(x)=\frac{\operatorname{Cov}(Y,A\mid X=x)}
                    {\operatorname{Var}(A\mid X=x)}.
\]
The interface accepts nuisance predictions, clusters and equal cluster
weighting, honesty, subsampling, tuning, grouped trees for uncertainty and a
seed for its native random-number generator. Defaults include 2,000 trees,
honesty enabled and no tuning. These options define a specific statistical and
computational operation; they are not optional metadata for a reproducible
comparison. \cite{v13_sw_grf}

For binary treatment, an effect conditional on $X=x$ differs from a population
average unless it is integrated over the desired population. For continuous
treatment, a slope-like summary differs from a finite contrast
$\mu(a_1,x)-\mu(a_0,x)$. Calling both outputs ``uplift'' obscures the decision.
A bank deciding whether to increase an incentive from one specified amount to
another needs the latter contrast, or a justified approximation to it.

An exact project example makes the discrepancy visible. Suppose the stipulated
mean outcome, in CNY, is
\[
 \mu(a)=20a-3a^2,\qquad A\sim\operatorname{Unif}[0,2].
\]
This is a synthetic response curve, not a fitted bank relationship. Its
covariance slope is
\[
 \frac{\operatorname{Cov}(A,20A-3A^2)}{\operatorname{Var}(A)}
 =20-3\frac{\operatorname{Cov}(A,A^2)}{\operatorname{Var}(A)}=14.
\]
The finite change from dose one to dose two is instead
\[
 \mu(2)-\mu(1)=28-17=11.
\]
Exact moments of the uniform distribution verify both calculations. The
difference is a target difference, not a forest error. Multiplying the slope
by a unit change would overstate this stipulated finite benefit by three CNY.
Neither number includes incentive cost unless that cost is already part of the
outcome definition. \cite{v13_project_exact}

Cluster options require a similar question about the averaging measure. Giving
each branch equal total weight is different from giving each customer equal
weight when branch sizes differ. The chosen measure affects both the target
and the interpretation of heterogeneity. An adapter should require an explicit
population-weight policy and record the effective cluster structure; it should
not silently translate a customer population into an equal-branch population.

The R wrapper's seed, threading and native interface make an R/native adapter
a natural candidate. This inspection does not qualify the C++ training,
prediction or variance implementation. The observed repository GPL text also
requires a complete licensing and distribution review before a port or embedded
binary decision. A rewrite is not automatically preferable to a well-specified
boundary around the original implementation. \cite{v13_sw_grf}

\section{A generalized propensity score is a density component}

CausalGPS's inspected \texttt{estimate\_gps} function accepts continuous
exposure and covariates, a formula, normal or kernel density mode and a
SuperLearner library selection. In its normal branch, the fitted exposure mean
and a residual standard deviation enter a normal density. Its kernel branch
fits exposure and residual scaling and calls a separate density helper. Returned
fields include identity, exposure, generalized propensity score, fitted mean
and scale, and residuals. \cite{v13_sw_causalgps}

For continuous $A$, a generalized propensity score is a conditional density
$r(a,x)=f_{A\mid X}(a\mid x)$, not a probability in $[0,1]$. Its units depend
on the treatment scale. If $B=cA$ for $c>0$, then
\[
 f_{B\mid X}(b\mid x)=\frac1c f_{A\mid X}(b/c\mid x).
\]
Thus converting an incentive from CNY to hundreds of CNY changes the density
numerically. A bridge that normalizes every ``propensity'' into a binary
probability can corrupt continuous-treatment calculations. Exposure units and
the exact density evaluation points belong in the contract.

The observed-density function alone does not provide a dose-response estimator.
To compare feasible doses one needs densities or another identified construction
at the relevant counterfactual doses, an outcome model or weighting/matching
procedure, support diagnostics and an uncertainty argument.
Section~\ref{sec:exec24-dose-response-reuse} follows the density delegate,
transformation factors and downstream weighting. It identifies the selected
kernel branch's residual-density export and the conditions needed for an
original-scale density interpretation. Those restrictions must survive any
cross-package conversion. The retained source analysis establishes a component
and concrete qualification cases; it does not establish the complete causal
workflow.

A useful banking support diagnostic asks whether both proposed incentive levels
occur with adequate conditional support among the intended customers. The
normal model can assign positive density far beyond observed incentive choices;
mathematical positivity of a parametric density is not evidence of practical
overlap. Reporting density values together with observed range, local support
and model sensitivity is more informative than presenting a successful fit as
permission to extrapolate. The observed package declaration is GPL-3; complete
dependency and redistribution choices remain part of adoption review.

\section{causaldrf describes a full response surface, with different limits}

The inspected \texttt{hi\_est} documentation describes fitting a treatment
model, a GPS-adjusted outcome response surface, and predictions averaged over
customers at requested dose-grid values. It lists Normal, LogNormal, transformed
normal, Poisson, negative-binomial, Gamma and Binomial treatment models, and
returns fitted model and parameter records. This supports a documented
response-surface comparison; it is not an execution result or a review of every
internal estimator~\citep{v13_sw_causaldrf}.

Writing a fitted response surface as $\widehat m(a,r)$, the described averaging
operation is of the form
\[
 \widehat\mu(a)=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
                      \widehat m\{a,\widehat r(a,X_i)\}.
\]
The GPS is evaluated at the proposed dose for each customer, rather than simply
reusing every customer's density at their observed dose. A finite effect then
requires a specified comparator, $\widehat\mu(a_1)-\widehat\mu(a_0)$.
A curve ordinate is an expected outcome, and labeling it as an effect without
the comparator misstates the answer.

The choice of dose grid also matters for decision support. If a business
constraint permits only three incentive levels, interpolation at arbitrary
levels has no immediate operating interpretation. If the optimum is selected
from a dense grid using the same fitted surface, uncertainty in that selection
is not the same as pointwise uncertainty at a fixed dose. A package comparison
should therefore distinguish fixed contrasts, whole curves and policy
optimization, and explain which one is actually supported.

The observed DESCRIPTION declares MIT plus a separate LICENSE file. The
initial documentation review did not include that accompanying notice or a
complete implementation review~\citep{v13_sw_causaldrf}. The later source
review recovered and inspected the notice, identifying the year and copyright
holder~\citep{v131_sw_causaldrf}. The appropriate decision remains a provisional
reference or adapter candidate: full implementation, interval behavior and
transitive dependency terms still require qualification. A recovered notice
is not a complete dependency inventory, and documented point estimation is
not evidence of qualified confidence intervals.

\section{A common servicing and incentive benchmark}

These components should be compared through a small set of declared banking
queries. One query fixes a two-period servicing regime and a terminal repayment
outcome. Another fixes a binary offer and asks for heterogeneity. A third fixes
two continuous incentive levels and asks for their net financial difference.
Each query has its own sampling unit, support requirements, feasible actions
and reference functional. A tool may be appropriate for one and unsupported
for another; that is useful information for architecture.

The comparison should retain a model that fits factual outcomes but fails an
intervention assumption, a weak-support population, a changed event definition
and a changed treatment unit. It should separately measure statistical error,
simulation error, numerical failure and computation. For longitudinal work,
the complete cost includes fitting transitions and histories, simulating
interventions, obtaining uncertainty and reporting diagnostics. Timing one
prediction call omits most of the workflow the bank would operate.

An eventual selection can combine components: symbolic identification, a
longitudinal engine, a heterogeneous-effect estimator and a dose-response
component can coexist behind one semantic contract. The common contract must
preserve their distinct assumptions and outputs. It should make unsupported
requests explicit rather than coercing every query into the nearest available
estimator.

\chapter{Longitudinal treatment, monitoring and mediation}
\label{sw:longitudinal}

Policy choice also determines what the bank observes. Monitoring can change
record availability, and a recorded engagement measure may become a mediator
rather than a baseline control. The compact example here connects these roles
to the detailed longitudinal and mediation comparisons, so that an interface
preserves the question before routing it to an R estimator.

\section{The history is part of the intervention}

The servicing problem is naturally sequential. An initial action \(A_0\), such
as an early contact, can change a later financial condition \(L_1\). A second
action \(A_1\) is then chosen using that condition, and a later account cost
\(Y\) is observed. The variable \(L_1\) can be both a consequence of the first
action and a determinant of the second. Treating it as an ordinary baseline
control erases that temporal structure. Treating both actions as one binary
indicator can erase the policy being evaluated.

Consider a finite project mechanism in which \(A_0,L_1,A_1\) are binary and
\[
 \Pr(L_1=1\mid\operatorname{do}(A_0=0))=0.8,
 \qquad
 \Pr(L_1=1\mid\operatorname{do}(A_0=1))=0.4.
\]
Conditional mean cost, in CNY per account over a fixed horizon, is specified as
\[
 m(a_0,l,a_1)=100l-50a_1l+20a_0+10a_1.
\]
The initial action costs 20 and reduces the chance of the adverse condition.
The later action costs 10 and reduces adverse-condition cost by 50 when that
condition is present. These numbers define the example; they are not estimates
of actual bank effects.

For a second-stage rule \(d\), replacing the two action equations gives
\begin{equation}
 V(a_0,d)=\sum_{l=0}^{1}m(a_0,l,d(l))
             \Pr(L_1=l\mid\operatorname{do}(A_0=a_0)).
 \label{sw:finite-policy}
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{sw:finite-policy} follows by enumerating the two possible
conditions under the specified intervention. It yields
\[
 V(0,0)=80,\qquad V(1,l\mapsto l)=44,\qquad V(1,1)=50.
\]
The adaptive policy is better than always giving the later action because it
avoids paying for that action in accounts without the adverse condition.
No policy-learning or global-optimality theorem is needed for this comparison
of three explicitly specified rules. A proposed general optimizer would have
additional obligations.

Now hold \(A_0=0\) and consider an observed logger that gives \(A_1=1\) with
probability 0.9 when \(L_1=1\), and 0.1 otherwise. The observed treated group
contains a disproportionate share of accounts with the adverse condition.
Direct enumeration gives
\[
 E[Y\mid A_1=1,A_0=0]=58.64865\ldots,
 \qquad E[Y\mid A_1=0,A_0=0]=30.76923\ldots.
\]
The observational difference is approximately \(+27.88\). Yet the intervention
contrast that treats everyone at the second stage, at \(A_0=0\), is
\(V(0,1)-V(0,0)=50-80=-30\). The sign reversal is selection by an observed
time-varying condition in this mechanism. It shows why an interface must retain
the treatment history and the distribution over which the policy is valued.

In an application, evaluating an expression like
\eqref{sw:finite-policy} from observations requires substantive assumptions
connecting observed conditional distributions to the intervention model. It
also requires that relevant histories and actions have support. The example
supplies its structural law directly; it does not demonstrate that a bank's
logger, monitoring or missingness mechanisms satisfy those assumptions.

\section{Point treatment and sequential software have different contracts}

The selected tmle interface concerns binary point-treatment effects with
missingness and subject-ID options. It is a candidate for a suitable static
effect question; that does not make it an arbitrary longitudinal policy engine.
The versioned documentation also contains a treatment-missingness wording
difference between DESCRIPTION and the selected Delta argument.
Section~\ref{exec20:rfx-scope} resolves the selected implementation's contract:
missing treatment and baseline covariates are rejected, while the no-variation
route estimates an outcome mean. Section~\ref{sec:exec24-tmle-reuse} retains
the additional interval and resampling restrictions. A convenient argument
name cannot extend those admitted meanings. \cite{v12_sw_tmle}

stremr's selected sequential g-computation and TMLE documentation is closer to
the servicing problem. It accepts time points, sequential outcome-model
formulas, treatment intervention probabilities, monitoring intervention
probabilities and related weighting choices. Treatment and monitoring are
separate intervention components. A bank that increases contact may also
increase the chance that financial stress is recorded; holding monitoring
fixed and changing it are different policy questions. The interface should
name which of these is being evaluated. \cite{v12_sw_stremr}

The same documentation makes several operational qualifications explicit.
The documentation labels \texttt{Qstratify} nonfunctional and an error source,
while the source review in Section~\ref{sec:exec23-longr-workers} finds a
corresponding branch. That discrepancy remains a qualification question;
it is not evidence of failure in every native call. The proposed profile
withholds that option pending qualification. Weight truncation and stabilization
are choices, and the reviewed stopping loops can differ from the requested
cap. A wrapper should report the effective weights, actual updates, final
residuals and warnings after execution. A truncated weight or stopped numerical
iteration is not evidence that positivity or statistical accuracy has been
established. It is part of the approximation and diagnostic record.

survtmle addresses another closely related but distinct problem: right
censoring, competing failures and cumulative incidence. Its selected interface
supports two treatment values. A competing failure must not be recoded as
ordinary censoring simply to fit a common input schema. In a banking setting,
default, repayment, restructuring and account closure can correspond to
different outcome or observation events, depending on the question. The
adapter must preserve the cause, horizon and target, and the analyst must
justify their interpretation. \cite{v12_sw_survtmle}

The initial software decision is to evaluate separate R operations for static
TMLE, sequential g-computation/TMLE, and competing-risk cumulative incidence.
One common transport envelope can contain their inputs and outputs, but it
should not imply scientific substitutability. The proposed tests include
treatment-confounder feedback, monitoring-dependent recording, administrative
censoring, absent action support, and the distinction between a failure event
and a missing observation. These trials must assess the specific estimand and
sampling law; a successful fit on complete independent records does not
qualify a dynamic bank panel.

The age of a saved distribution is recorded as evidence about that distribution,
not as a verdict on project maintenance. In particular, older stremr and
survtmle snapshots do not prove abandonment. Conversely, a recent version
string does not demonstrate correctness of every documented feature. Build
compatibility and method validity require their own observations.

\section{Mediation changes the target again}

A servicing team may want to decompose an action's effect into a part operating
through customer engagement and a part operating through other channels. This
is a different question from the total effect of an adaptive policy. The
mediator's definition and observation time matter, and a missing engagement
record may itself be affected by contact intensity.

medflex supplies a natural-effect-model interface based on expanded data. Its
selected documentation explains that the original mediator is excluded from
the final natural-effect formula and that a full bootstrap refits working
models. Both details affect integration. Expanded records must retain their
link to the original account, and a bootstrap operating on those records
independently would not generally reproduce a bootstrap of the original
subjects and fitting procedure. Reusing a fixed first-stage fit would also
change the documented full-bootstrap operation. \cite{v12_sw_medflex}

The proposed adapter should therefore export an expansion identity, the
original sampling unit, the effect scale, the mediator definition and the
models refitted in each resample. Section~\ref{exec23:medflex-scope} traces
the expansion, full refitting and original-subject covariance, including the
cross-derivative contribution. That review does not make expanded rows new
independent information. The adapter should not market data expansion as a
solution to unmeasured mediator--outcome confounding. Sensitivity summaries
can accompany the decomposition, but they remain conditional analyses of
assumptions. A total-effect engine and a mediation engine can both be useful
without their outputs being interchangeable.

\section{What an R boundary must preserve}

An R process boundary is a plausible first integration route because it permits
evaluation of the existing estimator with its native dependencies. It is not
yet a selected or qualified deployment. The input should contain a stable
account identifier, the information and action clock, event/availability
times, the observation convention, the intervention specification and the
target's units. Serialization must distinguish absent values from absent
records and preserve factor levels, censoring codes and repeated-subject
structure.

The output needs more than an estimate. It must identify the exact target,
weights and truncations, nuisance-model choices, fitted sample, uncertainty
procedure and unavailable quantities. A bridge may fail before the estimator
runs, during fitting, or while translating a result. These failures require
different remedies. The first remedy is an environment or transport repair;
the second may require numerical diagnostics or revised assumptions; the
third requires correction of the adapter. None is a causal conclusion about
the underlying servicing policy.

Porting is an alternative only after the complete method boundary is clear.
Reimplementing a final regression while omitting its expansion, sequential
weighting, model-refitting bootstrap or stopping conditions would create a
different procedure. The advantage of a native port must be demonstrated on
the full workflow, including data transfer, model fitting, uncertainty and
failure handling. A faster final matrix multiplication is not enough.

\section{Generalized synthetic control: preserve the panel target across a wrapper}
\label{exec21:gsynth-scope}

A branch manager evaluates a staggered rollout of a servicing policy. Some
branches adopt early, some late, and some remain untreated. A useful analysis
must say whether its answer concerns a typical branch, a typical treated
branch-month, or a portfolio weighted by an explicitly chosen exposure.
These targets differ before any model is fitted. A package wrapper can change
the answer by losing a weight or substituting an estimator even when the
underlying numerical engine is otherwise appropriate.

The selected pre-cutoff \texttt{gsynth}~1.4.0 source is a compatibility
wrapper around \texttt{fect}. Its maintainers describe the wrapper as retained
solely for backward compatibility. The reviewed dependency candidate is
\texttt{fect}~2.4.5, declared on 29~May~2026 and published through the selected
CRAN revision on 30~May. Matching pinned upstream and CRAN wrapper, manual,
license and change-log files agree byte for byte. This establishes a concrete
source comparison; it does not establish an installed combination. The
wrapper's declared lower bound, \texttt{fect >= 2.0.0}, permits multiple engine
versions with potentially different behavior. \cite{exec21_gsynth_wrapper,exec21_gsynth_engine}

\subsection{Weights and exposure duration define the business quantity}
\label{exec21:gsynth-target}

Let $i$ index branches and $t$ calendar periods. Let $D_{it}$ indicate an
active intervention, $\mathcal S$ denote the retained treated cells, and
$\delta_{it}=Y_{it}(1)-Y_{it}(0)$ the corresponding effect in stated monetary
units. With positive prespecified weights $w_{it}$, two possible targets are
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:gsynth-weights}
 \tau_{\rm cell}=\frac{1}{|\mathcal S|}\sum_{(i,t)\in\mathcal S}\delta_{it},
 \qquad
 \tau_w=\frac{\sum_{(i,t)\in\mathcal S}w_{it}\delta_{it}}
                  {\sum_{(i,t)\in\mathcal S}w_{it}}.
\end{equation}
If $\mathcal I$ is the retained treated-unit set and $T_i^+$ its observed
treated periods, a third target gives each unit equal weight:
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:gsynth-units}
 \tau_{\rm unit}=\frac{1}{|\mathcal I|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I}
       \frac{1}{T_i^+}\sum_{t:(i,t)\in\mathcal S}\delta_{it}.
\end{equation}
Identification of the missing $Y_{it}(0)$ is a separate question from these
definitions. A sample's retained cells need not represent the original
portfolio when observation or unit-removal rules depend on risk or outcome.

The selected \texttt{gsynth} manual promises that \texttt{weight} specifies
the weighting variable for the weighted average effect. The complete
95-line wrapper declares that argument but never reads it again and never
passes it to \texttt{fect}. Its documented weighted interface therefore has
a concrete source-level defect. A successful call or the stored call string
would not demonstrate that the requested weights reached estimation.
This is a source finding; the current review has not executed the native
wrapper. \cite{exec21_gsynth_wrapper}

For an exact illustration, two equally represented treated cells have effects
$-6$ and $10$ dollars. Their unweighted mean is $2$, whereas weights $9$ and
$1$ give $(-54+10)/10=-22/5=-4.4$. If the decision assumes a cost of USD~1
on the corresponding average-exposure basis, the two calculated margins are
USD~1 and USD~$-5.4$. A weighting omission can reverse the indicated decision;
these are authored arithmetic examples, not measured branch results or
observed outputs of either package. \cite{exec21_gsynth_reference}

Exposure duration creates another distinction. Suppose one unit experiences
effect $10$ in each of three treated periods, while another experiences
effect $-6$ in one treated period. Equation~\eqref{exec21:gsynth-weights}
gives $\tau_{\rm cell}=6$ and equation~\eqref{exec21:gsynth-units} gives
$\tau_{\rm unit}=2$. At an assumed per-period cost of $4$, averaging the
net effects using the same respective measures gives margins $2$ and $-2$.
A longer observed treatment history should not silently make one branch
three times as influential in an analysis intended to describe a typical
branch. \cite{exec21_gsynth_reference}

The selected engine exposes both treated-cell and unit-average calculations.
It also distinguishes weights for outcome-model fitting from weights for
aggregation. Supplying \texttt{W} fills both roles; supplying only
\texttt{W.agg} is intended to change aggregation, while \texttt{W.est}
alone leaves canonical aggregation unweighted. The inspected source rejects
distinct columns for the two roles, despite an error message referring to
earlier versions and a future release. Its executed conditional, rather than
that stale version message, determines the limitation. Zero-weight rows are
dropped, and weighted aggregates are routed into the ordinary output slots.
A proposed adapter must therefore record both the requested role and the
actual retained population; blindly multiplying supplied weights is not a
general substitute for missing role support. \cite{exec21_gsynth_engine}

\subsection{A factor model needs information in the relevant directions}
\label{exec21:gsynth-rank}

A useful working model for an untreated outcome is
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:gsynth-factor}
 Y_{it}(0)=\alpha_i+\xi_t+x_{it}^{\mathsf T}\beta
                     +\lambda_i^{\mathsf T}f_t+\varepsilon_{it},
\end{equation}
where $x_{it}$ contains covariates with a justified intervention-time meaning,
$f_t$ represents common latent factors and $\lambda_i$ is the unit's loading.
Even if donor observations reveal $f_t$ exactly, a treated unit's pre-period
outcomes must determine the component of $\lambda_i$ that matters after
treatment. Covariates caused by the intervention, anticipation, spillovers
to donors, or changing loadings require additional arguments. A low prediction
error on past observations cannot establish those substantive conditions.

For a precise obstruction, suppress the intercepts, observed covariates and
errors. Let the factors in two pre-periods and one post-period be
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:gsynth-witness}
 f_1=(1,0)^{\mathsf T},\quad f_2=(2,0)^{\mathsf T},\quad
 f_3=(1,1)^{\mathsf T}.
\end{equation}
Donor loadings $(0,1)$ and $(1,1)$ generate observed paths $(0,0,1)$ and
$(1,2,2)$. A treated loading of either $(2,0)$ or $(2,4)$ gives the same
pre-treatment path $(2,4)$. Hold its observed post-treatment outcome at $8$.
The two compatible untreated post outcomes are $2$ and $6$, giving effects
$6$ and $2$. With an assumed intervention cost of $4$, the net values are $2$
and $-2$. The complete observed panels agree between these two worlds.
\cite{exec21_gsynth_reference}

Writing $F_{\rm pre}$ for the pre-period factor matrix, the direction
$v=(0,1)^{\mathsf T}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{exec21:gsynth-null}
 F_{\rm pre}v=0,\qquad f_3^{\mathsf T}v=1.
\end{equation}
The pre-period factor rank is one while the full factor rank is two.
Repeating the two pre-factor rows over six distinct calendar periods does
not change that rank. A third donor with loading $(2,1)$ can raise the donor
count without resolving this missing treated-unit information. Thus minimum
period and donor counts are useful guards, but are not proofs of the relevant
rank condition. This example lies outside full pre-factor-rank qualification;
it is not evidence that an estimator valid under that qualification fails.
\cite{exec21_gsynth_reference}

The inspected engine includes count/rank restrictions and, in one unbounded
treated-loading branch, solves using the inverse of
$F_{\rm pre}^{\mathsf T}F_{\rm pre}$. A failed solve returns unavailable
effect fields. Near singularity can remain problematic even when the solve
returns. Its inspected rank-selection paths aggregate cross-validation
scores and can apply a minimum-error, one-standard-error or legacy
one-percent selection rule. The final rule may override an intermediate
rank update. These are predictive selection procedures, not tests proving
counterfactual identification. A restricted loading model, an informative
additional pre-period, an external loading restriction or a prospective
experiment could address a particular ambiguity, but each changes the
information or assumptions of the problem. \cite{exec21_gsynth_engine}

\subsection{Defaults, missingness and uncertainty must travel together}
\label{exec21:gsynth-inference}

In the selected wrapper, the default estimator is \texttt{gsynth}. Legacy
\texttt{EM=TRUE} selects \texttt{ife} only if the estimator argument was
omitted. The default requested inference is parametric for the gsynth route
and bootstrap for the IFE or matrix-completion routes. A parametric request
for the latter routes is changed to bootstrap with a warning; the legacy
name \texttt{nonparametric} maps to bootstrap. The wrapper also sets unit
fixed effects, five CV folds and tolerance $10^{-3}$, whereas the direct
engine's inspected defaults include two-way effects, twenty folds and
tolerance $10^{-5}$. Direct-engine adoption therefore requires explicit
configuration rather than assuming identical defaults.
\cite{exec21_gsynth_wrapper,exec21_gsynth_engine}

The engine's gsynth route forces a never-treated donor pool and rejects
treatment reversals. Too few pre-treatment observations or excessive
missingness can remove units; the returned result must disclose this change
in population. A package family's support for switching treatments does not
override the restrictions of this selected method. Missing-cell masks also
enter fitting and effect aggregation, so reporting only the number of
input rows conceals the denominator actually used. \cite{exec21_gsynth_engine}

For parametric uncertainty, the selected engine requires the isolated
never-treated pool. Its automatic error mode uses an autoregressive strategy
when cells are missing and an empirical strategy otherwise; explicitly
requested empirical or wild modes require a complete panel. In inspected
case-bootstrap code, units or cluster identifiers are resampled, with
support-conditioned retries in unit-resampling branches. Ordinary jackknife
with a cluster argument emits a warning that clustering is ignored. These
are different sampling mechanisms; a generic confidence-interval label
cannot make them interchangeable. \cite{exec21_gsynth_engine}

The inspected average-effect uncertainty uses the standard deviation of
bootstrap draws with missing values removed and, by default, a normal
interval. The basic-pivot option has a different construction. Any future
qualification must preserve attempted, valid and failed draws, resampling
units, selection/refitting behavior and reasons for unavailable output.
Dropping failed draws from a standard-deviation calculation is not evidence
that inference remains valid. Dependence across branches, common calendar
shocks, model misspecification and post-selection uncertainty each require a
declared law and a specific argument. This source comparison has executed
neither native package and makes no empirical coverage claim.
\cite{exec21_gsynth_engine}

\subsection{Reuse through an explicit panel contract}
\label{exec21:gsynth-integration}

The proposal retains the pinned wrapper as a compatibility reference and
favors evaluating a direct \texttt{fect} worker if a prototype is funded.
The ignored weight and implicit remapping make an unrestricted wrapper
pass-through unsuitable for a new interface. Porting the engine immediately
would duplicate panel conversion, rank selection, native numerical routines
and inference before a behavioral comparison exists. Classic synthetic
control, difference-in-differences, matrix-completion and state-space
approaches remain substantive comparators whose targets and identifying
assumptions must be matched to the banking question.

Both packages declare \texttt{MIT + file LICENSE}; the saved license files
contain the holder and year. That declaration supports identifying the
stated license, but this review has not inspected the complete standard
template or every dependency's terms. The wrapper says it needs no
compilation, while the selected engine requires compiled components,
R~4.1 or later, Rcpp and RcppArmadillo. A deployment estimate must include
that environment. Process isolation does not itself establish rights,
reproducibility or numerical validity. \cite{exec21_gsynth_wrapper,exec21_gsynth_engine}

A common library, CLI and MCP request should carry unique unit/time keys,
the complete calendar, action timing, outcome units, the donor population,
observation masks, target weights and their fitting/aggregation roles. It
should also freeze the method, fixed effects, rank candidates, selection
rule, tolerance, resampling law and dependency identities. The response
should return the actual configuration, retained sample and denominators,
conditioning and convergence diagnostics, original warnings, named effect
aggregates and a separately typed uncertainty result. An agent translating
the business question may propose these fields; it cannot infer a missing
weighting basis or certify donor validity from a fluent explanation.

Before admission, a public reference comparison should reproduce the
wrapper's weight omission and compare an explicitly configured engine on
known target calculations. It should then exercise unequal exposure
durations, missingness, reversals, donor shortages, weak factor support,
rank resets, failed solves, cluster warnings and bounded runtime failures.
Known-law factor panels can test selected calibration claims before any
costly bank experiment, provided selection and failure-denominator rules
are frozen in advance. Total cost includes validation, conversion, rank
search, repeated fits, uncertainty, serialization, review and maintenance.
These public comparisons can establish whether a panel prototype merits
investment while preserving the assumptions a later banking study must test.
\cite{exec21_gsynth_reference}
